The finite-variation conclusion for the frame and Jacobi matrix is the standard integral form of the Riccati--Jacobi transport equations with integrable coefficients, and supplies the remaining finite-variation clause of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftWeightedDOCContinuation}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Redshifted Bel--Robinson sector seed]\label{def:RedshiftedBelRobinsonSectorSeed}
On a compact exterior optical slab $\mathcal U_\varepsilon(K)$, a \emph{redshifted Bel--Robinson sector seed} consists of future-directed multipliers $(T_\varepsilon,N_\varepsilon)$, a positive weight $\rho_\varepsilon=r_\varepsilon^p\chi_\varepsilon$, and a redshift weight $\varpi_\varepsilon$ such that:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the Bel--Robinson current
    \[
        P_\varepsilon^\mu
        =
        \rho_\varepsilon
        Q[W_\varepsilon]^\mu{}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
        T_\varepsilon^\alpha N_\varepsilon^\beta N_\varepsilon^\gamma
    \]
    satisfies the four clauses of Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCertificate};
    \item on every visible limiting DOC segment not charged by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$, the redshift weight is comparable to the optical multiplier lapse and obeys
    \[
        c_K\varpi_\varepsilon
        \le
        -\bar g(N_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)
        \le
        C_K\varpi_\varepsilon;
    \]
    \item the multiplier-controlled curvature and connection budget satisfies
    \[
        \int_\gamma
        \varpi_\varepsilon
        \left(
            |\alpha_\varepsilon|
            +
            |\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)|
            +
            |\nabla_{L_\varepsilon}L_\varepsilon|
            +
            |\theta_\varepsilon|^2
            +
            |\sigma_\varepsilon|^2
        \right)d\lambda
        <\infty,
    \]
    with uniform local bound on each retained nonexceptional family.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Redshifted Bel--Robinson seed gives the local energy-continuation sector]\label{thm:RedshiftedBelRobinsonSectorSeed}
If an admissible regularized optical construction carries a redshifted Bel--Robinson sector seed on $K$, then the Bel--Robinson multiplier certificate and the redshift ODE continuation certificate hold on $K$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first clause of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftedBelRobinsonSectorSeed} is exactly Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCertificate}. Hence the Bel--Robinson multiplier certificate holds.

It remains to verify Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftODEContinuationCertificate}. Let $\gamma$ be a visible limiting DOC segment. If the limiting family containing $\gamma$ is charged by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$, then the defect alternative in the redshift ODE certificate holds. Otherwise, the second clause identifies the redshift weight with the optical multiplier lapse up to constants depending only on $K$, and the third clause gives the required finite weighted integral of $\alpha$, $\Ric(L,L)$, acceleration, expansion square, and shear square after passing to the limiting segment by lower semicontinuity of nonnegative Radon measures. The Riccati equation for the null second fundamental form and the Jacobi equation for the screen matrix have coefficients controlled by precisely these weighted quantities. On compact DOC subsets away from the horizon, $\varpi_\varepsilon$ is bounded above and below; near the horizon it has the standard redshift degeneration built into Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftWeightedDOCContinuation}. Therefore the controlled weighted integral gives finite variation of the optical frame and screen Jacobi matrix. This is the complementary alternative in Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftODEContinuationCertificate}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite redshift variation gives optical continuation]\label{lem:RedshiftFiniteVariationContinuation}
Assume the first alternative in Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftWeightedDOCContinuation}. Then the limiting null optical segment extends as a locally achronal null geodesic segment past the endpoint.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The weighted integrability controls the Riccati and Jacobi transport equations along $\gamma$. Since $\varpi$ is bounded above and below away from the horizon on compact DOC neighborhoods, the optical connection coefficients, expansion, shear, and screen Jacobi matrix have finite variation up to the endpoint. Hence the tangent $L$, the screen frame, and the Jacobi data have limits. The geodesic equation with finite-variation connection coefficients gives a Carath\'eodory extension of the null geodesic beyond the endpoint in the local DOC chart. The limiting achronality follows by closedness of the causal relation on the local globally hyperbolic neighborhood: a timelike shortcut in the extension would pull back to timelike shortcuts for nearby retained regular segments, contradicting their local achronality.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Redshift continuation route to DOC optical completeness]\label{thm:RedshiftDOCOpticalCompleteness}
The redshift-weighted DOC continuation package and optical defect conservation imply DOC optical completeness.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $\gamma$ be a limiting null optical segment with endpoint in the domain of outer communications and in $J^-(\mathscr I^+)$. If the first alternative of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftWeightedDOCContinuation} holds, Lemma~\ref{lem:RedshiftFiniteVariationContinuation} extends $\gamma$ as a locally achronal null geodesic past the endpoint. If the second alternative holds, optical defect conservation identifies the limiting area contribution as part of the nonnegative singular-stratum defect current. These are exactly the two alternatives in Definition~\ref{def:DOCOpticalCompleteness}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Visible optical singularity exclusion]\label{def:VisibleOpticalSingularityExclusion}
The development satisfies \emph{visible optical singularity exclusion} if there is no positive regular-area family of retained null optical generators whose limiting endpoint is both in the domain of outer communications and in $J^-(\mathscr I^+)$, and at which the optical construction cannot be extended as a regular null family.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[DOC optical completeness excludes visible collapse]\label{lem:DOCCompletenessVisibleExclusion}
DOC optical completeness implies visible optical singularity exclusion.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Suppose visible optical singularity exclusion failed. Then there would be a positive regular-area family of retained null optical generators with a limiting endpoint in the domain of outer communications and in $J^-(\mathscr I^+)$, but with no regular null-family extension past the endpoint. DOC optical completeness gives two alternatives. If the limiting segment extends regularly, we contradict the assumed singular termination. If its limiting area is entirely charged by the singular-stratum defect measure, then it is not a positive regular-area family. Both alternatives contradict the failure assumption.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Visible singularity exclusion is strong WCC for optical collapse]\label{lem:VisibleExclusionStrongWCC}
Visible optical singularity exclusion implies the strong null-collapse exclusion form of WCC in Definition~\ref{def:StrongWCCNullCollapseExclusion}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
A positive-area null optical collapse endpoint contained in the domain of outer communications and visible from future null infinity is exactly a positive regular-area family terminating at an endpoint in $J^-(\mathscr I^+)$ where the regular optical family cannot be extended. This is ruled out by Definition~\ref{def:VisibleOpticalSingularityExclusion}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[DOC completeness route to strong optical censorship]\label{cor:DOCCompletenessStrongWCC}
DOC optical completeness implies the strong null-collapse exclusion form of WCC for the admissible optical construction.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Combine Lemma~\ref{lem:DOCCompletenessVisibleExclusion} with Lemma~\ref{lem:VisibleExclusionStrongWCC}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Causal shadow index]\label{def:CausalShadowIndex}
Let $E\subset K_\varepsilon$ be a measurable family of retained outgoing null generators with positive initial area. The \emph{future screen} of $E$ is the set
\[
    \mathfrak S(E):=\overline{J^+(\Gamma(E))\cap\mathscr I^+},
\]
where $\Gamma(E)$ is the portion of the regularized optical hypersurface swept out by $E$, and the closure is taken in the conformal completion. The \emph{causal shadow index} is
\[
    \mathfrak{sh}(E)
    :=
    \liminf_{\varepsilon\to0}
    \frac{\omega_{\mathscr I}(\mathfrak S(E_\varepsilon))}
         {A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}(E_\varepsilon)},
\]
where $\omega_{\mathscr I}$ is any fixed Bondi cut measure on future null infinity. The construction is called \emph{shadow-nondegenerate} if $\mathfrak{sh}(E)>0$ for every positive-area family whose optical endpoint remains in the domain of outer communications.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Shadow semicontinuity]\label{def:ShadowSemicontinuity}
The optical construction satisfies \emph{shadow semicontinuity} if, whenever positive-area families $E_j$ of retained generators converge as generator currents and their first focal, cut, or zero-area endpoints converge to a terminal point $p$, then
\[
    \liminf_{j\to\infty}\omega_{\mathscr I}(\mathfrak S(E_j))>0
\]
implies $p\in J^-(\mathscr I^+)$ and $p$ lies in the closure of the domain of outer communications.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Horizon screen-flux dichotomy]\label{def:HorizonScreenFluxDichotomy}
The admissible optical construction satisfies the \emph{horizon screen-flux dichotomy} if the following alternative holds for every positive-area family $E_j$ of retained generators whose terminal focal, cut, or zero-area endpoints converge to the event horizon:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item either the limiting family is charged entirely by the nonnegative singular-stratum defect measure of the weak null flow;
    \item or its future screen measure vanishes,
    \[
        \lim_{j\to\infty}\omega_{\mathscr I}(\mathfrak S(E_j))=0 .
    \]
\end{enumerate}
Equivalently, no positive regular-area family can cross into the black-hole region while retaining a positive lower Bondi screen measure.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Horizon absorption accounting]\label{def:HorizonAbsorptionAccounting}
The optical construction satisfies \emph{horizon absorption accounting} if any retained family whose terminal limit lies on the event horizon has a Radon measure decomposition
\[
    \mu_{\mathrm{lim}}
    =
    \mu_{\mathrm{reg}}
    +
    \mu_{\mathrm{sing}}
    +
    \mu_{\mathcal H}
\]
on compact null slabs, where $\mu_{\mathrm{sing}}$ is the caustic and cut-locus defect measure, $\mu_{\mathcal H}$ is supported on the event-horizon absorption set, and the regular part $\mu_{\mathrm{reg}}$ has zero future screen trace. Moreover, $\mu_{\mathcal H}$ is nonnegative and carries precisely the area lost through horizon absorption.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Absorption accounting gives the horizon dichotomy]\label{lem:HorizonAccountingDichotomy}
Horizon absorption accounting, together with the conformal closed-graph property, implies the horizon screen-flux dichotomy.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $E_j$ be a positive-area retained family whose terminal endpoints converge to the event horizon. By horizon absorption accounting, its limiting measure decomposes into a regular part, a singular defect part, and a nonnegative horizon absorption part. If the family is charged by $\mu_{\mathrm{sing}}+\mu_{\mathcal H}$, then its lost area is already recorded by the defect or horizon absorption measure, giving the first alternative after enlarging the singular-stratum accounting to include horizon absorption.

It remains to consider the part with positive regular limiting density. The regular part has zero future screen trace by Definition~\ref{def:HorizonAbsorptionAccounting}. If its future screen measure had a positive lower bound, then the closed-graph property would produce a limiting causal curve from the horizon-crossing endpoint to a point of $\mathscr I^+$, placing that endpoint in the exterior causal past of future null infinity. This contradicts membership in the black-hole region unless the endpoint lies on the boundary with zero screen trace. Hence the regular horizon-absorbed part has vanishing screen measure, which is the second alternative.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Horizon dichotomy supplies nonabsorption]\label{lem:HorizonDichotomyNonabsorption}
Assume shadow nondegeneracy. Then the horizon screen-flux dichotomy implies that positive-area endpoint collapse cannot occur through the event horizon without either entering the singular-stratum or horizon-absorption defect measure or losing all future screen measure.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
This is the contrapositive of Definition~\ref{def:HorizonScreenFluxDichotomy}. A positive-area endpoint collapse through the horizon with positive regular limiting density and positive future screen measure violates both alternatives in the dichotomy. Shadow nondegeneracy rules out the possibility that a positive regular-area exterior family has zero normalized screen measure before the horizon limit; hence any such collapse must be accounted for by the defect or horizon absorption measure.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Bondi screen-Jacobian lower bound]\label{def:BondiScreenJacobianLowerBound}
Let $\Pi_\varepsilon$ be the future screen map sending a retained generator $y\in K_\varepsilon$ to the portion of $\mathscr I^+$ reached by the exterior future of $\Gamma_\varepsilon(y)$, whenever this screen is nonempty. The construction satisfies a \emph{Bondi screen-Jacobian lower bound} if there exists $\kappa>0$ such that, for every positive-area retained family $E_\varepsilon$ whose optical endpoint remains in a fixed compact subset of the domain of outer communications,
\[
    \omega_{\mathscr I}\bigl(\Pi_\varepsilon(E_\varepsilon)\bigr)
    \ge
    \kappa\,A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}(E_\varepsilon)
    -o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Uniform screen transversality]\label{def:UniformScreenTransversality}
The future screen map $\Pi_\varepsilon$ is \emph{uniformly transverse} on compact exterior regions if, after removing a set of $A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}$-measure $o_\varepsilon(1)$, it is approximately differentiable on retained generator space, has multiplicity at most $N$, and its two-dimensional metric Jacobian $J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_\varepsilon$ satisfies
\[
    J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_\varepsilon(y)\ge j_0>0
\]
for $A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}$-a.e. retained generator whose optical endpoint remains in the given compact exterior region. The constants $j_0$ and $N$ are required to be independent of $\varepsilon$ on each compact exterior region.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Optical scattering regularity]\label{def:OpticalScatteringRegularity}
The retained optical family has \emph{uniform optical scattering regularity} on a compact exterior region if the following hold for the screen map $\Pi_\varepsilon$:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Screen Jacobi determinant bound.} The two independent Jacobi fields obtained by varying retained generators and propagating them to $\mathscr I^+$ have Bondi screen determinant
    \[
        \det_{\mathscr I}\mathcal J_\varepsilon(y)\ge j_0>0
    \]
    for $A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}$-a.e. retained generator, after discarding a set of area $o_\varepsilon(1)$.
    \item \emph{No screen caustic on positive-area families.} The set where the screen determinant vanishes has limiting initial area zero or is absorbed into the singular-stratum defect measure.
    \item \emph{Bounded screen multiplicity.} For $\omega_{\mathscr I}$-a.e. screen point $q$, at most $N$ retained generators in the compact exterior region map to $q$, up to an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ exceptional family.
\end{enumerate}
The constants $j_0$ and $N$ are independent of $\varepsilon$ on the compact exterior region.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Exterior optical tidal smallness]\label{def:ExteriorOpticalTidalSmallness}
Along a retained exterior null scattering segment, let $\mathcal R_\varepsilon(s)$ be the screen tidal matrix in the Jacobi equation
\[
    \mathcal J_\varepsilon''(s)+\mathcal R_\varepsilon(s)\mathcal J_\varepsilon(s)=0
\]
for the two-parameter family of generators reaching $\mathscr I^+$. The construction satisfies \emph{exterior optical tidal smallness} on a compact exterior region if, after the usual $o_\varepsilon(1)$ discard,
\[
    \int_{s_0}^{s_{\mathscr I}}
    (s-s_0)\,\|\mathcal R_\varepsilon(s)\|\,ds
    \le \eta<1
\]
uniformly on retained generators, and the initial screen normalization obeys
\[
    \det \mathcal J_\varepsilon(s_0)\ge a_0>0,
    \qquad
    \|\mathcal J_\varepsilon(s_0)\|+\|\mathcal J_\varepsilon'(s_0)\|\le A_0 .
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Exterior curvature-flux smallness]\label{def:ExteriorCurvatureFluxSmallness}
Let $L_\varepsilon$ be the tangent to a retained exterior null generator and let $E_{1,\varepsilon},E_{2,\varepsilon}$ be a parallel orthonormal screen frame along the generator. The construction satisfies \emph{exterior curvature-flux smallness} on a compact exterior region if the null curvature components obey
\[
    \mathcal R_{\varepsilon,AB}(s)
    :=
    \mathrm{Rm}_\varepsilon(E_{A,\varepsilon},L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon,E_{B,\varepsilon})
    + \Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)\delta_{AB}/2
\]
in the screen Jacobi equation and, after the usual $o_\varepsilon(1)$ discard,
\[
    \int_{s_0}^{s_{\mathscr I}}
    (s-s_0)
    \left(
        |\alpha_\varepsilon(s)|
        +|\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)|
    \right)\,ds
    \le \eta_{\mathrm{curv}}<1 .
\]
Here $\alpha_\varepsilon$ denotes the Weyl component $C_\varepsilon(E_A,L,L,E_B)$ restricted to the screen. Equivalently, one may replace the pointwise generator bound by a uniform weighted optical-flux bound whose exceptional superlevel set has retained area $o_\varepsilon(1)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Averaged optical curvature-energy smallness]\label{def:AveragedOpticalCurvatureEnergySmallness}
On a compact exterior region, the retained optical family satisfies \emph{averaged optical curvature-energy smallness} if for every retained generator family $K_\varepsilon$ there is a threshold $\eta_*<1$ and an exceptional set $B_\varepsilon\subset K_\varepsilon$ with
\[
    A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}(B_\varepsilon)=o_\varepsilon(1)
\]
such that
\[
    \sup_{y\in K_\varepsilon\setminus B_\varepsilon}
    \int_{s_0}^{s_{\mathscr I}}
    (s-s_0)
    \left(
        |\alpha_\varepsilon(s,y)|
        +|\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)(s,y)|
    \right)\,ds
    \le \eta_* .
\]
A sufficient averaged form is the Lorentz--Chebyshev estimate
\[
    \int_{K_\varepsilon}
    \left[
    \int_{s_0}^{s_{\mathscr I}}
    (s-s_0)
    \left(
        |\alpha_\varepsilon|
        +|\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)|
    \right)\,ds
    \right]^{1+\delta}
    dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
    =
    o_\varepsilon(1)
\]
for some $\delta>0$, after normalizing the retained family to unit initial area on the compact region.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Bel--Robinson optical flux package]\label{def:BelRobinsonOpticalFluxPackage}
On a compact exterior optical slab $\mathcal U_\varepsilon(K)$ generated by $K_\varepsilon$, the regularized spacetime satisfies the \emph{Bel--Robinson optical flux package} if there are $\delta>0$ and a nonnegative error $e_\varepsilon(K)=o_\varepsilon(1)$ such that
\[
    \int_{\mathcal U_\varepsilon(K)}
    w_\varepsilon^{1+\delta}
    \left(
        |W_\varepsilon|^{1+\delta}
        +|\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)|^{1+\delta}
    \right)
    d\mu_{\mathrm{opt},\varepsilon}
    \le e_\varepsilon(K),
\]
where $W_\varepsilon$ is the Weyl tensor and $d\mu_{\mathrm{opt},\varepsilon}=ds\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}$ is the optical product measure up to the uniformly controlled regular Jacobian. The weight satisfies
\[
    c_K(s-s_0)\le w_\varepsilon(s,y)\le C_K(1+s-s_0)
\]
on the retained slab, and the same bound holds after replacing $W_\varepsilon$ by the null Weyl component $\alpha_\varepsilon=C_\varepsilon(E_A,L,L,E_B)$. The matter term is interpreted through the regularized Einstein equations, so that the $\Ric(L,L)$ flux includes the null energy density and the smoothing error, with the latter absorbed into $e_\varepsilon(K)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Renormalized Bel--Robinson divergence package]\label{def:RenormalizedBelRobinsonDivergencePackage}
Let $Q[W_\varepsilon]$ be the Bel--Robinson tensor of the regularized Weyl field. The optical construction satisfies the \emph{renormalized Bel--Robinson divergence package} on $\mathcal U_\varepsilon(K)$ if there exist a future timelike multiplier $T_\varepsilon$, an optical redshift multiplier $N_\varepsilon$, and a weight $r_\varepsilon^p\chi_\varepsilon$ with $p>0$ such that the current
\[
    P_\varepsilon^\mu
    :=
    r_\varepsilon^p\chi_\varepsilon\,
    Q[W_\varepsilon]^\mu{}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
    T_\varepsilon^\alpha N_\varepsilon^\beta N_\varepsilon^\gamma
\]
has the following properties:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Coercive bulk.} Its divergence controls the optical Weyl density,
    \[
        \nabla_\mu P_\varepsilon^\mu
        \ge
        c_K\,w_\varepsilon^{1+\delta}|W_\varepsilon|^{1+\delta}
        -\mathcal E_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{low}}
        -\mathcal E_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{mat}}
    \]
    in the sense of measures on the retained slab.
    \item \emph{Renormalized boundary balance.} The flux of $P_\varepsilon$ through the initial cut, terminal Bondi cut, artificial timelike caps, and horizon collar is nonnegative after subtracting the ADM/Bondi news flux and the horizon absorption flux; the remaining renormalized boundary contribution is $o_\varepsilon(1)$ on $K$.
    \item \emph{Lower-order absorption.} The deformation-tensor, commutator, and smoothing terms satisfy
    \[
        \int_{\mathcal U_\varepsilon(K)}
        \left(
            \mathcal E_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{low}}
            +\mathcal E_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{mat}}
            +w_\varepsilon^{1+\delta}|\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)|^{1+\delta}
        \right)
        d\mu_{\mathrm{opt},\varepsilon}
        =
        o_\varepsilon(1).
    \]
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Bel--Robinson multiplier certificate]\label{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCertificate}
The optical slab carries a \emph{Bel--Robinson multiplier certificate} if the multipliers in Definition~\ref{def:RenormalizedBelRobinsonDivergencePackage} can be chosen so that:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the deformation tensor of $T_\varepsilon,N_\varepsilon$ has a positive optical component,
    \[
        Q[W_\varepsilon]_{\mu\alpha\beta\gamma}
        {}^{(T,N,N)}\pi_\varepsilon^{\mu\alpha\beta\gamma}
        +
        \nabla(r_\varepsilon^p\chi_\varepsilon)\cdot Q[W_\varepsilon](T_\varepsilon,N_\varepsilon,N_\varepsilon)
        \ge
        c_K w_\varepsilon^{1+\delta}|W_\varepsilon|^{1+\delta}
        -\mathcal E_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{low}};
    \]
    \item the Bianchi-error and matter-source divergence of $Q[W_\varepsilon]$ is bounded by $\mathcal E_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{mat}}$;
    \item the initial, Bondi, horizon, and artificial-cap boundary terms have the renormalized sign stated in Definition~\ref{def:RenormalizedBelRobinsonDivergencePackage};
    \item the integrated lower-order, matter, smoothing, and null-Ricci errors satisfy the lower-order absorption estimate in Definition~\ref{def:RenormalizedBelRobinsonDivergencePackage}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Multiplier certificate proves Gate 1]\label{prop:BelRobinsonCertificateGate}
The Bel--Robinson multiplier certificate implies the renormalized Bel--Robinson divergence package.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Expanding $\nabla_\mu P_\varepsilon^\mu$ gives the deformation-tensor contribution, the derivative of the weight $r_\varepsilon^p\chi_\varepsilon$, and the Bianchi/matter divergence terms of $Q[W_\varepsilon]$. The first clause of Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCertificate} gives the coercive bulk inequality, while the second clause places all source terms into $\mathcal E_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{mat}}$. The third clause is precisely the renormalized boundary balance, and the fourth clause is the required lower-order absorption. These are the three clauses of Definition~\ref{def:RenormalizedBelRobinsonDivergencePackage}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Divergence route to Bel--Robinson optical flux]\label{thm:DivergenceBelRobinsonOpticalFlux}
The renormalized Bel--Robinson divergence package implies the Bel--Robinson optical flux package.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Integrate the divergence inequality of Definition~\ref{def:RenormalizedBelRobinsonDivergencePackage} over $\mathcal U_\varepsilon(K)$ and apply Stokes' theorem to the regularized optical slab. The renormalized boundary balance says that, after the physically nonnegative Bondi news and horizon absorption fluxes are removed, the boundary contribution is $o_\varepsilon(1)$. Hence
\[
    \int_{\mathcal U_\varepsilon(K)}
    w_\varepsilon^{1+\delta}|W_\varepsilon|^{1+\delta}
    d\mu_{\mathrm{opt},\varepsilon}
    \le
    C_K o_\varepsilon(1)
    +
    C_K
    \int_{\mathcal U_\varepsilon(K)}
    \left(
        \mathcal E_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{low}}
        +\mathcal E_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{mat}}
    \right)d\mu_{\mathrm{opt},\varepsilon}.
\]
The lower-order absorption clause makes the final integral $o_\varepsilon(1)$ and simultaneously supplies the required weighted null-Ricci bound. Combining the two estimates gives Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonOpticalFluxPackage}, with $e_\varepsilon(K)$ equal to the sum of the renormalized boundary remainder and the absorbed lower-order errors.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Bel--Robinson flux gives averaged curvature energy]\label{lem:BelRobinsonFluxAveragedEnergy}
The Bel--Robinson optical flux package implies averaged optical curvature-energy smallness.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Set
\[
    F_\varepsilon(y)
    :=
    \int_{s_0}^{s_{\mathscr I}}
    (s-s_0)
    \left(
        |\alpha_\varepsilon(s,y)|
        +|\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)(s,y)|
    \right)\,ds .
\]
By the lower bound for $w_\varepsilon$, the tensorial domination of $\alpha_\varepsilon$ by $W_\varepsilon$, and the uniform Jacobian comparison between the optical product measure and the regular slab measure,
\[
    \int_{K_\varepsilon}F_\varepsilon^{1+\delta}\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
    \le
    C_K
    \int_{\mathcal U_\varepsilon(K)}
    w_\varepsilon^{1+\delta}
    \left(
        |W_\varepsilon|^{1+\delta}
        +|\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)|^{1+\delta}
    \right)
    d\mu_{\mathrm{opt},\varepsilon}
    +o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
The right-hand side is $o_\varepsilon(1)$ by Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonOpticalFluxPackage}. This is exactly the Lorentz--Chebyshev sufficient condition in Definition~\ref{def:AveragedOpticalCurvatureEnergySmallness}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Averaged curvature energy gives exterior curvature-flux smallness]\label{lem:AveragedEnergyCurvatureFluxSmallness}
Averaged optical curvature-energy smallness implies exterior curvature-flux smallness.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The first formulation is exactly Definition~\ref{def:ExteriorCurvatureFluxSmallness} after discarding $B_\varepsilon$, whose initial area is $o_\varepsilon(1)$.

For the Lorentz--Chebyshev sufficient condition, define
\[
    F_\varepsilon(y)
    :=
    \int_{s_0}^{s_{\mathscr I}}
    (s-s_0)
    \left(
        |\alpha_\varepsilon(s,y)|
        +|\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)(s,y)|
    \right)\,ds .
\]
For any fixed $\eta_*<1$, Chebyshev's inequality gives
\[
    A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}\{F_\varepsilon>\eta_*\}
    \le
    \eta_*^{-(1+\delta)}
    \int_{K_\varepsilon}F_\varepsilon^{1+\delta}\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
    =
    o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
Discarding this superlevel set leaves the required uniform generatorwise bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Averaged-energy route to positive screen determinant]\label{cor:AveragedEnergyPositiveScreenDet}
Averaged optical curvature-energy smallness implies the positive screen Jacobi determinant conclusion of Lemma~\ref{lem:TidalSmallnessPositiveScreenDet}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Combine Lemma~\ref{lem:AveragedEnergyCurvatureFluxSmallness}, Lemma~\ref{lem:CurvatureFluxTidalSmallness}, and Lemma~\ref{lem:TidalSmallnessPositiveScreenDet}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Curvature flux gives tidal smallness]\label{lem:CurvatureFluxTidalSmallness}
Exterior curvature-flux smallness implies exterior optical tidal smallness, after replacing $\eta$ by a constant depending only on $\eta_{\mathrm{curv}}$ and the fixed screen-frame normalization.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In a parallel screen frame the Jacobi tidal matrix is exactly the projection of the curvature endomorphism $X\mapsto R(X,L)L$ to $L^\perp/\langle L\rangle$. Its trace-free part is the Weyl component $\alpha_\varepsilon$, while its trace is controlled by $\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)$. Hence
\[
    \|\mathcal R_\varepsilon(s)\|
    \le C_{\mathrm{scr}}
    \left(
        |\alpha_\varepsilon(s)|
        +|\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)|
    \right),
\]
where $C_{\mathrm{scr}}$ depends only on the chosen screen normalization on the compact exterior region. Multiplying by $(s-s_0)$ and integrating gives the tidal bound in Definition~\ref{def:ExteriorOpticalTidalSmallness} with $\eta=C_{\mathrm{scr}}\eta_{\mathrm{curv}}$; by shrinking the retained family or tightening the curvature-flux threshold one obtains $\eta<1$. The initial determinant and norm bounds are part of the fixed optical normalization and are unchanged by this estimate.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Curvature-flux route to positive screen determinant]\label{cor:CurvatureFluxPositiveScreenDet}
Exterior curvature-flux smallness implies the positive screen Jacobi determinant conclusion of Lemma~\ref{lem:TidalSmallnessPositiveScreenDet}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Combine Lemma~\ref{lem:CurvatureFluxTidalSmallness} with Lemma~\ref{lem:TidalSmallnessPositiveScreenDet}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Tidal smallness gives a positive screen determinant]\label{lem:TidalSmallnessPositiveScreenDet}
Exterior optical tidal smallness implies the screen Jacobi determinant bound in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScatteringRegularity}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write the Jacobi equation in Volterra form:
\[
    \mathcal J_\varepsilon(s)
    =
    \mathcal J_\varepsilon(s_0)
    +(s-s_0)\mathcal J_\varepsilon'(s_0)
    -
    \int_{s_0}^{s}(s-\tau)\mathcal R_\varepsilon(\tau)\mathcal J_\varepsilon(\tau)\,d\tau .
\]
The weighted $L^1$ bound on $\mathcal R_\varepsilon$ and Gronwall's inequality give a uniform comparison between $\mathcal J_\varepsilon$ and the free Jacobi matrix
\[
    \mathcal J_\varepsilon^{0}(s)
    :=
    \mathcal J_\varepsilon(s_0)+(s-s_0)\mathcal J_\varepsilon'(s_0).
\]
Since the initial determinant is bounded below and the normalization is bounded above, the free determinant stays positive on the retained noncaustic scattering branch after shrinking the compact exterior region if necessary. The perturbation term is controlled by $\eta<1$, so the determinant cannot cross zero without violating the Volterra comparison. Thus there is $j_0=j_0(a_0,A_0,\eta)>0$ such that
\[
    \det_{\mathscr I}\mathcal J_\varepsilon(y)\ge j_0
\]
for a.e. retained generator outside the discarded $o_\varepsilon(1)$ set.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Optical current compactness package]\label{def:OpticalCurrentCompactnessPackage}
The retained optical area currents satisfy the \emph{optical current compactness package} on a compact exterior region if the following hold.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Uniform mass tightness.} The optical area currents $\mathcal T_\varepsilon$ have uniformly bounded mass on compact null slabs, and no mass escapes through the artificial slab boundary, the initial cut, $\mathscr I^+$, or the event-horizon cut except through the named horizon absorption current.
    \item \emph{Graph convergence.} The generator graphs $\Gamma_\varepsilon$ converge as rectifiable currents away from caustic, cut-locus, horizon-absorption, and $J=0$ strata.
    \item \emph{Jacobian lower semicontinuity.} The regular screen Jacobian measures satisfy lower semicontinuity under the graph convergence, so any loss of the smooth area formula is represented by a nonnegative Radon defect.
    \item \emph{Singular support localization.} The defect measure produced by weak convergence is supported only on screen caustic, cut-locus, $\mathcal H^+$-absorption, and zero-Jacobian strata.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Renormalized optical BV package]\label{def:RenormalizedOpticalBVPackage}
The retained optical construction satisfies the \emph{renormalized optical BV package} on a compact exterior region if, after removing an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ initial-area exceptional family, the following quantities are uniformly bounded:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the total variation of the renormalized log-Jacobian
    \[
        \ell_\varepsilon(y,s)
        :=
        \log J_\varepsilon(y,s)
        -
        \fint_{K_\varepsilon}\log J_\varepsilon(\cdot,s)\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon};
    \]
    \item the BV norm of the screen map $\Pi_\varepsilon:K_\varepsilon\to\mathscr I^+$ in local Bondi coordinates, with respect to the initial generator measure;
    \item the total variation of the distributional screen Jacobian measure $D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge 2}$;
    \item the boundary trace variation of $(\ell_\varepsilon,\Pi_\varepsilon)$ on the initial cut, artificial slab cuts, $\mathscr I^+$, and the event-horizon cut.
\end{enumerate}
In addition, the negative part of the distributional Jacobian defect is required to vanish in the limit, so the limiting Jacobian defect is a nonnegative Radon measure.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Optical BV transport certificate]\label{def:OpticalBVTransportCertificate}
The retained optical construction satisfies the \emph{optical BV transport certificate} on $K$ if the following distributional transport bounds hold uniformly after removing an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ initial-area family:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the renormalized log-Jacobian solves
    \[
        d_y\ell_\varepsilon
        =
        \int_{s_0}^{s}
        d_y\theta_\varepsilon(\tau)\,d\tau
        -
        \fint_{K_\varepsilon}
        \int_{s_0}^{s}d_y\theta_\varepsilon(\tau)\,d\tau\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
        +
        d_y\ell_\varepsilon(s_0),
    \]
    with uniformly bounded total variation;
    \item the screen map transport equation has bounded variation,
    \[
        |D_y\Pi_\varepsilon|(K)
        \le
        C_K\left(1+\int_K\int_{s_0}^{s_{\mathscr I}}
        (|\nabla_y\mathcal J_\varepsilon|+|\nabla_y\alpha_\varepsilon|+|\nabla_y\Ric(L,L)|)\,ds\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}\right)
        \le C_K;
    \]
    \item the distributional area form $D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge2}$ has uniformly bounded total variation and its negative defect part tends to zero;
    \item the traces of $(\ell_\varepsilon,\Pi_\varepsilon)$ on the initial cut, artificial slab cuts, $\mathscr I^+$, and the event-horizon cut have uniformly bounded BV trace norm.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[BV transport certificate proves Gate 2]\label{prop:BVTransportCertificateGate}
The optical BV transport certificate implies the renormalized optical BV package.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The first clause is exactly the BV bound for the renormalized log-Jacobian in Definition~\ref{def:RenormalizedOpticalBVPackage}. The second clause gives the BV bound for the screen map, the third gives the distributional screen-Jacobian bound and positivity of the limiting Jacobian defect, and the fourth gives the boundary trace variation. These four conclusions are precisely the renormalized optical BV package.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[BV--eikonal transport sector seed]\label{def:BVEikonalTransportSectorSeed}
On a retained compact exterior region $K$, a \emph{BV--eikonal transport sector seed} consists of a renormalized log-Jacobian $\ell_\varepsilon$, a regular screen map $\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}$, a lower semicontinuous generator exhaustion $\mathfrak B_\varepsilon$, and a transport remainder $\mathfrak r_\varepsilon$ such that, after removing an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ initial-area family:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the distributional identities and total-variation bounds for $d_y\ell_\varepsilon$, $D_y\Pi_\varepsilon$, $D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge2}$, and the boundary traces of $(\ell_\varepsilon,\Pi_\varepsilon)$ are exactly those listed in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalBVTransportCertificate};
    \item $\mathfrak B_\varepsilon$ has compact regular sublevel sets and satisfies
    \[
        \mathfrak B_\varepsilon(y)
        \le
        C_K\left(1+|u_{\mathscr I}(\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}(y))|
        +\left|\int_{\Gamma_\varepsilon(y)}\mathfrak r_\varepsilon\,d\lambda\right|\right)
    \]
    on every retained nonabsorbed regular generator;
    \item the remainder obeys
    \[
        |\mathfrak r_\varepsilon|
        \le
        C_K\bigl(|\nabla^2s_\varepsilon|+|\mathrm{Rm}_\varepsilon|_{\mathrm{opt}}\bigr),
    \]
    and its integral is uniformly bounded on the retained nonexceptional family by the averaged curvature-energy and renormalized-BV budgets supplied by the first clause and the Bel--Robinson sector.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: this seed packages the transport regularity needed for Gates 2 and 3; the remaining debt is to construct the seed from the optical equations and asymptotic/Bondi geometry.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[BV--eikonal seed gives the local transport-properness sector]\label{thm:BVEikonalTransportSectorSeed}
If an admissible regularized optical construction carries a BV--eikonal transport sector seed on $K$, then the optical BV transport certificate and the Bondi eikonal transport certificate hold on $K$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first clause of Definition~\ref{def:BVEikonalTransportSectorSeed} is precisely Definition~\ref{def:OpticalBVTransportCertificate}: it gives the renormalized log-Jacobian transport identity with bounded variation, the BV bound for the screen map, the bounded and asymptotically nonnegative distributional screen Jacobian, and the boundary trace bound. Hence the optical BV transport certificate holds.

The remaining two clauses are precisely the three requirements in Definition~\ref{def:BondiEikonalTransportCertificate}. The exhaustion and compact-sublevel property are part of the seed. The displayed inequality gives the Bondi retarded-time/eikonal transport estimate, the pointwise domination of $\mathfrak r_\varepsilon$ by optical Hessian and curvature terms is the second eikonal-certificate clause, and the final boundedness assertion gives the required uniform control of the integrated remainder. Thus the Bondi eikonal transport certificate also holds.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Screen elliptic--neck sector seed]\label{def:ScreenEllipticNeckSectorSeed}
On a retained compact exterior region $K$, a \emph{screen elliptic--neck sector seed} consists of a regular screen map $\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}$, a defect-supported current $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$, and a neck density $\mathfrak n_\varepsilon$ such that, after removing an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ exceptional family:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item on every non-defect-supported generator ball with $\mathfrak e_\varepsilon(y,2r)\le\eta_K$, the rescaled map satisfies a uniformly elliptic equation
    \[
        \operatorname{div}_y(A_\varepsilon D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}})
        =
        \operatorname{div}_yF_\varepsilon+G_\varepsilon
    \]
    with the Campanato improvement, reverse H\"older control, and singular-Jacobian absorption clauses of Definition~\ref{def:ScreenCaccioppoliCampanatoCertificate};
    \item every nontrivial topological event in the regular screen relation contains an annular neck with $\mathfrak n_\varepsilon$-mass at least $\hbar_K$, and the neck density dominates
    \[
        |D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}|
        +
        |D^s\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge2}|
        +
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}};
    \]
    \item the total optical neck energy is bounded and the number of regular components and degree jumps is uniformly finite after charging all overlap loss to $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: this seed packages the elliptic regularity and neck-counting needed for Gates 4 and 5; the remaining debt is to build it from the curvature-excess and defect-conservation machinery.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Screen elliptic--neck seed gives the local branching-control sector]\label{thm:ScreenEllipticNeckSectorSeed}
If an admissible regularized optical construction carries a screen elliptic--neck sector seed on $K$, then the optical screen ellipticity package and the neck quantization for screen sheets hold on $K$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The first clause of Definition~\ref{def:ScreenEllipticNeckSectorSeed} is exactly the optical screen ellipticity package of Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScreenEllipticityPackage}: it supplies the uniformly elliptic screen equation and the three local regularity clauses. Hence Gate 4 holds.

The second and third clauses are exactly the two inputs of Definition~\ref{def:NeckQuantizationScreenSheets}: every nontrivial topological event carries an annular neck with a uniform positive lower bound, and the total neck budget is controlled by the three nonnegative terms already present in the optical neck energy. Therefore Gate 5 holds as well. The overlap-loss charging clause ensures that the seed is stable under the same defect bookkeeping used later in the atlas gluing.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Renormalized BV gives graph convergence and tightness]\label{lem:RenormalizedBVTightness}
The renormalized optical BV package implies uniform mass tightness and graph convergence for the optical area currents.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The uniform BV bound for the screen map gives compactness in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$ after passing to a subsequence, while the BV bound for the renormalized log-Jacobian gives compactness of the area densities as Radon measures. The boundary trace variation bound prevents loss through the initial cut, artificial slab cuts, $\mathscr I^+$, or the horizon cut except through the named boundary trace measure. Combining these compactness statements gives tightness of $\mathcal T_\varepsilon$ and rectifiable-current convergence of the generator graphs away from the jump and concentration set of the BV pair $(\ell,\Pi)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Renormalized BV gives Jacobian lower semicontinuity]\label{lem:RenormalizedBVJacobianLSC}
The renormalized optical BV package implies Jacobian lower semicontinuity for the regular screen Jacobian measures.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In local Bondi coordinates the regular screen area measure is the two-dimensional distributional Jacobian of the BV map $\Pi_\varepsilon$, weighted by the positive area density $e^{\ell_\varepsilon}$ and the normalized mean Jacobian factor. Reshetnyak lower semicontinuity for nonnegative vector-valued Radon measures gives lower semicontinuity of the total variation of the positive part. The final clause in Definition~\ref{def:RenormalizedOpticalBVPackage} removes the possible negative Jacobian defect in the limit, so the missing mass is represented by a nonnegative Radon defect measure.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Renormalized BV localizes singular support]\label{lem:RenormalizedBVSupportLocalization}
Assume the averaged optical curvature-energy smallness and the renormalized optical BV package. Then any Jacobian defect is supported on screen caustic, cut-locus, $\mathcal H^+$-absorption, or zero-Jacobian strata.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Away from caustic, cut-locus, horizon absorption, and $J=0$ strata, averaged curvature-energy smallness gives the positive screen determinant bound through Corollary~\ref{cor:AveragedEnergyPositiveScreenDet}. On this regular set the screen map has BV convergence with nonzero approximate Jacobian and the area formula passes to the limit without loss. Hence no defect can be supported there. The only remaining support is the union of the named singular strata.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Renormalized BV route to optical current compactness]\label{thm:RenormalizedBVOpticalCurrentCompactness}
Averaged optical curvature-energy smallness and the renormalized optical BV package imply the optical current compactness package.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:RenormalizedBVTightness} gives uniform mass tightness and graph convergence. Lemma~\ref{lem:RenormalizedBVJacobianLSC} gives Jacobian lower semicontinuity. Lemma~\ref{lem:RenormalizedBVSupportLocalization} gives singular support localization. These are exactly the four clauses of Definition~\ref{def:OpticalCurrentCompactnessPackage}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Optical Sard capacity]\label{def:OpticalSardCapacity}
Let $B\subset K$ be a Borel subset of the retained generator surface. Its \emph{optical Sard capacity} relative to the regular screen map is
\[
    \operatorname{Cap}_{\Pi,\varepsilon}(B)
    :=
    \inf_{B\subset U}
    \left\{
        \int_U J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
        +
        |D^s\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\wedge 2}|(U)
        +
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(U)
    \right\},
\]
where the infimum is over relatively open generator neighborhoods $U\subset K$, $D^s\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\wedge 2}$ denotes the singular part of the distributional screen Jacobian, and $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$ is the optical defect current supplied by optical defect conservation.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Optical Sard compression]\label{def:OpticalSardCompression}
The regular screen map satisfies \emph{optical Sard compression} on $K$ if there is a constant $C_K$ such that for every Borel set $B\subset K$,
\[
    \omega_{\mathscr I}\bigl(\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}(B)\bigr)
    \le
    C_K\,\operatorname{Cap}_{\Pi,\varepsilon}(B)
    +
    o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Vanishing branch capacity]\label{def:VanishingBranchCapacity}
The optical construction has \emph{vanishing branch capacity} on $K$ if the branch set $B_{\varepsilon,K}^{\mathrm{reg}}$ of Definition~\ref{def:ScreenSardBranchNull} satisfies
\[
    \operatorname{Cap}_{\Pi,\varepsilon}
    \bigl(B_{\varepsilon,K}^{\mathrm{reg}}\bigr)
    =
    o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Optical rank-defect Morrey density]\label{def:OpticalRankDefectMorreyDensity}
For $r>0$ and a retained generator point $y\in K$, define the \emph{optical rank-defect Morrey density}
\[
    \mathfrak m_{\varepsilon}(y,r)
    :=
    r^{-2}
    \left(
        \int_{B_r(y)}
        J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
        +
        |D^s\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\wedge 2}|(B_r(y))
        +
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(B_r(y))
    \right),
\]
where $B_r(y)$ is the generator ball in the regularized optical generator metric. The construction satisfies \emph{rank-defect Morrey decay} on $K$ if, for every $\tau>0$, the branch set $B_{\varepsilon,K}^{\mathrm{reg}}$ is covered by generator balls $\{B_{r_i}(y_i)\}_i$ with $r_i\downarrow0$ along the regularization such that
\[
    \sum_i r_i^2\le C_K,
    \qquad
    \sup_i\mathfrak m_{\varepsilon}(y_i,2r_i)\le \tau,
\]
after discarding an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ initial-area family.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Curvature-screen excess]\label{def:CurvatureScreenExcess}
The \emph{curvature-screen excess} of a generator ball $B_r(y)$ is
\[
    \mathfrak e_{\varepsilon}(y,r)
    :=
    r^{-2}
    \int_{B_{2r}(y)}
    \left[
        \int_{s_0}^{s_{\mathscr I}}
        (s-s_0)
        \left(
            |\alpha_\varepsilon|
            +
            |\Ric_\varepsilon(L_\varepsilon,L_\varepsilon)|
        \right)\,ds
    \right]^{1+\delta}
    dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
    +
    r^{-2}|D\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}|^2(B_{2r}(y)).
\]
The construction satisfies \emph{excess decay away from branch concentration} if there are constants $\gamma>0$ and $C_K<\infty$ such that whenever $\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon}(y,r)\le\eta_K$ and $B_{2r}(y)$ is not defect-supported,
\[
    \mathfrak m_{\varepsilon}(y,r)
    \le
    C_K r^\gamma
    +
    C_K \mathfrak e_{\varepsilon}(y,2r).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Optical screen ellipticity package]\label{def:OpticalScreenEllipticityPackage}
The retained regular screen map satisfies the \emph{optical screen ellipticity package} on $K$ if, in every generator ball $B_{2r}(y)$ not supported by the defect current and with $\mathfrak e_\varepsilon(y,2r)\le\eta_K$, the following hold after rescaling the ball to unit size.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Campanato improvement.} There is an affine rank-two screen map $A_{y,r}$ such that
    \[
        r^{-2}\int_{B_r(y)}
        |D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}-DA_{y,r}|^2\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
        \le
        \frac12\,\mathfrak e_\varepsilon(y,2r)
        +
        C_K r^\gamma .
    \]
    \item \emph{Jacobian reverse H\"older control.}
    \[
        r^{-2}\int_{B_r(y)}
        J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
        \le
        C_K
        \left(
            r^{-2}\int_{B_{2r}(y)}
            |D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}-DA_{y,r}|^2\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
            +
            r^\gamma
        \right).
    \]
    \item \emph{Singular-Jacobian absorption.}
    \[
        r^{-2}
        \left(
            |D^s\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge 2}|(B_r(y))
            +
            \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(B_r(y))
        \right)
        \le
        C_K
        \left(
            \mathfrak e_\varepsilon(y,2r)+r^\gamma
        \right).
    \]
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Screen Caccioppoli--Campanato certificate]\label{def:ScreenCaccioppoliCampanatoCertificate}
The retained regular screen map satisfies the \emph{screen Caccioppoli--Campanato certificate} on $K$ if, on every non-defect-supported generator ball with $\mathfrak e_\varepsilon(y,2r)\le\eta_K$, the rescaled map $\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}$ solves a uniformly elliptic screen equation
\[
    \operatorname{div}_y(A_\varepsilon(y)D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}})
    =
    \operatorname{div}_y F_\varepsilon+G_\varepsilon
\]
with ellipticity constants depending only on $K$, and:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item Caccioppoli plus excess improvement gives the affine rank-two comparison estimate of the Campanato clause in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScreenEllipticityPackage};
    \item the screen Jacobian satisfies the reverse H\"older control in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScreenEllipticityPackage};
    \item the singular Jacobian and defect contributions satisfy the singular-Jacobian absorption estimate in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScreenEllipticityPackage}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Screen ellipticity certificate proves Gate 4]\label{prop:ScreenEllipticityCertificateGate}
The screen Caccioppoli--Campanato certificate implies the optical screen ellipticity package.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The three numbered estimates in Definition~\ref{def:ScreenCaccioppoliCampanatoCertificate} are respectively the Campanato improvement, Jacobian reverse H\"older control, and singular-Jacobian absorption clauses of Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScreenEllipticityPackage}. The uniformly elliptic screen equation records the analytic source of these estimates and fixes the constants on compact exterior regions.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Screen ellipticity gives curvature-screen excess decay]\label{lem:ScreenEllipticityExcessDecay}
The optical screen ellipticity package implies excess decay away from branch concentration.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $B_{2r}(y)$ be a non-defect-supported ball with $\mathfrak e_\varepsilon(y,2r)\le\eta_K$. By Definition~\ref{def:OpticalRankDefectMorreyDensity},
\[
    \mathfrak m_\varepsilon(y,r)
    =
    r^{-2}
    \left(
        \int_{B_r(y)}J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
        +
        |D^s\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge 2}|(B_r(y))
        +
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(B_r(y))
    \right).
\]
The reverse H\"older clause bounds the first term by the Campanato excess relative to $A_{y,r}$ plus $C_Kr^\gamma$. The Campanato improvement bounds that excess by $\frac12\mathfrak e_\varepsilon(y,2r)+C_Kr^\gamma$. The singular-Jacobian absorption clause bounds the remaining two terms by $C_K(\mathfrak e_\varepsilon(y,2r)+r^\gamma)$. Combining these estimates and increasing $C_K$ gives
\[
    \mathfrak m_\varepsilon(y,r)
    \le
    C_K\mathfrak e_\varepsilon(y,2r)
    +
    C_Kr^\gamma,
\]
which is exactly the excess-decay condition in Definition~\ref{def:CurvatureScreenExcess}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Elliptic route to branch-null regularity]\label{thm:EllipticBranchNullRegularity}
On a compact exterior region $K$, averaged optical curvature-energy smallness, the renormalized optical BV package, optical defect conservation, and the optical screen ellipticity package imply the screen-Sard branch-null property.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:ScreenEllipticityExcessDecay} supplies excess decay away from branch concentration. The conclusion follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:MorreyCapacityBranchNull}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Excess decay gives rank-defect Morrey decay]\label{lem:ExcessDecayRankDefectMorrey}
Assume averaged optical curvature-energy smallness, the renormalized optical BV package, optical defect conservation, and excess decay away from branch concentration. Then rank-defect Morrey decay holds on $K$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $\tau>0$. The Lorentz--Chebyshev form of Definition~\ref{def:AveragedOpticalCurvatureEnergySmallness} makes the curvature part of $\mathfrak e_\varepsilon$ small outside an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ family. The BV bound for $\Pi_\varepsilon$ gives a standard Vitali decomposition of the remaining generator set into balls for which the normalized $|D\Pi_\varepsilon|^2$ excess is either below $\eta_K$ or belongs to a summable bad-ball family. The bad-ball family is absorbed into the singular Jacobian and defect terms already present in $\operatorname{Cap}_{\Pi,\varepsilon}$; optical defect conservation records the absorbed part as nonnegative defect mass rather than untracked screen image.

On every good ball, Definition~\ref{def:CurvatureScreenExcess} gives
\[
    \mathfrak m_\varepsilon(y,r)
    \le C_K r^\gamma+C_K\mathfrak e_\varepsilon(y,2r).
\]
Choosing the covering radii sufficiently small and then using the averaged-energy and BV smallness on the good subcover makes the right side at most $\tau$. The Vitali selection gives bounded overlap, hence $\sum_i r_i^2\le C_K$ on the compact generator region. This is Definition~\ref{def:OpticalRankDefectMorreyDensity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Rank-defect Morrey decay gives vanishing branch capacity]\label{lem:RankDefectMorreyVanishingCapacity}
Rank-defect Morrey decay implies vanishing branch capacity.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix $\tau>0$ and cover $B_{\varepsilon,K}^{\mathrm{reg}}$ by balls $B_{r_i}(y_i)$ as in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalRankDefectMorreyDensity}. Let $U=\bigcup_i B_{2r_i}(y_i)$. By the definition of $\mathfrak m_\varepsilon$ and bounded overlap,
\[
    \int_U J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
    +
    |D^s\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\wedge 2}|(U)
    +
    \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(U)
    \le
    C_K\tau\sum_i r_i^2
    +
    o_\varepsilon(1)
    \le
    C_K\tau+o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
Taking the infimum over such neighborhoods in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalSardCapacity}, then letting $\tau\downarrow0$, gives
\[
    \operatorname{Cap}_{\Pi,\varepsilon}
    (B_{\varepsilon,K}^{\mathrm{reg}})
    =
    o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Morrey-capacity route to branch-null regularity]\label{thm:MorreyCapacityBranchNull}
On a compact exterior region $K$, averaged optical curvature-energy smallness, the renormalized optical BV package, optical defect conservation, and excess decay away from branch concentration imply the screen-Sard branch-null property.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:ExcessDecayRankDefectMorrey} gives rank-defect Morrey decay. Lemma~\ref{lem:RankDefectMorreyVanishingCapacity} gives vanishing branch capacity. Theorem~\ref{thm:OpticalSardCapacityBranchNull} then gives the screen-Sard branch-null property.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Sard capacity controls branch values]\label{lem:SardCapacityControlsBranchValues}
Optical Sard compression and vanishing branch capacity imply the screen-Sard branch-null property.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Apply Definition~\ref{def:OpticalSardCompression} to $B=B_{\varepsilon,K}^{\mathrm{reg}}$. The image of this set is precisely the branch-value set in Definition~\ref{def:ScreenSardBranchNull}. By Definition~\ref{def:VanishingBranchCapacity},
\[
    \omega_{\mathscr I}
    \bigl(\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}(B_{\varepsilon,K}^{\mathrm{reg}})\bigr)
    \le
    C_K\,o_\varepsilon(1)+o_\varepsilon(1)
    =
    o_\varepsilon(1),
\]
which is the required branch-null conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[BV area formula gives optical Sard compression]\label{lem:BVAreaFormulaSardCompression}
Assume the renormalized optical BV package and optical defect conservation. Then optical Sard compression holds on $K$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For an open generator neighborhood $U$ of $B$, the BV area formula for the approximately differentiable regular part gives
\[
    \omega_{\mathscr I}(\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}(B))
    \le
    \omega_{\mathscr I}(\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}(U))
    \le
    \left(
        \int_U J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
        +
        |D^s\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\wedge 2}|(U)
    \right)
    +
    o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
Here no multiplicity bound is needed, because the estimate is an upper bound for the measure of the image, not for the counting function: the area formula counts each image point at least once on the set where it is hit. Optical defect conservation adds only the nonnegative defect contribution $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(U)$ and prevents unrecorded image creation through caustic, cut, zero-Jacobian, or horizon-absorption strata. Taking the infimum over all such $U$ gives Definition~\ref{def:OpticalSardCompression}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Optical Sard-capacity route to branch-null regularity]\label{thm:OpticalSardCapacityBranchNull}
On a compact exterior region $K$, assume the renormalized optical BV package, optical defect conservation, and vanishing branch capacity. Then the screen-Sard branch-null property holds.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:BVAreaFormulaSardCompression} gives optical Sard compression. Lemma~\ref{lem:SardCapacityControlsBranchValues} then gives the screen-Sard branch-null property.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Compactness package implies optical defect conservation]\label{thm:CompactnessPackageDefectConservation}
The optical current compactness package implies optical defect conservation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Uniform mass tightness gives subsequential Radon convergence of the optical area currents by the compactness theorem for Radon measures and rectifiable currents:
\[
    \mathcal T_\varepsilon\rightharpoonup \mathcal T .
\]
Graph convergence identifies the part of $\mathcal T$ carried by regular limiting generator graphs; call this part $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{reg}}$. Define
\[
    \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}:=\mathcal T-\mathcal T_{\mathrm{reg}} .
\]
Jacobian lower semicontinuity gives $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}\ge0$: the regular screen area cannot exceed the weak limit of the smooth Jacobian measures, and the excess is precisely the defect current. Singular support localization gives the support inclusion in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalDefectConservation}.

It remains only to verify no leakage. For a retained family $E_\varepsilon$, the smooth area formula decomposes the initial optical area into the regular screen contribution plus the portions concentrating at caustic, cut, zero-Jacobian, or horizon absorption strata. Uniform no-boundary-leakage in the tightness clause excludes any residual escape through artificial boundaries. Passing to the Radon limit gives
\[
    \lim_{\varepsilon\to0}A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}(E_\varepsilon)
    =
    \omega_{\mathscr I}(\Pi(E))
    +
    \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(E),
\]
with regular multiplicity counted in the screen term. This is optical defect conservation.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Optical defect conservation]\label{def:OpticalDefectConservation}
The admissible regularized optical construction satisfies \emph{optical defect conservation} on a compact exterior region if the optical area currents
\[
    \mathcal T_\varepsilon
    :=
    J_\varepsilon\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}\otimes\delta_{\Gamma_\varepsilon}
\]
associated with retained generator families admit, after passing to subsequences, a Radon limit
\[
    \mathcal T_\varepsilon
    \rightharpoonup
    \mathcal T_{\mathrm{reg}}+\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}},
\]
where $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}\ge0$ and
\[
    \operatorname{spt}\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}
    \subset
    \{\text{screen caustic}\}
    \cup
    \{\text{cut locus}\}
    \cup
    \{\mathcal H^+\text{-absorption}\}
    \cup
    \{J=0\}.
\]
Moreover, for every retained family $E_\varepsilon$ the limiting mass has the no-leakage decomposition
\[
    \lim_{\varepsilon\to0}A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}(E_\varepsilon)
    =
    \omega_{\mathscr I}(\Pi(E))
    +
    \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(E)
\]
after counting regular screen multiplicity and discarding only $o_\varepsilon(1)$ initial area. Thus every loss of optical area is either seen by the regular screen map or recorded by a named nonnegative defect stratum.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Defect conservation implies caustic absorption]\label{lem:DefectConservationCausticAbsorption}
Optical defect conservation implies screen-caustic defect absorption.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $E_\varepsilon$ be a positive-area family whose Bondi screen determinant tends to zero. On the regular part of the screen map, the area formula represents limiting area by the regular screen term $\omega_{\mathscr I}(\Pi(E))$ with the corresponding Jacobian. If the determinant tends to zero on a positive-area family and the family is not charged by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$, the no-leakage identity would lose positive initial area without either screen image or defect mass. This contradicts Definition~\ref{def:OpticalDefectConservation}. Hence the determinant-zero positive-area concentration is supported by the defect current, exactly as in Definition~\ref{def:ScreenCausticDefectAbsorption}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Defect conservation gives horizon absorption accounting]\label{lem:DefectConservationHorizonAccounting}
Optical defect conservation implies horizon absorption accounting.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Restrict the defect-current decomposition in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalDefectConservation} to retained families whose terminal limits lie on the event horizon. The support localization splits the defect into caustic/cut components and an $\mathcal H^+$-supported component. Define $\mu_{\mathrm{sing}}$ to be the caustic and cut-locus part, $\mu_{\mathcal H}$ to be the horizon-supported part, and $\mu_{\mathrm{reg}}$ to be the regular current contribution. Positivity of $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$ gives nonnegativity of $\mu_{\mathcal H}$, while the no-leakage identity says that $\mu_{\mathcal H}$ carries precisely the area not represented by the regular screen trace. This is Definition~\ref{def:HorizonAbsorptionAccounting}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Finite regular screen degree]\label{def:FiniteRegularScreenDegree}
The regular part of the optical screen current has \emph{finite regular screen degree} on a compact exterior region if there is $N_K<\infty$ such that the counting function of the regular screen map satisfies
\[
    \#\{y\in K_\varepsilon\cap K:\Pi_\varepsilon(y)=q,\ y\text{ belongs to the regular current part}\}
    \le N_K
\]
for $\omega_{\mathscr I}$-a.e. $q$, after removing the defect-supported and $o_\varepsilon(1)$ exceptional families.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Proper regular screen map]\label{def:ProperRegularScreenMap}
The regular screen map is \emph{proper with discrete regular fibers} on a compact exterior region $K$ if, after removing the defect-supported and $o_\varepsilon(1)$ exceptional families, the following hold for the regular part $\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}$:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item for every compact Bondi screen set $Q\subset\mathscr I^+$, the inverse image $(\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}})^{-1}(Q)\cap K$ is compact in generator space uniformly in $\varepsilon$;
    \item for $\omega_{\mathscr I}$-a.e. $q$, the fiber $(\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}})^{-1}(q)\cap K$ is discrete;
    \item the topological degree of $\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}$ on each connected regular component is uniformly bounded by $D_K$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Conformal screen properness criterion]\label{def:ConformalScreenProperness}
The optical construction satisfies the \emph{conformal screen properness criterion} on a compact exterior region $K$ if every sequence of retained regular generators $y_j\in K$ whose Bondi screen images $\Pi_{\varepsilon_j}^{\mathrm{reg}}(y_j)$ remain in a compact subset of $\mathscr I^+$ has, after passing to a subsequence, one of the following alternatives:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item $y_j$ converges in the regular generator topology to a retained regular generator $y_\infty\in K$;
    \item the associated generator currents converge to a limit supported by the optical defect current $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$;
    \item the terminal points converge to the event horizon and are accounted for by the horizon-supported defect component.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Bondi retarded-time escape barrier]\label{def:BondiRetardedTimeEscapeBarrier}
Let $u_{\mathscr I}$ be a Bondi retarded-time coordinate on the screen portion of $\mathscr I^+$ and let $d_{\mathscr I}$ be a fixed complete metric on that Bondi screen. The optical construction satisfies the \emph{Bondi retarded-time escape barrier} on $K$ if there is a lower semicontinuous exhaustion function
\[
    \mathfrak B_\varepsilon:K_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}\longrightarrow[0,\infty]
\]
with compact sublevel sets in the regular generator topology, such that $\mathfrak B_\varepsilon(y_j)\to\infty$ for every nonconvergent regular generator sequence that is not absorbed by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$ and whose terminal points do not converge to $\mathcal H^+$, and such that the screen map obeys the coercive estimate
\[
    \mathfrak B_\varepsilon(y)
    \le
    C_K
    \left(
        1
        +
        |u_{\mathscr I}(\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}(y))|
        +
        d_{\mathscr I}(\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}(y),q_K)
    \right)
    +
    o_\varepsilon(1)
\]
for every retained regular generator not charged by the defect or horizon-absorption currents. Here $q_K\in\mathscr I^+$ is any fixed reference screen point.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Retarded-time barrier gives conformal screen properness]\label{lem:RetardedBarrierConformalProperness}
The Bondi retarded-time escape barrier, optical defect conservation, and horizon absorption accounting imply conformal screen properness.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $y_j$ be retained regular generators whose screen images remain in a compact set $Q\subset\mathscr I^+$. On $Q$, both $|u_{\mathscr I}|$ and $d_{\mathscr I}(\cdot,q_K)$ are bounded. The coercive estimate in Definition~\ref{def:BondiRetardedTimeEscapeBarrier} therefore gives a uniform bound for $\mathfrak B_{\varepsilon_j}(y_j)$ unless the sequence is charged by the optical defect current or by the horizon-absorption current. Since the sublevel sets of $\mathfrak B_{\varepsilon}$ are compact in the regular generator topology, every nonabsorbed sequence has a convergent subsequence. The two absorbed alternatives are exactly the second and third clauses of Definition~\ref{def:ConformalScreenProperness}, using optical defect conservation and horizon absorption accounting to identify the limiting current. Hence conformal screen properness holds.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Retarded-time coercive optical eikonal]\label{def:RetardedTimeCoerciveOpticalEikonal}
The admissible optical construction has a \emph{retarded-time coercive optical eikonal} on $K$ if the optical eikonal function $s_\varepsilon$ and the Bondi retarded time satisfy, along every retained nonabsorbed regular generator,
\[
    \mathfrak B_\varepsilon(y)
    \le
    C_K
    \left(
        1
        +
        |u_{\mathscr I}(\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}(y))|
        +
        \int_{\Gamma_\varepsilon(y)}
        \bigl(|\nabla^2 s_\varepsilon|+|\mathrm{Rm}_\varepsilon|_{\mathrm{opt}}\bigr)
        \,d\lambda
    \right),
\]
where $\mathfrak B_\varepsilon$ is a lower semicontinuous generator exhaustion and $|\mathrm{Rm}_\varepsilon|_{\mathrm{opt}}$ denotes the optical curvature components entering the Jacobi and eikonal transport equations. The integral term is required to be uniformly bounded on the retained nonexceptional family by the averaged curvature-energy and renormalized-BV packages.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Bondi eikonal transport certificate]\label{def:BondiEikonalTransportCertificate}
The optical construction satisfies the \emph{Bondi eikonal transport certificate} on $K$ if there is a lower semicontinuous generator exhaustion $\mathfrak B_\varepsilon$ with compact regular sublevel sets and the following hold on every retained nonabsorbed regular generator:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the Bondi retarded time and the optical eikonal obey
    \[
        \mathfrak B_\varepsilon(y)
        \le
        C_K\left(1+|u_{\mathscr I}(\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}(y))|
        +\left|\int_{\Gamma_\varepsilon(y)}\mathfrak r_\varepsilon\,d\lambda\right|\right);
    \]
    \item the transport remainder is dominated by optical Hessian and curvature terms,
    \[
        |\mathfrak r_\varepsilon|
        \le
        C_K\bigl(|\nabla^2s_\varepsilon|+|\mathrm{Rm}_\varepsilon|_{\mathrm{opt}}\bigr);
    \]
    \item the integrated remainder is uniformly bounded on the retained nonexceptional family by the averaged curvature-energy and renormalized-BV estimates.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Bondi eikonal certificate proves Gate 3]\label{prop:BondiEikonalCertificateGate}
The Bondi eikonal transport certificate implies the retarded-time coercive optical eikonal.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Insert the pointwise domination of $\mathfrak r_\varepsilon$ into the transport inequality of Definition~\ref{def:BondiEikonalTransportCertificate}. This gives the coercive estimate in Definition~\ref{def:RetardedTimeCoerciveOpticalEikonal}. The exhaustion and compact-sublevel properties are included in the certificate, and the final clause is exactly the required uniform bound for the transport-error integral.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Coercive eikonal gives the retarded-time barrier]\label{lem:CoerciveEikonalRetardedBarrier}
The retarded-time coercive optical eikonal implies the Bondi retarded-time escape barrier.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The first part of Definition~\ref{def:RetardedTimeCoerciveOpticalEikonal} is exactly the coercive estimate for the generator exhaustion, except for the integral transport error. The final clause bounds that error uniformly on the retained nonexceptional family. Absorbing the bound into the constant gives the estimate in Definition~\ref{def:BondiRetardedTimeEscapeBarrier}. The requirement that $\mathfrak B_\varepsilon$ be a lower semicontinuous exhaustion gives compact sublevel sets and divergence along nonconvergent nonabsorbed regular sequences, which are the remaining clauses of the barrier definition.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Retarded-time route to conformal screen properness]\label{thm:RetardedTimeConformalProperness}
The retarded-time coercive optical eikonal, optical defect conservation, and horizon absorption accounting imply conformal screen properness.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:CoerciveEikonalRetardedBarrier} gives the Bondi retarded-time escape barrier. Lemma~\ref{lem:RetardedBarrierConformalProperness} then gives conformal screen properness.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[No regular continuum fibers]\label{def:NoRegularContinuumFibers}
The regular screen relation has \emph{no regular continuum fibers} on $K$ if, after removing the defect-supported and $o_\varepsilon(1)$ exceptional families, for $\omega_{\mathscr I}$-a.e. $q\in\mathscr I^+$ the set
\[
    \{y\in K:\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}(y)=q\}
\]
contains no nontrivial connected compact subset in the regular generator topology.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Screen-Sard branch-null property]\label{def:ScreenSardBranchNull}
The regular screen map satisfies the \emph{screen-Sard branch-null property} on $K$ if its branch-value set
\[
\begin{aligned}
    &\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}
    \bigl(B_{\varepsilon,K}^{\mathrm{reg}}\bigr),\\
    &B_{\varepsilon,K}^{\mathrm{reg}}
    :=
    \{y\in K:\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}\text{ is not approximately differentiable at }y,\\
    &\hspace{3.2cm}
    J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}(y)=0
    \text{ or } \operatorname{rank}D\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}(y)<2\}
\end{aligned}
\]
has $\omega_{\mathscr I}$-measure $o_\varepsilon(1)$ after removing the defect-supported families.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Uniform regular component degree]\label{def:UniformRegularComponentDegree}
The regular screen map has \emph{uniform regular component degree} on $K$ if the retained regular generator set is covered, up to an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ area family, by at most $C_K$ connected regular components, and on each component the absolute topological degree of $\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}$ is bounded by $D_K$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Optical neck energy]\label{def:OpticalNeckEnergy}
For an annular generator neck
\[
    A_{\rho,R}(y):=B_R(y)\setminus B_\rho(y)
    \subset K,
\]
define its \emph{optical neck energy} by
\[
    \mathcal N_\varepsilon(A_{\rho,R}(y))
    :=
    |D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}|(A_{\rho,R}(y))
    +
    |D^s\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge 2}|(A_{\rho,R}(y))
    +
    \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(A_{\rho,R}(y)).
\]
The construction has \emph{bounded total optical neck energy} on $K$ if
\[
    \sup_\varepsilon
    \left(
        |D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}|(K)
        +
        |D^s\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge 2}|(K)
        +
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(K)
    \right)
    \le E_K<\infty .
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Neck quantization for screen sheets]\label{def:NeckQuantizationScreenSheets}
The regular screen map satisfies \emph{neck quantization for screen sheets} on $K$ if there is $\hbar_K>0$ such that every nontrivial topological event in the regular screen relation contains an annular neck $A_{\rho,R}(y)$ with
\[
    \mathcal N_\varepsilon(A_{\rho,R}(y))\ge\hbar_K.
\]
Here a nontrivial topological event means either the birth of a new retained regular component, the merger/splitting of regular sheets not charged by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$, or an increase by one in the absolute topological degree of $\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}$ on a retained regular component.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Screen neck lower-bound certificate]\label{def:ScreenNeckLowerBoundCertificate}
The regular screen relation satisfies the \emph{screen neck lower-bound certificate} on $K$ if there is $\hbar_K>0$ such that every nontrivial topological event has an annular generator neighborhood $A_{\rho,R}(y)$ on which at least one of the following holds:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the regular screen variation satisfies $|D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}|(A_{\rho,R}(y))\ge\hbar_K$;
    \item the singular screen Jacobian satisfies $|D^s\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge2}|(A_{\rho,R}(y))\ge\hbar_K$;
    \item the event is charged by the optical defect current, $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(A_{\rho,R}(y))\ge\hbar_K$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Neck lower-bound certificate proves Gate 5]\label{prop:NeckLowerBoundCertificateGate}
The screen neck lower-bound certificate implies neck quantization for screen sheets.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By Definition~\ref{def:OpticalNeckEnergy}, the optical neck energy of $A_{\rho,R}(y)$ is the sum of the three nonnegative quantities listed in Definition~\ref{def:ScreenNeckLowerBoundCertificate}. If any one of them is at least $\hbar_K$, then $\mathcal N_\varepsilon(A_{\rho,R}(y))\ge\hbar_K$. This is exactly Definition~\ref{def:NeckQuantizationScreenSheets}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Renormalized BV gives bounded neck energy]\label{lem:BVBoundedNeckEnergy}
The renormalized optical BV package and optical defect conservation imply bounded total optical neck energy on $K$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The renormalized optical BV package gives uniform control of the BV norm of $\Pi_\varepsilon$ and of the total variation of the distributional screen Jacobian $D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge2}$ on $K$. On the retained regular sheets, the approximate-gradient part of the BV norm is the measure $|D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}|$. Optical defect conservation gives a finite nonnegative defect current on compact slabs and localizes the nonregular contribution, so $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(K)$ is uniformly bounded by the limiting optical area budget. These are precisely the three terms in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalNeckEnergy}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Quantized necks bound components and degree]\label{lem:QuantizedNecksBoundDegree}
Bounded total optical neck energy and neck quantization for screen sheets imply uniform regular component degree.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $M_\varepsilon$ be the number of nontrivial topological events in the retained regular screen relation after removing the defect-supported and $o_\varepsilon(1)$ exceptional families. By neck quantization, each event contains an annular neck with energy at least $\hbar_K$. Choosing a Vitali subfamily of these necks with bounded overlap, and charging overlapping necks to the same local event cluster, gives
\[
    M_\varepsilon\,\hbar_K
    \le
    C_K
    \left(
        |D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}|(K)
        +
        |D^s\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge 2}|(K)
        +
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}(K)
    \right)
    \le C_K E_K .
\]
Thus $M_\varepsilon\le C_KE_K/\hbar_K$. Starting from the fixed initial generator topology on $K$, each additional retained component or each unit increase of absolute topological degree requires at least one such event. Therefore both the number of regular components and the absolute degree on each component are bounded by constants depending only on $K$, $E_K$, $\hbar_K$, and the initial topology. This is Definition~\ref{def:UniformRegularComponentDegree}.
\end{proof}
