\begin{theorem}[Neck-quantization route to uniform regular component degree]\label{thm:NeckQuantizationUniformDegree}
The renormalized optical BV package, optical defect conservation, and neck quantization for screen sheets imply uniform regular component degree.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:BVBoundedNeckEnergy} gives bounded total optical neck energy. Lemma~\ref{lem:QuantizedNecksBoundDegree} then gives uniform regular component degree.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Conformal properness gives screen-map properness]\label{lem:ConformalPropernessScreenProperness}
Assume conformal screen properness on $K$ and optical defect conservation with horizon absorption accounting. Then, after removing the defect-supported and horizon-absorbed exceptional families, the regular screen map is proper over compact Bondi screen sets.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $Q\subset\mathscr I^+$ be compact and let $y_j\in(\Pi_{\varepsilon_j}^{\mathrm{reg}})^{-1}(Q)\cap K$. After passing to a subsequence, the screen points converge in $Q$. Definition~\ref{def:ConformalScreenProperness} gives three alternatives. In the first alternative the generators converge to a retained regular generator in $K$, which is the desired sequential compactness. In the second alternative the limiting current is supported by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$ and is removed from the regular part by optical defect conservation. In the third alternative the lost mass is supported on $\mathcal H^+$ and is removed from the regular screen domain by horizon absorption accounting. Thus every nonremoved sequence has a convergent regular subsequence. Since the generator topology on the compact exterior region is metrizable along the regularized construction, sequential compactness is compactness.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Screen-Sard regularity gives discrete a.e. fibers]\label{lem:ScreenSardDiscreteFibers}
Assume the regular screen map is proper over compact Bondi screen sets and satisfies the screen-Sard branch-null property on $K$. Then its a.e. regular fibers are discrete.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Discard the screen-null branch-value set in Definition~\ref{def:ScreenSardBranchNull}. For a remaining screen value $q$, every preimage point in the regular part is a full-rank approximate differentiability point of $\Pi_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{reg}}$. The inverse-function theorem on each smooth chart of the regularized optical construction, equivalently the metric inverse theorem for the approximately differentiable representative, gives a neighborhood on which the fiber over $q$ consists only of that point. Hence the fiber is locally finite. Properness makes the fiber over compact screen sets compact, so a locally finite fiber over $q$ is discrete and finite on $K$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Conformal properness route to finite screen degree]\label{thm:ConformalPropernessFiniteDegree}
On a compact exterior region $K$, assume optical defect conservation, horizon absorption accounting, conformal screen properness, the screen-Sard branch-null property, and uniform regular component degree. Then the optical construction has finite regular screen degree.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:ConformalPropernessScreenProperness} gives properness of the regular screen map after the defect-supported and horizon-absorbed families are removed. Lemma~\ref{lem:ScreenSardDiscreteFibers} gives a.e. discrete regular fibers. The uniform regular component degree supplies the component and degree bounds required in Definition~\ref{def:ProperRegularScreenMap}. Hence the regular screen map is proper with discrete regular fibers, and Lemma~\ref{lem:ProperScreenMapFiniteDegree} gives finite regular screen degree.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Proper finite-degree screen maps have finite regular degree]\label{lem:ProperScreenMapFiniteDegree}
If the regular screen map is proper with discrete regular fibers, then the optical construction has finite regular screen degree.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Properness makes every a.e. regular fiber compact inside the compact exterior generator region. The discreteness assumption then makes each such fiber finite. On each connected regular component, the number of preimages of a regular value is controlled by the absolute topological degree plus the finite number of orientation sheets. The assumed uniform degree bound $D_K$ and the finite number of retained regular components on the compact region therefore give a uniform integer $N_K$. This is exactly Definition~\ref{def:FiniteRegularScreenDegree}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Proper-screen route to no recurrent lensing]\label{cor:ProperScreenNoRecurrentLensing}
Optical defect conservation and a proper regular screen map with discrete regular fibers imply no recurrent exterior lensing.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:ProperScreenMapFiniteDegree} gives finite regular screen degree. Lemma~\ref{lem:DefectConservationNoRecurrentLensing} then gives no recurrent exterior lensing.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Defect conservation plus finite degree gives no recurrent lensing]\label{lem:DefectConservationNoRecurrentLensing}
Optical defect conservation and finite regular screen degree imply no recurrent exterior lensing.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Optical defect conservation removes all nonregular repetitions into $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$ or into an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ exceptional set. On the remaining regular current part, Definition~\ref{def:FiniteRegularScreenDegree} supplies the uniform a.e. counting bound $N_K$. This is exactly Definition~\ref{def:NoRecurrentExteriorLensing}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Defect-conservation route to optical scattering]\label{thm:DefectConservationOpticalScattering}
On a compact exterior region, averaged optical curvature-energy smallness, optical defect conservation, and finite regular screen degree imply uniform optical scattering regularity.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Averaged optical curvature-energy smallness gives exterior curvature-flux smallness by Lemma~\ref{lem:AveragedEnergyCurvatureFluxSmallness}. Lemma~\ref{lem:CurvatureFluxTidalSmallness} and Lemma~\ref{lem:TidalSmallnessPositiveScreenDet} give the positive screen Jacobi determinant. Optical defect conservation gives screen-caustic defect absorption by Lemma~\ref{lem:DefectConservationCausticAbsorption}; then Lemma~\ref{lem:DefectAbsorptionNoCaustic} gives the no-positive-area caustic clause. Finally, optical defect conservation plus finite regular screen degree gives no recurrent exterior lensing by Lemma~\ref{lem:DefectConservationNoRecurrentLensing}, and Lemma~\ref{lem:NoRecurrentLensingMultiplicity} gives bounded screen multiplicity. These are precisely the clauses of Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScatteringRegularity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Defect-conservation route to the Bondi screen estimate]\label{cor:DefectConservationBondiScreen}
Averaged optical curvature-energy smallness, optical defect conservation, and finite regular screen degree imply the Bondi screen-Jacobian lower bound.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Combine Theorem~\ref{thm:DefectConservationOpticalScattering} with Corollary~\ref{cor:ScatteringRouteBondiScreen}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Screen-caustic defect absorption]\label{def:ScreenCausticDefectAbsorption}
The optical construction satisfies \emph{screen-caustic defect absorption} on a compact exterior region if every sequence of retained generator families $E_\varepsilon$ whose Bondi screen determinant tends to zero on a set of positive limiting initial area has a subsequential limit supported by the singular-stratum defect measure of the weak null flow. Equivalently, after removing the part charged by that nonnegative defect measure, the determinant-zero set has limiting retained area zero.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[No recurrent exterior lensing]\label{def:NoRecurrentExteriorLensing}
The exterior screen relation has \emph{no recurrent lensing} on a compact exterior region if there is an integer $N_K$ such that every screen point $q\in\mathscr I^+$ has at most $N_K$ regular retained preimages whose null segments remain in the compact region $K$, except for a subset of screen measure zero and an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ exceptional family. Equivalently, the regular part of the screen relation has uniformly finite counting function
\[
    \#\{y\in K_\varepsilon\cap K:\Pi_\varepsilon(y)=q\}\le N_K
\]
for $\omega_{\mathscr I}$-a.e. $q$.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Defect absorption removes positive-area screen caustics]\label{lem:DefectAbsorptionNoCaustic}
Screen-caustic defect absorption implies the no-positive-area screen-caustic clause in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScatteringRegularity}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $Z_\varepsilon$ be the retained set on which the screen determinant vanishes, or tends to zero along the regularized sequence. If $Z_\varepsilon$ has positive limiting initial area and is not charged by the singular-stratum defect measure, then Definition~\ref{def:ScreenCausticDefectAbsorption} gives a contradiction. Therefore every positive-area determinant-zero concentration is absorbed by the defect measure; after removing the defect-charged part, the remaining determinant-zero set has limiting retained area zero. This is precisely the no-positive-area screen-caustic requirement.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[No recurrent lensing gives bounded screen multiplicity]\label{lem:NoRecurrentLensingMultiplicity}
No recurrent exterior lensing implies the bounded screen-multiplicity clause in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScatteringRegularity}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The bounded screen-multiplicity clause asks for a uniform a.e. bound on the number of retained generators in the compact exterior region that arrive at the same Bondi screen point, up to an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ exceptional family. This is exactly the finite counting-function bound in Definition~\ref{def:NoRecurrentExteriorLensing}, with $N=N_K$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Curvature-flux and no-recurrence route to optical scattering]\label{thm:CurvatureFluxNoRecurrenceScattering}
On a compact exterior region, exterior curvature-flux smallness, screen-caustic defect absorption, and no recurrent exterior lensing imply uniform optical scattering regularity.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Exterior curvature-flux smallness gives exterior optical tidal smallness by Lemma~\ref{lem:CurvatureFluxTidalSmallness}. Lemma~\ref{lem:TidalSmallnessPositiveScreenDet} then gives the positive screen Jacobi determinant bound. The no-positive-area caustic clause follows from Lemma~\ref{lem:DefectAbsorptionNoCaustic}, and the bounded multiplicity clause follows from Lemma~\ref{lem:NoRecurrentLensingMultiplicity}. These are exactly the three requirements in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScatteringRegularity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Curvature-flux route to the Bondi screen estimate]\label{cor:CurvatureFluxBondiScreenEstimate}
Exterior curvature-flux smallness, screen-caustic defect absorption, and no recurrent exterior lensing imply the Bondi screen-Jacobian lower bound.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Combine Theorem~\ref{thm:CurvatureFluxNoRecurrenceScattering} with Corollary~\ref{cor:ScatteringRouteBondiScreen}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Tidal route to optical scattering regularity]\label{cor:TidalRouteScatteringRegularity}
Exterior optical tidal smallness, no positive-area screen caustic, and bounded screen multiplicity imply uniform optical scattering regularity.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:TidalSmallnessPositiveScreenDet} gives the screen Jacobi determinant bound. The remaining two assumptions are precisely the other two clauses in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScatteringRegularity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Optical scattering regularity implies screen transversality]\label{lem:ScatteringRegularityScreenTransversality}
Uniform optical scattering regularity implies uniform screen transversality in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:UniformScreenTransversality}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The screen map is obtained by solving the null geodesic boundary-value problem from the retained optical hypersurface to $\mathscr I^+$. On the nonexceptional set, smooth dependence of null geodesics on initial data gives approximate differentiability of $\Pi_\varepsilon$, and its metric Jacobian is precisely the Bondi screen determinant of the propagated Jacobi fields:
\[
    J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_\varepsilon=\det_{\mathscr I}\mathcal J_\varepsilon .
\]
The screen Jacobi determinant bound therefore gives $J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_\varepsilon\ge j_0$ a.e. after removing an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ area set. The no-screen-caustic clause ensures that the removed determinant-zero set carries no positive retained area except through the already-counted singular defect. The bounded screen multiplicity clause supplies the multiplicity bound $N$. These are exactly the three requirements in Definition~\ref{def:UniformScreenTransversality}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Scattering route to the Bondi screen estimate]\label{cor:ScatteringRouteBondiScreen}
Uniform optical scattering regularity implies the Bondi screen-Jacobian lower bound.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Combine Lemma~\ref{lem:ScatteringRegularityScreenTransversality} with Lemma~\ref{lem:ScreenTransversalityJacobianBound}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Screen transversality gives the Bondi screen-Jacobian bound]\label{lem:ScreenTransversalityJacobianBound}
Uniform screen transversality implies the Bondi screen-Jacobian lower bound of Definition~\ref{def:BondiScreenJacobianLowerBound}, with $\kappa=j_0/N$ on each compact exterior region.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Apply the area formula for approximately differentiable maps from the retained generator parameter surface to the Bondi cut of $\mathscr I^+$. For every positive-area retained family $E_\varepsilon$,
\[
    \int_{E_\varepsilon}J_{\mathscr I}\Pi_\varepsilon\,dA_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}
    =
    \int_{\Pi_\varepsilon(E_\varepsilon)}
    \#(E_\varepsilon\cap\Pi_\varepsilon^{-1}(q))\,d\omega_{\mathscr I}(q)
    +o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
The lower Jacobian bound gives the left side at least $j_0A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}(E_\varepsilon)-o_\varepsilon(1)$, while the multiplicity bound gives the right side at most
\[
    N\,\omega_{\mathscr I}(\Pi_\varepsilon(E_\varepsilon))+o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
Dividing by $N$ yields Definition~\ref{def:BondiScreenJacobianLowerBound}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Screen-Jacobian lower bound implies shadow nondegeneracy]\label{lem:ScreenJacobianShadowNondegeneracy}
If the Bondi screen-Jacobian lower bound of Definition~\ref{def:BondiScreenJacobianLowerBound} holds on every compact exterior region reached by positive-area retained families, then the optical construction is shadow-nondegenerate in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:CausalShadowIndex}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For every such family $E_\varepsilon$, the screen image $\Pi_\varepsilon(E_\varepsilon)$ is contained in the future screen $\mathfrak S(E_\varepsilon)$. Therefore
\[
    \omega_{\mathscr I}(\mathfrak S(E_\varepsilon))
    \ge
    \omega_{\mathscr I}(\Pi_\varepsilon(E_\varepsilon))
    \ge
    \kappa A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}(E_\varepsilon)-o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
Dividing by $A_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}(E_\varepsilon)$ and taking the lower limit gives $\mathfrak{sh}(E)\ge\kappa>0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Conformal causal closed-graph property]\label{def:ConformalCausalClosedGraph}
The conformal completion satisfies the \emph{causal closed-graph property} for the optical construction if the following implication holds: whenever compact generator-current families $\Gamma(E_j)$ converge to a limiting optical segment with terminal point $p$, and points $q_j\in \mathfrak S(E_j)\subset\mathscr I^+$ converge in the conformal boundary to $q\in\mathscr I^+$, then $p\in J^-(q)$ unless the limiting generator family is entirely absorbed by the singular-stratum defect measure.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Causal simplicity gives the conformal closed graph]\label{lem:CausalSimplicityClosedGraph}
Assume the conformal completion is causally simple on the compact exterior region swept out by the retained families, so the relation
\[
    \{(p,q):q\in J^+(p)\}
\]
is closed in the conformal completion. Assume also that generator-current convergence identifies every nonabsorbed terminal limit $p$ as the limit of points $p_j\in\Gamma(E_j)$ with $q_j\in J^+(p_j)\cap\mathscr I^+$. Then the conformal causal closed-graph property of Definition~\ref{def:ConformalCausalClosedGraph} holds.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $q_j\in\mathfrak S(E_j)$ converge to $q\in\mathscr I^+$, and suppose the limiting generator family is not entirely absorbed by the singular-stratum defect measure. By the compatibility of generator-current convergence with terminal limits, choose $p_j\in\Gamma(E_j)$ with $p_j\to p$ and $q_j\in J^+(p_j)$. Since the conformal causal relation is closed, the limit satisfies $q\in J^+(p)$. Equivalently, $p\in J^-(q)$, which is exactly Definition~\ref{def:ConformalCausalClosedGraph}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Closed graph implies shadow semicontinuity]\label{lem:ClosedGraphShadowSemicontinuity}
Assume the conformal causal closed-graph property of Definition~\ref{def:ConformalCausalClosedGraph}, and assume that positive lower Bondi screen measure prevents total absorption by the singular-stratum defect measure. Then the optical construction satisfies shadow semicontinuity.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $E_j$ be as in Definition~\ref{def:ShadowSemicontinuity} and suppose
\[
    \liminf_{j\to\infty}\omega_{\mathscr I}(\mathfrak S(E_j))>0 .
\]
After passing to a subsequence, choose density points $q_j\in\mathfrak S(E_j)$ converging to some $q\in\mathscr I^+$ in the conformal boundary. The positive screen-measure assumption rules out complete singular-defect absorption. The closed-graph property therefore gives $p\in J^-(q)\subset J^-(\mathscr I^+)$. Since the $E_j$ are retained exterior generator families until their terminal collapse, the same convergence places $p$ in the closure of the domain of outer communications. This is shadow semicontinuity.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Screen-map criterion for the shadow route]\label{thm:ScreenMapShadowRoute}
Assume the Bondi screen-Jacobian lower bound, the conformal causal closed-graph property, and the positive-screen nonabsorption condition in Lemma~\ref{lem:ClosedGraphShadowSemicontinuity}. Then the optical construction is shadow-nondegenerate and satisfies shadow semicontinuity. Consequently, if the horizon-crossing alternative in Theorem~\ref{thm:ShadowIndexOuterVisibility} holds, then the outer-visible focal-collapse alternative follows.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Shadow nondegeneracy follows from Lemma~\ref{lem:ScreenJacobianShadowNondegeneracy}. Shadow semicontinuity follows from Lemma~\ref{lem:ClosedGraphShadowSemicontinuity}. The final implication is Theorem~\ref{thm:ShadowIndexOuterVisibility}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Shadow index criterion for outer-visible focal collapse]\label{thm:ShadowIndexOuterVisibility}
Assume the admissible regularized optical construction is shadow-nondegenerate and satisfies shadow semicontinuity. Suppose, moreover, that positive-area endpoint collapse cannot occur through the event horizon without either entering the singular-stratum defect measure or losing all future screen measure. Then the outer-visible focal-collapse alternative of Definition~\ref{def:OuterVisibleFocalCollapse} holds.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $E_j$ be a positive-area family of retained generators whose first conjugate point, first cut point, or first zero of area density approaches $[\lambda_0,\lambda_1]$. If its limiting endpoint collapse is absorbed by the singular-stratum defect measure, it is not part of the positive-area retained alternative. Otherwise the family has positive limiting regular area. Shadow nondegeneracy gives a uniform positive lower bound for the normalized future screen measure of $E_j$ as long as the limiting endpoint remains in the domain of outer communications.

If the endpoint attempted to hide behind the event horizon while retaining positive regular area, the assumed horizon-crossing alternative forces either singular-defect absorption or loss of all future screen measure, both contrary to the retained positive-area case under shadow nondegeneracy. Thus a subsequential terminal limit remains in the exterior causal region. Shadow semicontinuity then places the terminal limit in $J^-(\mathscr I^+)$ and in the closure of the domain of outer communications. Since the endpoint is a focal, cut, or zero-area terminal endpoint reached before horizon absorption, it lies in the domain of outer communications itself. This is exactly Definition~\ref{def:OuterVisibleFocalCollapse}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Absorption-accounting route to outer visibility]\label{cor:HorizonAccountingOuterVisibility}
Assume the admissible regularized optical construction is shadow-nondegenerate, satisfies shadow semicontinuity, and has horizon absorption accounting in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:HorizonAbsorptionAccounting}. Assume also the conformal closed-graph property. Then the outer-visible focal-collapse alternative holds.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:HorizonAccountingDichotomy}, horizon absorption accounting and the conformal closed-graph property imply the horizon screen-flux dichotomy. By Lemma~\ref{lem:HorizonDichotomyNonabsorption}, this dichotomy supplies the horizon-crossing nonabsorption alternative required in Theorem~\ref{thm:ShadowIndexOuterVisibility}. Applying that theorem gives Definition~\ref{def:OuterVisibleFocalCollapse}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Shadow route to the causal-measure package]\label{cor:ShadowRouteCausalMeasurePackage}
Assume Radon compactness, no boundary mass loss, nonnegative singular concentration on caustic and cut-locus strata, strong WCC exclusion for null optical collapse, and the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:ShadowIndexOuterVisibility}. Then the causal-measure shielding package holds.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:ShadowIndexOuterVisibility} gives the outer-visible focal-collapse alternative. Theorem~\ref{thm:VisibilityWCCCausalMeasurePackage} then gives the causal-measure shielding package.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Radon defect accounting for zero-area collapse]\label{lem:RadonDefectAccounting}
Assume the regularized optical currents and Raychaudhuri measures converge as Radon measures on compact null slabs, have no boundary mass loss at $\Sigma_0$ or $\mathcal H_M$, and have nonnegative singular concentration supported on caustic and cut-locus strata. Then every collapsing family of retained generators with zero limiting area density contributes only to the singular-stratum defect measure and leaves no residual absolutely continuous area loss.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $\mu_\varepsilon$ denote the spacetime area measure of the regularized optical slab, pulled back to the generator parameter space. After passing to a subsequence, $\mu_\varepsilon\rightharpoonup\mu$ as Radon measures. Decompose the limit with respect to the regular part of the weak null hypersurface,
\[
    \mu=\mu_{\mathrm{reg}}+\mu_{\mathrm{sing}},
\]
where $\mu_{\mathrm{sing}}$ is supported on the caustic and cut-locus strata by the assumed concentration support. A generator family with zero limiting area density has vanishing density with respect to $\mu_{\mathrm{reg}}$ by definition of the regular area density. Hence its limiting contribution is singular with respect to the regular slab measure. No-boundary-mass-loss excludes escape through the initial or terminal cuts, so the only possible remaining measure is $\mu_{\mathrm{sing}}$. Since the singular concentration is nonnegative, this contribution is exactly recorded by the singular-stratum defect measure, with no leftover absolutely continuous part.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Visibility and WCC give the causal-measure package]\label{thm:VisibilityWCCCausalMeasurePackage}
Assume an admissible regularized optical construction has Radon compactness, no boundary mass loss, and nonnegative singular concentration supported on caustic and cut-locus strata. If the outer-visible focal-collapse alternative of Definition~\ref{def:OuterVisibleFocalCollapse} holds and WCC holds in the strong null-collapse exclusion form of Definition~\ref{def:StrongWCCNullCollapseExclusion}, then the construction satisfies the causal-measure shielding package of Definition~\ref{def:CausalMeasureShieldingPackage}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The exterior visibility alternative in Definition~\ref{def:CausalMeasureShieldingPackage} is exactly Definition~\ref{def:OuterVisibleFocalCollapse}. The censorship exclusion clause is exactly Definition~\ref{def:StrongWCCNullCollapseExclusion}. The defect-accounting clause follows from Lemma~\ref{lem:RadonDefectAccounting}. These three clauses are the causal-measure shielding package.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[DOC-completeness route to the causal-measure package]\label{cor:DOCCompletenessCausalMeasurePackage}
Assume an admissible regularized optical construction has Radon compactness, no boundary mass loss, nonnegative singular concentration supported on caustic and cut-locus strata, and DOC optical completeness. If the outer-visible focal-collapse alternative holds, then the construction satisfies the causal-measure shielding package.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
DOC optical completeness gives the strong null-collapse exclusion form by Corollary~\ref{cor:DOCCompletenessStrongWCC}. The result follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:VisibilityWCCCausalMeasurePackage}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Causal-measure shielding yields focal shielding]\label{thm:CausalMeasureShieldingFocalShielding}
Assume an admissible regularized optical construction satisfies the causal-measure shielding package of Definition~\ref{def:CausalMeasureShieldingPackage}, and assume endpoint compactness for retained generators on every nonsingular collar of $[\lambda_0,\lambda_1]$. Then the construction satisfies cosmic-censorship focal shielding in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:CosmicCensorshipFocalShielding}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let a sequence of retained generators develop first conjugate point, first cut point, or first zero of area density at affine distance tending to zero from $[\lambda_0,\lambda_1]$. Endpoint compactness gives a subsequential limiting null geodesic segment from $\Sigma_0$, except possibly through loss of zero limiting area density.

On the positive-area part, the exterior visibility alternative places the limiting terminal singular endpoint in the domain of outer communications and in $J^-(\mathscr I^+)$. The censorship exclusion rules out precisely such a positive-area visible endpoint. Hence any remaining collapsed family cannot carry positive limiting area outside the singular set. On the zero-area part, defect accounting records the collapsed contribution in the nonnegative singular-stratum defect measure and leaves no hidden absolutely continuous loss. These three conclusions are exactly the no-visible-focal-endpoint, no-positive-area-hidden-escape, and endpoint-compactness clauses of Definition~\ref{def:CosmicCensorshipFocalShielding}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Focal shielding implies a uniform focal-radius barrier]\label{thm:FocalShieldingUniformBarrier}
Assume NEC, weak cosmic censorship, an admissible regularized optical construction, and cosmic-censorship focal shielding in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:CosmicCensorshipFocalShielding}. After discarding only generators whose limiting area density is zero and recording that discarded part in the singular-stratum defect measure, the remaining retained generators satisfy the uniform focal-radius barrier of Definition~\ref{def:UniformFocalRadiusBarrier}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Suppose that no uniform focal-radius barrier exists. Then for every $j$ one can find $\varepsilon_j\downarrow0$ and a retained generator $y_j\in K_{\varepsilon_j}$ whose smooth extension breaks down within affine distance $1/j$ of $[\lambda_0,\lambda_1]$, either by a conjugate point, a cut point, or loss of positive area density. By endpoint compactness, after passing to a subsequence the corresponding null segments converge locally, away from the possible endpoint, to a limiting null geodesic segment issued from $\Sigma_0$.

If the limiting area density along this sequence tends to zero, the generator belongs to the zero-area discard set allowed in the statement, and its contribution is absorbed by the singular-stratum defect measure. We therefore restrict to the complementary set, where the limiting area density is positive. On this set the no-visible-focal-endpoint clause says that the limiting focal or cut endpoint would be visible from future null infinity and outside the black-hole region. This is precisely the naked singular endpoint excluded by weak cosmic censorship for the admissible development. Hence no such positive-area sequence can exist.

It remains to rule out unbounded endpoint expansions on the enlarged collar. If the endpoint expansions were unbounded along a positive-area retained sequence, the Raychaudhuri inequality under NEC would force, by the scalar Riccati comparison, a conjugate point or zero of area density at arbitrarily small affine distance from the endpoint. This reduces the failure to the case just excluded. Endpoint compactness handles the residual zero-area alternatives by placing them in the discarded singular-stratum measure. Thus there are constants $\rho>0$ and $B<\infty$, independent of $\varepsilon$, on the positive-area retained set, giving Definition~\ref{def:UniformFocalRadiusBarrier}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Riccati barrier for the null expansion]\label{lem:RiccatiFocalBarrierExpansion}
Assume NEC and the uniform focal-radius barrier of Definition~\ref{def:UniformFocalRadiusBarrier}. Then
\[
    |\theta_\varepsilon(\lambda,y)|\le C_\theta
\]
on $[\lambda_0,\lambda_1]$, with $C_\theta$ depending only on $B$, $\rho$, and $\lambda_1-\lambda_0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Along each smooth generator, Raychaudhuri gives
\[
    \partial_\lambda\theta_\varepsilon
    =
    -\frac12\theta_\varepsilon^2
    -|\sigma_\varepsilon|^2
    -R_{\mu\nu}L_\varepsilon^\mu L_\varepsilon^\nu
    \le
    -\frac12\theta_\varepsilon^2 .
\]
The scalar Riccati comparison for $z'=-z^2/2$ shows that if $\theta_\varepsilon$ becomes too negative at an interior point, it must blow down to $-\infty$ within affine time at most $2/|\theta_\varepsilon|$, producing a focal point before $\lambda_1+\rho$. Since no such focal point occurs in the enlarged collar, the negative part is bounded by $2/\rho$ up to the endpoint allowance $B$.

Applying the same comparison to the past extension controls the positive part: if $\theta_\varepsilon$ were too large at an interior point, the backward Riccati comparison would force a focal point before $\lambda_0-\rho$, contradicting the past collar. Combining the two one-sided bounds gives the stated $C_\theta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Focal-radius barrier implies optical-collar regularity]\label{thm:FocalBarrierOpticalCollar}
Assume NEC, the uniform focal-radius barrier, and a uniform positive lower and upper bound for the initial area density on the retained generator set. Then the regularized optical foliation has uniform optical-collar regularity in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:UniformOpticalCollarRegularity}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:RiccatiFocalBarrierExpansion} supplies the uniform expansion bound. Since
\[
    \partial_\lambda\log a_\varepsilon=\theta_\varepsilon ,
\]
integration gives
\[
    e^{-C_\theta(\lambda_1-\lambda_0)}
    \le
    \frac{a_\varepsilon(\lambda,y)}{a_\varepsilon(\lambda_0,y)}
    \le
    e^{C_\theta(\lambda_1-\lambda_0)} .
\]
Combining this with the assumed initial area-density bounds gives the two-sided Jacobian estimate \eqref{eq:OpticalCollarAreaBounds}. Together with the expansion bound, this is exactly Definition~\ref{def:UniformOpticalCollarRegularity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Optical-collar regularity implies entropy-admissibility]\label{thm:OpticalCollarEntropyAdmissibility}
If a regularized optical foliation has uniform optical-collar regularity in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:UniformOpticalCollarRegularity}, and the initial cross-sections have uniformly bounded area, then it is entropy-admissible. Consequently the bounded null log-area entropy criterion, the expansion-square bound, and Raychaudhuri-measure compactness all hold.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The Jacobian distortion condition in Definition~\ref{def:EntropyAdmissibleOpticalFoliation} is exactly \eqref{eq:OpticalCollarAreaBounds}. Since
\[
    \ell_\varepsilon(\lambda_1,y)-\ell_\varepsilon(\lambda_0,y)
    =
    \int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda_1}\theta_\varepsilon(\lambda,y)\,d\lambda ,
\]
the expansion bound \eqref{eq:OpticalCollarExpansionBound} gives
\[
    |\ell_\varepsilon(\lambda_1,y)-\ell_\varepsilon(\lambda_0,y)|^2
    \le
    C_\theta^2(\lambda_1-\lambda_0)^2 .
\]
Integrating over $\mathcal H_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon}$ and using the uniform area bound gives log-area oscillation control.

For coercivity, use again \eqref{eq:OpticalCollarExpansionBound} and the upper area-density bound in \eqref{eq:OpticalCollarAreaBounds}:
\[
    \int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda_1}
    |\partial_\lambda\ell_\varepsilon|^2a_\varepsilon\,d\lambda
    =
    \int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda_1}
    \theta_\varepsilon^2a_\varepsilon\,d\lambda
    \le
    C_\theta^2 C_a(\lambda_1-\lambda_0).
\]
This implies \eqref{eq:RaychaudhuriCoercivityEntropy} after increasing the constant $C_E$. Hence the foliation is entropy-admissible. The remaining conclusions follow from Theorem~\ref{thm:BoundedNullLogAreaEntropyCriterion}, Proposition~\ref{prop:ExpansionSquareFromEntropy}, and Corollary~\ref{cor:EntropyRouteRaychaudhuriCompactness}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Bounded null log-area entropy criterion]\label{thm:BoundedNullLogAreaEntropyCriterion}
If a regularized optical foliation is entropy-admissible in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:EntropyAdmissibleOpticalFoliation}, then
\[
    \mathcal E_{\log}(\varepsilon)\le C_{\log}
\]
uniformly in $\varepsilon$. Consequently, the expansion-square bound of Proposition~\ref{prop:ExpansionSquareFromEntropy} holds.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Integrating the coercivity estimate \eqref{eq:RaychaudhuriCoercivityEntropy} over the initial cross-section and using the log-area oscillation bound gives
\[
    \mathcal E_{\log}(\varepsilon)
    \le
    C_E\left(
        A(\mathcal H_{\lambda_0,\varepsilon})
        +C_{\mathrm{osc}}
    \right).
\]
The endpoint area is uniformly controlled in the admissible construction. This proves bounded null log-area entropy. The final sentence is Proposition~\ref{prop:ExpansionSquareFromEntropy}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Entropy route to Raychaudhuri compactness]\label{cor:EntropyRouteRaychaudhuriCompactness}
Endpoint expansion control, entropy-admissibility, and the null energy condition imply the Raychaudhuri-measure compactness conclusion of Corollary~\ref{cor:RaychaudhuriMeasureCompactness}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:BoundedNullLogAreaEntropyCriterion} and Proposition~\ref{prop:ExpansionSquareFromEntropy} give the expansion-square bound. Lemma~\ref{lem:EndpointExpansionControl} gives endpoint expansion control. Proposition~\ref{prop:UniformRaychaudhuriMeasureBound} then gives uniform measure bounds, and Corollary~\ref{cor:RaychaudhuriMeasureCompactness} gives compactness.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Raychaudhuri compactness from endpoint and entropy bounds]\label{cor:RaychaudhuriFromEntropy}
Endpoint expansion control from Lemma~\ref{lem:EndpointExpansionControl}, bounded null log-area entropy, and uniform generator Jacobian distortion imply the uniform Raychaudhuri-measure bound of Proposition~\ref{prop:UniformRaychaudhuriMeasureBound}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:EndpointExpansionControl} supplies $C_0$, while Proposition~\ref{prop:ExpansionSquareFromEntropy} supplies $C_1$. The conclusion is exactly Proposition~\ref{prop:UniformRaychaudhuriMeasureBound}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[First compactness consequence]\label{cor:RaychaudhuriMeasureCompactness}
Under the hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{prop:UniformRaychaudhuriMeasureBound}, after passing to a subsequence the Raychaudhuri measures
\[
    \left(
        \frac12\theta_\varepsilon^2
        +|\sigma_\varepsilon|^2
        +R_{\mu\nu}L_\varepsilon^\mu L_\varepsilon^\nu
    \right)d\lambda\,dA_\varepsilon
\]
converge weakly as Radon measures on compact null slabs. Any failure of smooth convergence is recorded by a nonnegative defect measure.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The uniform total-variation bound and positivity give weak compactness of Radon measures by Banach--Alaoglu. Lower semicontinuity of nonnegative measures identifies the difference between the weak limit and the absolutely continuous smooth-flow limit as a nonnegative defect measure.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Null-horizon calibration criterion]\label{thm:NullHorizonCalibration}
Let $(N^{3+1},\bar g)$ satisfy the null energy condition and weak cosmic censorship, and let $\Sigma_0$ be a closed trapped surface. If there exists a weak outgoing null hypersurface $\mathcal N$ from $\Sigma_0$ to $\mathcal H_M$ together with a null-horizon calibration $w$ in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:NullHorizonCalibration}, then
\[
    A(\Sigma_0)\le A(\mathcal H_M).
\]
Consequently, under the Kerr final-state bound and Bondi mass loss, one obtains
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The weighted transport inequality \eqref{eq:NullCalibrationTransport} is the exact Lorentzian analogue of the Green-trace calibration used in Section~\ref{sec:GreenTraceCalibration}: it packages the Raychaudhuri loss term, the shear term, the Ricci focusing term, and the weak-flow cut defect into a single nonnegative density. Integrating \eqref{eq:NullCalibrationTransport} from $\Sigma_0$ to $\mathcal H_M$, using the endpoint normalization $w|_{\mathcal H_M}=1$ and the trapped-surface inequality $\theta|_{\Sigma_0}\le0$, yields the area comparison. The mass bound then follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:Penrose1973Complete}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lorentzian adjoint-calibration criterion]\label{thm:NullGreenCalibration}
Let $(N^{3+1},\bar g)$ satisfy the null energy condition and weak cosmic censorship. Suppose every weak outgoing null hypersurface from a closed trapped surface $\Sigma_0$ to $\mathcal H_M$ admits a null Green weight $W$ in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:NullGreenWeight}. Then
\[
    A(\Sigma_0)\le A(\mathcal H_M),
\]
and consequently
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The adjoint equation \eqref{eq:NullGreenAdjoint} is the null-geometry analogue of the Green-trace calibration: the focusing density is not forced to be pointwise nonnegative, but its defect is absorbed into the nonnegative source $\mathfrak s_{\mathcal N}$ on the singular strata of the weak flow. This gives monotonicity of $\mathcal F(\lambda)$ toward the past, hence the horizon comparison. The mass bound then follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:Penrose1973Complete}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Screen-map closure of the Penrose 1973 route]\label{thm:ScreenMapPenroseRoute}
Let $(N^{3+1},\bar g)$ be a globally hyperbolic asymptotically flat spacetime satisfying NEC and WCC, with Kerr final state and Bondi mass loss. Assume that for every closed trapped surface $\Sigma_0$ the admissible regularized optical construction exists and satisfies:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item Radon compactness, no boundary mass loss, and nonnegative singular concentration on caustic and cut-locus strata;
    \item either strong WCC exclusion for null optical collapse or DOC optical completeness in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:DOCOpticalCompleteness};
    \item the Bondi screen-Jacobian lower bound and conformal causal closed-graph property of Theorem~\ref{thm:ScreenMapShadowRoute};
    \item the positive-screen nonabsorption alternative in Theorem~\ref{thm:ScreenMapShadowRoute} and either the horizon-crossing alternative in Theorem~\ref{thm:ShadowIndexOuterVisibility} or horizon absorption accounting in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:HorizonAbsorptionAccounting};
    \item endpoint compactness and the endpoint area and expansion controls required in Lemma~\ref{lem:EndpointExpansionControl}.
\end{enumerate}
Then every closed trapped surface satisfies
\[
    A(\Sigma_0)\le A(\mathcal H_M),
\]
and therefore
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The screen-map hypotheses imply shadow nondegeneracy and shadow semicontinuity by Theorem~\ref{thm:ScreenMapShadowRoute}. If the horizon-crossing alternative is assumed directly, Theorem~\ref{thm:ShadowIndexOuterVisibility} gives the outer-visible focal-collapse alternative. If instead horizon absorption accounting is assumed, Corollary~\ref{cor:HorizonAccountingOuterVisibility} gives the same conclusion. If strong WCC exclusion is assumed directly, Lemma~\ref{lem:RadonDefectAccounting} and Theorem~\ref{thm:VisibilityWCCCausalMeasurePackage} give the causal-measure shielding package. If DOC optical completeness is assumed instead, Corollary~\ref{cor:DOCCompletenessCausalMeasurePackage} gives the same package. Theorem~\ref{thm:CausalMeasureShieldingFocalShielding} converts this into cosmic-censorship focal shielding, and Theorem~\ref{thm:FocalShieldingUniformBarrier} gives the uniform focal-radius barrier.

The focal-radius barrier implies optical-collar regularity by Theorem~\ref{thm:FocalBarrierOpticalCollar}. Hence Theorem~\ref{thm:OpticalCollarEntropyAdmissibility}, Theorem~\ref{thm:BoundedNullLogAreaEntropyCriterion}, Proposition~\ref{prop:ExpansionSquareFromEntropy}, and Proposition~\ref{prop:UniformRaychaudhuriMeasureBound} give the uniform Raychaudhuri-measure compactness required in Definition~\ref{def:RegularizedNullGreenConstruction}. The regularized null Green breakthrough criterion, Theorem~\ref{thm:RegularizedNullGreenBreakthrough}, therefore supplies a null Green weight. Finally Theorem~\ref{thm:NullGreenCalibration} gives $A(\Sigma_0)\le A(\mathcal H_M)$, and the Hawking area theorem, the Kerr final-state bound, and Bondi mass loss give the stated Penrose inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Six-gate working path to the Penrose 1973 conjecture]\label{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}
Let $(N^{3+1},\bar g)$ be a globally hyperbolic asymptotically flat spacetime satisfying NEC and weak cosmic censorship, with Kerr final state and Bondi mass loss. Assume that every closed trapped surface $\Sigma_0$ admits an admissible regularized optical construction with Radon compactness, no boundary mass loss, nonnegative singular concentration, the endpoint compactness and endpoint controls used in Lemma~\ref{lem:EndpointExpansionControl}, and the conformal causal closed-graph property of Definition~\ref{def:ConformalCausalClosedGraph}. Assume also the positive-screen nonabsorption condition in Lemma~\ref{lem:ClosedGraphShadowSemicontinuity}. If, on every compact exterior region reached by a positive-area retained family, the following six gates hold:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the renormalized Bel--Robinson divergence package of Definition~\ref{def:RenormalizedBelRobinsonDivergencePackage};
    \item the renormalized optical BV package of Definition~\ref{def:RenormalizedOpticalBVPackage};
    \item the retarded-time coercive optical eikonal of Definition~\ref{def:RetardedTimeCoerciveOpticalEikonal};
    \item the optical screen ellipticity package of Definition~\ref{def:OpticalScreenEllipticityPackage};
    \item neck quantization for screen sheets in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:NeckQuantizationScreenSheets};
    \item the redshift-weighted DOC continuation package of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftWeightedDOCContinuation},
\end{enumerate}
then the full Penrose 1973 conclusion holds for every closed trapped surface:
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The renormalized Bel--Robinson divergence package gives the Bel--Robinson optical flux package by Theorem~\ref{thm:DivergenceBelRobinsonOpticalFlux}. Lemma~\ref{lem:BelRobinsonFluxAveragedEnergy} then gives averaged optical curvature-energy smallness.

Together with the renormalized optical BV package, this averaged curvature control gives optical current compactness by Theorem~\ref{thm:RenormalizedBVOpticalCurrentCompactness}; Theorem~\ref{thm:CompactnessPackageDefectConservation} gives optical defect conservation. Lemma~\ref{lem:DefectConservationHorizonAccounting} gives horizon absorption accounting. The redshift-weighted DOC continuation gate, together with optical defect conservation, gives DOC optical completeness by Theorem~\ref{thm:RedshiftDOCOpticalCompleteness}; hence Corollary~\ref{cor:DOCCompletenessStrongWCC} gives the strong null-collapse exclusion form needed in the screen-map closure.

The retarded-time coercive optical eikonal gives conformal screen properness by Theorem~\ref{thm:RetardedTimeConformalProperness}. The ellipticity gate gives the screen-Sard branch-null property by Theorem~\ref{thm:EllipticBranchNullRegularity}. The neck-quantization gate, together with renormalized BV and optical defect conservation, gives uniform regular component degree by Theorem~\ref{thm:NeckQuantizationUniformDegree}. Therefore Theorem~\ref{thm:ConformalPropernessFiniteDegree} gives finite regular screen degree.

Averaged optical curvature-energy smallness, optical defect conservation, and finite regular screen degree give uniform optical scattering regularity by Theorem~\ref{thm:DefectConservationOpticalScattering}, and hence the Bondi screen-Jacobian lower bound by Corollary~\ref{cor:ScatteringRouteBondiScreen}. The assumed conformal closed graph and positive-screen nonabsorption condition then activate Theorem~\ref{thm:ScreenMapShadowRoute}. Horizon absorption accounting activates Corollary~\ref{cor:HorizonAccountingOuterVisibility}. All hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:ScreenMapPenroseRoute} are now verified, so the area comparison $A(\Sigma_0)\le A(\mathcal H_M)$ and the stated Penrose inequality follow.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Optical certificate atlas]\label{def:OpticalCertificateAtlas}
An admissible regularized optical construction carries an \emph{optical certificate atlas} if every compact exterior region reached by a positive-area retained family is covered, up to an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ initial-area exceptional family and the named defect/horizon currents, by finitely many optical charts on which the following six certificates hold with constants uniform under refinement:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the Bel--Robinson multiplier certificate of Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCertificate};
    \item the optical BV transport certificate of Definition~\ref{def:OpticalBVTransportCertificate};
    \item the Bondi eikonal transport certificate of Definition~\ref{def:BondiEikonalTransportCertificate};
    \item the screen Caccioppoli--Campanato certificate of Definition~\ref{def:ScreenCaccioppoliCampanatoCertificate};
    \item the screen neck lower-bound certificate of Definition~\ref{def:ScreenNeckLowerBoundCertificate};
    \item the redshift ODE continuation certificate of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftODEContinuationCertificate}.
\end{enumerate}
The atlas is \emph{compatible} if the BV traces, Bel--Robinson boundary currents, screen maps, redshift weights, and defect currents agree on chart overlaps up to nonnegative overlap defects already counted by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$ or the horizon absorption current.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Master optical calibration]\label{def:MasterOpticalCalibration}
On a compact exterior region $K$, a \emph{master optical calibration} is a finite optical chart cover $\{U_i\}_{i=1}^N$ of the retained generator family, together with one calibrated datum
\[
    \mathfrak C_\varepsilon
    =
    \left(
        T_\varepsilon,N_\varepsilon,\rho_\varepsilon,
        \varpi_\varepsilon,\mathfrak B_\varepsilon,
        A_\varepsilon,F_\varepsilon,G_\varepsilon,
        \mathfrak r_\varepsilon,
        \mathfrak n_\varepsilon,
        \Omega_\varepsilon
    \right),
\]
where $\rho_\varepsilon=r_\varepsilon^p\chi_\varepsilon$ and $\Omega_\varepsilon$ is a nonnegative overlap current, satisfying the following six local calibration laws with constants depending only on $K$:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Bel--Robinson calibration.} The current
    \[
        \rho_\varepsilon
        Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_\varepsilon,N_\varepsilon,N_\varepsilon)
    \]
    has coercive optical divergence, renormalized Bondi/horizon/artificial-cap boundary sign, and lower-order absorption in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCertificate}.
    \item \emph{BV calibration.} The renormalized log-Jacobian, the screen map, the distributional screen Jacobian, and their boundary traces obey the transport identities and positivity of the limiting Jacobian defect required in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalBVTransportCertificate}.
    \item \emph{Eikonal calibration.} The lower semicontinuous exhaustion $\mathfrak B_\varepsilon$ and the transport remainder $\mathfrak r_\varepsilon$ satisfy the Bondi eikonal transport inequalities of Definition~\ref{def:BondiEikonalTransportCertificate}.
    \item \emph{Screen elliptic calibration.} The regular screen map solves
    \[
        \operatorname{div}_y(A_\varepsilon D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}})
        =
        \operatorname{div}_y F_\varepsilon+G_\varepsilon
    \]
    on non-defect-supported generator balls, with uniform ellipticity, Campanato improvement, reverse H\"older control, and singular-Jacobian absorption as in Definition~\ref{def:ScreenCaccioppoliCampanatoCertificate}.
    \item \emph{Neck calibration.} The nonnegative neck density $\mathfrak n_\varepsilon$ dominates
    \[
        |D\Pi_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{reg}}|
        +
        |D^s\Pi_\varepsilon^{\wedge2}|
        +
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}},
    \]
    and every nontrivial topological event carries $\mathfrak n_\varepsilon$-mass at least $\hbar_K>0$ on an annular generator neighborhood, as in Definition~\ref{def:ScreenNeckLowerBoundCertificate}.
    \item \emph{Redshift ODE calibration.} The redshift weight $\varpi_\varepsilon$ gives the finite weighted Riccati--Jacobi variation alternative of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftODEContinuationCertificate}.
\end{enumerate}
The datum is \emph{globally calibrated} if on every overlap $U_i\cap U_j$ the differences of the Bel--Robinson boundary currents, BV traces, screen area forms, eikonal exhaustions, neck densities, and redshift weights are represented by a nonnegative current $\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}$ with
\[
    \sum_{i<j}\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \le
    \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)
\]
as Radon measures on $K$, where $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$ denotes the horizon absorption current.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Overlap-positive calibration cocycle]\label{def:OverlapPositiveCalibrationCocycle}
Let $\{U_i\}_{i=1}^N$ be a finite optical chart cover of a retained compact exterior region $K$, and suppose each $U_i$ carries local data satisfying the six local calibration laws in Definition~\ref{def:MasterOpticalCalibration}. An \emph{overlap-positive calibration cocycle} consists of a subordinate smooth partition of unity $\{\psi_i\}$ and nonnegative Radon currents $\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}$ on $U_i\cap U_j$ such that:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item the negative parts of all chart-transition mismatches of Bel--Robinson boundary currents, BV traces, screen area forms, eikonal exhaustions, neck densities, and redshift weights are bounded by $\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}$;
    \item every partition commutator produced by differentiating $\psi_i$ in the Bel--Robinson divergence identity, BV transport identities, screen elliptic equation, eikonal transport inequality, neck-energy accounting, or redshift Riccati--Jacobi ODE has negative part bounded by $\sum_{i<j}\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}+o_\varepsilon(1)$;
    \item the total overlap current is already charged by the named nonnegative currents,
    \[
        \sum_{i<j}\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}
        \le
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)
    \]
    as Radon measures on $K$.
\end{enumerate}
The constants in these estimates are required to be uniform under refinement of the cover.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Defect-exact transition gauge]\label{def:DefectExactTransitionGauge}
Let $\{U_i\}_{i=1}^N$ be a finite optical chart cover carrying local master calibration data. A \emph{defect-exact transition gauge} is a choice, on every overlap $U_i\cap U_j$, of transition potentials $\mathfrak A_{\varepsilon,ij}$ and nonnegative transition charges $\Theta_{\varepsilon,ij}$ with the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Exact transition splitting.} The full transition mismatch
    \[
        \mathfrak D_{\varepsilon,ij}
        :=
        \mathfrak C_{\varepsilon,i}-\mathfrak C_{\varepsilon,j}
    \]
    in the Bel--Robinson boundary current, BV traces, screen area form, eikonal exhaustion, neck density, and redshift weight has the distributional form
    \[
        \mathfrak D_{\varepsilon,ij}
        =
        \mathscr D_{\varepsilon,ij}\mathfrak A_{\varepsilon,ij}
        +
        \mathfrak R_{\varepsilon,ij}.
    \]
    Here $\mathscr D_{\varepsilon,ij}$ is the componentwise operator naturally entering the corresponding estimate: divergence for Bel--Robinson currents, trace derivative for BV data, distributional area derivative for screen forms, optical transport for eikonal and redshift data, and annular boundary for neck densities.
    \item \emph{Defect sign.} After pairing with the dual cone of the relevant estimate, the negative part of the residual satisfies
    \[
        \bigl(\mathfrak R_{\varepsilon,ij}\bigr)^-
        \le
        \Theta_{\varepsilon,ij}.
    \]
    The positive part is allowed to remain as a favorable overlap contribution.
    \item \emph{Commutator absorption.} For some subordinate partition of unity $\{\psi_i\}$,
    \[
        \sum_i |D\psi_i|\,|\mathfrak A_{\varepsilon,ij}|
        +
        \bigl(\mathscr D_{\varepsilon,ij}\psi_i\bigr)^-|\mathfrak A_{\varepsilon,ij}|
        \le
        C_K\Theta_{\varepsilon,ij}+o_\varepsilon(1)
    \]
    as Radon measures on each overlap, with the obvious componentwise interpretation for transport and trace derivatives.
    \item \emph{Global charge bound.}
    \[
        \sum_{i<j}\Theta_{\varepsilon,ij}
        \le
        C_K\left(\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}\right)
        +o_\varepsilon(1)
    \]
    as Radon measures on $K$.
\end{enumerate}
The constants are required to depend only on the retained compact exterior region $K$ and the bounded overlap number of the cover.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Defect-exact gauges produce positive cocycles]\label{thm:DefectExactGaugePositiveCocycle}
Assume that a finite optical chart cover of a retained compact exterior region carries local master calibration data and a defect-exact transition gauge. Then the cover admits an overlap-positive calibration cocycle.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Set
\[
    \Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}:=C_K\Theta_{\varepsilon,ij}
\]
with $C_K$ large enough to absorb the bounded overlap number and the constants in Definition~\ref{def:DefectExactTransitionGauge}. We verify the three clauses of Definition~\ref{def:OverlapPositiveCalibrationCocycle}.

First consider a direct transition mismatch on $U_i\cap U_j$. By the exact splitting,
\[
    \mathfrak D_{\varepsilon,ij}
    =
    \mathscr D_{\varepsilon,ij}\mathfrak A_{\varepsilon,ij}
    +
    \mathfrak R_{\varepsilon,ij}.
\]
The exact part contributes only through the boundary, trace, transport, or annular endpoint term appropriate to the corresponding calibration law. These exact contributions cancel in opposite orientations when the two charts are compared, except for the terms created by the partition used to localize the comparison. The residual negative part is bounded by $\Theta_{\varepsilon,ij}$ by the defect-sign clause. Thus the negative part of every direct chart-transition mismatch is bounded by $\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}$.

Now differentiate the localized exact term. Componentwise, every sector has the same algebraic form:
\[
    \mathscr D_{\varepsilon,ij}(\psi_i\mathfrak A_{\varepsilon,ij})
    =
    \psi_i\,\mathscr D_{\varepsilon,ij}\mathfrak A_{\varepsilon,ij}
    +
    [\mathscr D_{\varepsilon,ij},\psi_i]\mathfrak A_{\varepsilon,ij}.
\]
For a divergence this is the usual $(D\psi_i)\cdot\mathfrak A_{\varepsilon,ij}$ term; for BV traces it is the trace derivative of $\psi_i$ times the transition potential; for screen area, eikonal, neck, and redshift sectors it is the corresponding distributional product-rule commutator. The commutator-absorption clause says precisely that the negative part of the sum of these localized commutators is bounded by $C_K\Theta_{\varepsilon,ij}+o_\varepsilon(1)$, hence by $\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}+o_\varepsilon(1)$.

Finally, summing over overlaps gives
\[
    \sum_{i<j}\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \le
    C_K\sum_{i<j}\Theta_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \le
    C_K\left(\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}\right)
    +o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
After enlarging the named defect and horizon currents by the harmless compact constant already allowed in the uniform calibration estimates, this is exactly the total-overlap charge condition. Hence the currents $\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}$ define an overlap-positive calibration cocycle.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Bel--Robinson overlap envelope]\label{def:BelRobinsonOverlapEnvelope}
Let $\{U_i\}_{i=1}^N$ be a finite optical chart cover of a retained compact exterior region $K$, and let $(T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},\rho_{\varepsilon,i})$ be local Bel--Robinson multiplier data on each $U_i$. A \emph{Bel--Robinson overlap envelope} is a family of nonnegative Radon currents $\Omega^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}$ on $U_i\cap U_j$ such that for every subordinate partition of unity $\{\psi_i\}$,
\[
    \rho_{\varepsilon,\psi}
    Q[W_\varepsilon]\!\left(\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,\psi},N_{\varepsilon,\psi},N_{\varepsilon,\psi}\right)
    \le
    \sum_i \psi_i\,\rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
    +
    \sum_{i<j}\Omega^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij},
\]
after pairing with every future causal covector in the dual cone, where
\[
    T_{\varepsilon,\psi}:=\sum_i\psi_iT_{\varepsilon,i},
    \qquad
    N_{\varepsilon,\psi}:=\sum_i\psi_iN_{\varepsilon,i},
    \qquad
    \rho_{\varepsilon,\psi}:=\sum_i\psi_i\rho_{\varepsilon,i},
\]
and
\[
    \sum_{i<j}\Omega^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \le
    \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)
\]
as Radon measures on $K$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Bel--Robinson multiplier coherence]\label{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCoherence}
The local Bel--Robinson multipliers are \emph{coherent on overlaps} if, after normalizing by the optical frame on $K$, the vectors $T_{\varepsilon,i}$ and $N_{\varepsilon,i}$ lie in a fixed compact subcone of the future timelike cone, the weights obey
\[
    0<c_K\le \rho_{\varepsilon,i}\le C_K,
\]
and for every subordinate partition $\{\psi_i\}$ the pairwise mismatch current
\[
\begin{split}
    d\mathfrak M^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    &:=
    C_K\psi_i\psi_j
    \left(
        |T_{\varepsilon,i}-T_{\varepsilon,j}|
        +
        |N_{\varepsilon,i}-N_{\varepsilon,j}|
        +
        |\rho_{\varepsilon,i}-\rho_{\varepsilon,j}|
    \right) \\
    &\quad\cdot
    \left(
        \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
        +
        \rho_{\varepsilon,j}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j})
    \right)
\end{split}
\]
is charged by the named nonnegative currents:
\[
    \sum_{i<j}\mathfrak M^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \le
    \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)
\]
after pairing with every future causal covector in the dual cone. The innovation contract is: multiplier coherence proves the Bel--Robinson overlap envelope from local overlap smallness; the remaining debt is to derive the mismatch-current charge from the optical transition geometry.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Redshift-aligned Bel--Robinson transition geometry]\label{def:RedshiftAlignedBRTransitionGeometry}
The local Bel--Robinson multiplier data are \emph{redshift-aligned by the optical transition geometry} if, on every overlap $U_i\cap U_j$, there is a nonnegative scalar transition density $\upsilon^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}$ with the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Compact cone generation.} In each optical frame, the multipliers and weight have the form
    \[
        (T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},\rho_{\varepsilon,i})
        =
        \mathfrak P_K(\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,i},\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,i}),
    \]
    where $\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,i}$ is the local optical frame, $\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,i}$ denotes the redshift lapse, radius, and cutoff parameters, and $\mathfrak P_K$ is uniformly Lipschitz on a compact parameter set. Its vector components take values in a fixed compact subcone of the future timelike cone and its scalar component lies in $[c_K,C_K]$.
    \item \emph{Transition control.} The density $\upsilon^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}$ controls the normalized optical transition variation:
    \[
        |T_{\varepsilon,i}-T_{\varepsilon,j}|
        +
        |N_{\varepsilon,i}-N_{\varepsilon,j}|
        +
        |\rho_{\varepsilon,i}-\rho_{\varepsilon,j}|
        \le
        C_K\upsilon^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \]
    on $U_i\cap U_j$.
    \item \emph{Flux-weighted transition charge.} For every subordinate partition of unity $\{\psi_i\}$,
    \[
    \begin{split}
        &\sum_{i<j}
        C_K\psi_i\psi_j\upsilon^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}
        \left(
            \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
            +
            \rho_{\varepsilon,j}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j})
        \right) \\
        &\qquad\le
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)
    \end{split}
    \]
    as Radon measures on $K$, after pairing with every future causal covector in the dual cone.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: redshift-aligned transition geometry proves Bel--Robinson multiplier coherence; the remaining debt is the geometric construction of the transition charge $\upsilon^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}$ from the optical frame equations.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Optical-frame transition charge certificate]\label{def:OpticalFrameTransitionChargeCertificate}
Let $\{U_i\}_{i=1}^N$ be a finite optical chart cover of a retained compact exterior region $K$, carrying local Bel--Robinson multiplier data
\[
    (T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},\rho_{\varepsilon,i})
    =
    \mathfrak P_K(\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,i},\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,i})
\]
as in Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftAlignedBRTransitionGeometry}.  The cover admits an \emph{optical-frame transition charge certificate} if, on each overlap $U_i\cap U_j$, there are nonnegative scalar densities
\[
    \omega^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij},
    \qquad
    \omega^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij},
    \qquad
    \omega^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij},
\]
with the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Compact optical gauge.} The frames $\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,i}$, the redshift lapse, radius, and cutoff parameters $\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,i}$, and all transition maps between overlapping frames lie in a fixed compact optical-gauge chart over $K$.  On this compact set the multiplier map $\mathfrak P_K$ is uniformly Lipschitz, its vector components lie in a fixed compact subcone of the future timelike cone, and its scalar component lies in $[c_K,C_K]$.
    \item \emph{Frame--redshift variation density.}  With
    \[
        \Upsilon^{\mathrm{of}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
        :=
        \omega^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
        +
        \omega^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
        +
        \omega^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij},
    \]
    the normalized frame distance and parameter distance satisfy
    \[
        d_{\mathfrak E,K}(\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,i},\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,j})
        +
        |\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,i}-\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,j}|
        \le
        \Upsilon^{\mathrm{of}}_{\varepsilon,ij}.
    \]
    Here $d_{\mathfrak E,K}$ denotes the distance in the fixed compact optical-gauge chart.  The intended geometric content is that $\omega^{\mathrm{fr}}$ is produced by the optical frame equations, $\omega^{\mathrm{rs}}$ by the redshift lapse comparison, and $\omega^{\mathrm{cut}}$ by the radius and cutoff transport comparison.
    \item \emph{Flux-weighted variation charge.}  For every subordinate partition of unity $\{\psi_i\}$,
    \[
    \begin{split}
        &\sum_{i<j}
        C_K\psi_i\psi_j\Upsilon^{\mathrm{of}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
        \left(
            \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
            +
            \rho_{\varepsilon,j}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j})
        \right) \\
        &\qquad\le
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)
    \end{split}
    \]
    as Radon measures on $K$, after pairing with every future causal covector in the dual cone.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: this certificate reduces redshift-aligned Bel--Robinson transition geometry to a local optical-frame variation estimate plus a flux-weighted charge estimate.  It does not assume the global area comparison or the Penrose conclusion.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Flux-weighted null-structure transition system]\label{def:FluxWeightedNullStructureTransitionSystem}
On an overlap $U_i\cap U_j$, write
\[
    \delta\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,ij}
    :=
    d_{\mathfrak E,K}(\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,i},\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,j}),
    \qquad
    \delta\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,ij}
    :=
    |\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,i}-\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,j}|
\]
in the compact optical-gauge chart of Definition~\ref{def:OpticalFrameTransitionChargeCertificate}.  The local optical charts satisfy a \emph{flux-weighted null-structure transition system} if the following hold.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Synchronized initial gauge.} The frame and parameter mismatches have vanishing incoming trace up to an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ transition mass already charged by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$.
    \item \emph{Triangular transition transport.} Along each retained generator, in the sense of Radon measures,
    \[
        D_L(\delta\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,ij}+\delta\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,ij})
        \le
        a_{\varepsilon,ij}
        (\delta\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,ij}+\delta\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,ij})
        +
        b^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
        +
        b^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
        +
        b^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij},
    \]
    where $a_{\varepsilon,ij}\ge0$ is a scalar optical-connection variation coefficient and the three nonnegative source densities measure, respectively, frame-connection mismatch, redshift-lapse mismatch, and radius/cutoff mismatch.
    \item \emph{Uniform optical Hardy operator.} The accumulated coefficient
    \[
        A_{\varepsilon,ij}(\lambda)
        :=
        \int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda}a_{\varepsilon,ij}(s)\,ds
    \]
    is uniformly bounded above on $K$, after recording any exceptional endpoint loss in $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$.  The associated positive Hardy operator is
    \[
        \mathcal H_{\varepsilon,ij}f(\lambda)
        :=
        e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}(\lambda)}
        \int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda}
        e^{-A_{\varepsilon,ij}(s)}f(s)\,ds .
    \]
    It is required to be admissible for the Bel--Robinson flux measure in the following sense: for every nonnegative source family $f_{\varepsilon,ij}$ obtained from the three transition source densities and every subordinate partition of unity,
    \[
    \begin{split}
        &\sum_{i<j}
        C_K\psi_i\psi_j
        \mathcal H_{\varepsilon,ij}f_{\varepsilon,ij}
        \left(
            \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
            +
            \rho_{\varepsilon,j}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j})
        \right) \\
        &\qquad\le
        \sum_{i<j}
        C_K\psi_i\psi_j
        e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}f_{\varepsilon,ij}
        \left(
            \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
            +
            \rho_{\varepsilon,j}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j})
        \right)
        +o_\varepsilon(1)
    \end{split}
    \]
    after pairing with every future causal covector.  This is the only one-dimensional Hardy input in the transition system.
    \item \emph{Flux-weighted source charge.} For every subordinate partition of unity $\{\psi_i\}$,
    \[
    \begin{split}
        &\sum_{i<j}
        C_K\psi_i\psi_j
        e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}
        \left(
            b^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
            +
            b^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
            +
            b^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
        \right) \\
        &\quad\cdot
        \left(
            \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
            +
            \rho_{\varepsilon,j}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j})
        \right) \\
        &\qquad\le
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)
    \end{split}
    \]
    as Radon measures on $K$, after pairing with every future causal covector in the dual cone.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: this system is the local null-structure object one should prove from the frame equations.  It replaces a direct multiplier-overlap estimate by a triangular transport inequality, a one-dimensional flux Hardy estimate, and one flux-weighted source charge.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[One-dimensional flux Hardy criterion]\label{lem:OneDimensionalFluxHardyCriterion}
Let $I=[\lambda_0,\lambda_1]$, let $A$ be an absolutely continuous nonnegative function on $I$, and let
\[
    \mathcal H_A f(\lambda)
    :=
    e^{A(\lambda)}
    \int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda}e^{-A(s)}f(s)\,ds .
\]
Let $d\mu=F(\lambda)\,d\lambda$ be a nonnegative absolutely continuous flux measure.  Assume the backward tail domination
\begin{equation}\label{eq:FluxHardyTailDomination}
    e^{-A(s)}
    \int_s^{\lambda_1}e^{A(\lambda)}F(\lambda)\,d\lambda
    \le
    C_H e^{A(s)}F(s)
    \qquad\text{for a.e. }s\in I .
\end{equation}
Then, for every nonnegative $f$,
\[
    \int_I \mathcal H_A f\,d\mu
    \le
    C_H\int_I e^A f\,d\mu .
\]
Consequently, the Hardy-admissibility clause in Definition~\ref{def:FluxWeightedNullStructureTransitionSystem} follows whenever the paired Bel--Robinson overlap flux densities satisfy \eqref{eq:FluxHardyTailDomination} uniformly on all retained generators, overlaps, partitions, and future causal covectors, with any endpoint concentration charged to $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Tonelli's theorem and positivity,
\[
\begin{split}
    \int_I \mathcal H_A f(\lambda)F(\lambda)\,d\lambda
    &=
    \int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda_1}
    e^{A(\lambda)}
    \int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda}e^{-A(s)}f(s)\,ds\,
    F(\lambda)\,d\lambda \\
    &=
    \int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda_1}
    f(s)e^{-A(s)}
    \left(
        \int_s^{\lambda_1}e^{A(\lambda)}F(\lambda)\,d\lambda
    \right)ds .
\end{split}
\]
Applying the tail domination \eqref{eq:FluxHardyTailDomination} gives
\[
    \int_I \mathcal H_A f\,d\mu
    \le
    C_H\int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda_1}f(s)e^{A(s)}F(s)\,ds
    =
    C_H\int_I e^A f\,d\mu .
\]
For weak limits of smooth approximants, apply the estimate before passing to the limit.  The left side is lower semicontinuous for nonnegative measures, while any terminal mass not represented by an absolutely continuous density is exactly the endpoint concentration recorded in the horizon absorption current.  This gives the stated Bel--Robinson Hardy-admissibility consequence.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Compact-gauge Kato transition inequality]\label{lem:CompactGaugeKatoTransition}
Let $\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,i}:=(\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,i},\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,i})$ denote the optical frame and parameter state in a compact optical-gauge chart over $K$.  Suppose that on an overlap $U_i\cap U_j$ the states satisfy, in the sense of vector-valued Radon measures along each retained generator,
\[
    D_L\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,i}
    =
    \Phi_K(\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,i})
    +
    S^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,i}
    +
    S^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,i}
    +
    S^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,i},
\]
where $\Phi_K$ is $L_K$-Lipschitz on the compact chart and
\[
    |S^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,i}-S^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,j}|
    \le b^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij},
    \quad
    |S^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,i}-S^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,j}|
    \le b^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij},
    \quad
    |S^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,i}-S^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,j}|
    \le b^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij}.
\]
Then
\[
    D_L d_{\mathfrak X,K}(\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,i},\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,j})
    \le
    L_K d_{\mathfrak X,K}(\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,i},\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,j})
    +
    b^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    +
    b^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    +
    b^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
\]
as nonnegative Radon measures.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In the compact chart the distance $d_{\mathfrak X,K}$ is equivalent, up to constants depending only on $K$, to the Euclidean norm of the coordinate difference
\[
    Z_{\varepsilon,ij}:=\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,i}-\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,j}.
\]
For smooth approximants, Kato's inequality gives
\[
    D_L|Z_{\varepsilon,ij}|
    \le
    |D_LZ_{\varepsilon,ij}|.
\]
Subtracting the two structure equations yields
\[
\begin{split}
    |D_LZ_{\varepsilon,ij}|
    &\le
    |\Phi_K(\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,i})-\Phi_K(\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,j})|
    +
    |S^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,i}-S^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,j}| \\
    &\quad+
    |S^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,i}-S^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,j}|
    +
    |S^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,i}-S^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,j}| \\
    &\le
    L_K|Z_{\varepsilon,ij}|
    +
    b^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    +
    b^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    +
    b^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij}.
\end{split}
\]
The equivalence of compact-chart norms only changes $L_K$ and the harmless constant in front of the source densities.  Passing from smooth approximants to BV/Radon limits is justified by lower semicontinuity of variation and the distributional Kato inequality for vector-valued BV functions.  This proves the stated measure inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Redshift sink gives Hardy tail domination]\label{lem:RedshiftSinkHardyTailDomination}
Let $A$ and $F$ be as in Lemma~\ref{lem:OneDimensionalFluxHardyCriterion}.  Assume that, on the retained part of the generator, the paired flux has a redshift sink
\[
    -D_L(e^A F)\ge \kappa_K e^AF
\]
in the distributional sense, for some $\kappa_K>0$, with any terminal concentration recorded in the horizon absorption current.  Then the tail domination \eqref{eq:FluxHardyTailDomination} holds with $C_H=\kappa_K^{-1}$, after the same horizon-absorption recording.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Integrating the distributional inequality from $s$ to $\lambda_1$ gives
\[
    \kappa_K\int_s^{\lambda_1}e^{A(\lambda)}F(\lambda)\,d\lambda
    \le
    e^{A(s)}F(s)-e^{A(\lambda_1)}F(\lambda_1)+\mathcal E_{\mathrm{end}}([s,\lambda_1]),
\]
where the nonnegative endpoint concentration $\mathcal E_{\mathrm{end}}$ is part of the horizon absorption accounting.  Dropping the favorable terminal flux and recording $\mathcal E_{\mathrm{end}}$ in $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$ gives
\[
    \int_s^{\lambda_1}e^{A(\lambda)}F(\lambda)\,d\lambda
    \le
    \kappa_K^{-1}e^{A(s)}F(s).
\]
Since $A\ge0$, multiplying by $e^{-A(s)}$ yields
\[
    e^{-A(s)}
    \int_s^{\lambda_1}e^{A(\lambda)}F(\lambda)\,d\lambda
    \le
    \kappa_K^{-1}F(s)
    \le
    \kappa_K^{-1}e^{A(s)}F(s),
\]
which is \eqref{eq:FluxHardyTailDomination}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Transported strict redshift flux certificate]\label{def:TransportedStrictRedshiftFluxCertificate}
The overlap $U_i\cap U_j$ carries a \emph{transported strict redshift flux certificate} if, for every subordinate partition of unity $\{\psi_i\}$ used in the overlap gluing and every future causal covector $\xi$, the following hold along each retained generator.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Transported overlap weight.} The overlap factor
    \[
        \Psi_{\varepsilon,ij}:=\psi_i\psi_j
    \]
    is nonincreasing toward the future in the redshift direction:
    \[
        -D_L\Psi_{\varepsilon,ij}\ge0
    \]
    as a Radon measure, after recording any terminal cutoff loss in $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$.
    \item \emph{Strict one-chart redshift sink.} With
    \[
        F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
        :=
        \rho_{\varepsilon,i}
        Q[W_\varepsilon](\xi,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i}),
        \qquad
        F_{\varepsilon,j,\xi}
        :=
        \rho_{\varepsilon,j}
        Q[W_\varepsilon](\xi,T_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j}),
    \]
    each local flux obeys
    \[
        -D_L(e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,\ell,\xi})
        \ge
        \kappa_K e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,\ell,\xi},
        \qquad
        \ell\in\{i,j\},
    \]
    in the distributional sense, with any endpoint concentration recorded in the horizon absorption current.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the paired overlap redshift sink is reduced to the standard local redshift multiplier mechanism plus a generator-transported overlap partition.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Transported overlap partitions from optical flow boxes]\label{lem:FlowBoxTransportedOverlapPartitions}
Let $K$ be a retained compact exterior region on which the nonabsorbed regular part of the optical construction is covered by finitely many generator-saturated optical flow boxes.  Thus, after discarding only the named defect and horizon-absorbed families, each refined chart has coordinates
\[
    \Phi_i:(\lambda_-,\lambda_+)\times V_i\longrightarrow U_i,
    \qquad
    L=\partial_\lambda,
\]
and the projection $\pi_i:U_i\to V_i$ is constant along the retained generators.  Then the cover admits a subordinate partition of unity $\{\psi_i\}$ such that, on every overlap,
\[
    \Psi_{\varepsilon,ij}:=\psi_i\psi_j
    \qquad\text{satisfies}\qquad
    -D_L\Psi_{\varepsilon,ij}=0
\]
