as a Radon measure on the regular part.  If a terminal cutoff is inserted at the horizon collar, its negative derivative is recorded in $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$, and the transported-overlap inequality in Definition~\ref{def:TransportedStrictRedshiftFluxCertificate} still holds.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On the quotient generator space of the saturated refinement choose a smooth partition of unity $\{\vartheta_i\}$ subordinate to the sets $V_i$.  Define
\[
    \psi_i:=\vartheta_i\circ\pi_i
\]
on $U_i$ and use the usual agreement of the quotient coordinates on overlaps.  Since $\pi_i$ is constant along $L$, one has $D_L\psi_i=0$ on the regular flow-box part.  Hence
\[
    D_L(\psi_i\psi_j)
    =
    (D_L\psi_i)\psi_j+\psi_i(D_L\psi_j)
    =
    0 .
\]
For BV limits of smooth regularizations this identity passes to Radon measures by weak convergence of the transported functions and lower semicontinuity of total variation.

If the original optical cover is not saturated, refine it by intersecting with the regular flow boxes of the optical generator map on $K$.  Compactness of the retained regular quotient gives a finite refinement, while the complement consists only of the caustic, cut-locus, zero-area, or horizon-absorbed strata already represented by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$.  A cutoff placed in a terminal horizon collar can be chosen nonincreasing in the future redshift direction.  Its derivative contributes a nonnegative term to $-D_L\Psi_{\varepsilon,ij}$, and the lost terminal mass is precisely part of the horizon absorption accounting.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Tube redshift disintegration gives strict one-chart flux decay]\label{thm:TubeRedshiftDisintegrationStrictFlux}
Fix an optical chart $U_i$, an overlap coefficient $A_{\varepsilon,ij}$, and a future causal covector $\xi$.  Set
\[
    F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    :=
    \rho_{\varepsilon,i}
    Q[W_\varepsilon](\xi,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i}),
    \qquad
    G_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    :=
    e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}.
\]
Assume that the redshifted Bel--Robinson multiplier identity has the following tube form: for every generator-saturated Borel set $B$ in the quotient of $U_i$ and every $\lambda_-<s<t<\lambda_+$,
\[
    \int_B G_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(t,y)\,d\nu_\varepsilon(y)
    +
    \kappa_K
    \int_s^t\int_B G_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(\lambda,y)\,d\nu_\varepsilon(y)\,d\lambda
    \le
    \int_B G_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(s,y)\,d\nu_\varepsilon(y)
    +
    \mathcal E^{\mathrm{end}}_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}([s,t]\times B),
\]
where $\kappa_K>0$ is independent of $i,j,\varepsilon,\xi$, $d\nu_\varepsilon$ is the quotient generator measure, and the endpoint measure $\mathcal E^{\mathrm{end}}_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}$ is supported only on defect or horizon-terminal strata charged by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$.
Then, on every retained generator not charged by this endpoint measure,
\[
    -D_L\!\left(e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}\right)
    \ge
    \kappa_K e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
\]
in the distributional sense.  The same conclusion holds for weak limits of smooth regularizations, with the terminal concentration recorded in $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Write $G=G_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}$ and suppress the fixed indices.  On a compact subinterval whose associated tube is disjoint from the support of $\mathcal E^{\mathrm{end}}$, the assumed inequality gives
\[
    \int_B G(t,y)\,d\nu_\varepsilon(y)
    -
    \int_B G(s,y)\,d\nu_\varepsilon(y)
    +
    \kappa_K
    \int_s^t\int_B G(\lambda,y)\,d\nu_\varepsilon(y)\,d\lambda
    \le 0
\]
for every quotient Borel set $B$.  By testing first on simple functions in $y$ and then on nonnegative $C^1$ functions of $\lambda$, this is exactly the product-measure inequality
\[
    D_LG+\kappa_KG\le0
\]
on the regular part.  Disintegrating with respect to $d\nu_\varepsilon(y)$ gives
\[
    D_LG(\cdot,y)+\kappa_KG(\cdot,y)\le0
\]
for $d\nu_\varepsilon$-almost every retained generator.  Equivalently,
\[
    -D_LG(\cdot,y)\ge \kappa_KG(\cdot,y).
\]

If the interval meets the terminal support of $\mathcal E^{\mathrm{end}}$, the same argument gives the inequality with an additional nonnegative endpoint measure.  By hypothesis this measure is already part of the defect or horizon absorption accounting, so it is harmless for Definition~\ref{def:TransportedStrictRedshiftFluxCertificate}.  For weak limits, apply the tube inequality to smooth approximants and pass to the limit using weak lower semicontinuity for nonnegative Radon measures and the slice disintegration theorem.  This proves the distributional generator inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Overlap-tilted redshift surplus certificate]\label{def:OverlapTiltedRedshiftSurplusCertificate}
Fix an optical chart $U_i$, an overlap $U_i\cap U_j$, and a future causal covector $\xi$.  The one-chart Bel--Robinson flux
\[
    F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    :=
    \rho_{\varepsilon,i}
    Q[W_\varepsilon](\xi,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
\]
carries an \emph{overlap-tilted redshift surplus certificate} if, with the overlap accumulation $A_{\varepsilon,ij}$, the following hold on every generator-saturated tube $[s,t]\times B$ in the regular quotient.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Untilted redshift balance.}  There are nonnegative Radon measures
    $\mathfrak r_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}$ and
    $\mathcal E^{\mathrm{bd}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}$ such that
    \[
        -D_LF_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
        \ge
        \mathfrak r_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
        -
        \mathcal E^{\mathrm{bd}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    \]
    in the distributional generator sense.  Equivalently,
    \[
        \int_B F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(t,y)\,d\nu_\varepsilon(y)
        +
        \mathfrak r_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}([s,t]\times B)
        \le
        \int_B F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(s,y)\,d\nu_\varepsilon(y)
        +
        \mathcal E^{\mathrm{bd}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}([s,t]\times B).
    \]
    \item \emph{Surplus over the overlap tilt.}  For some $\kappa_K>0$ independent of $i,j,\varepsilon,\xi$,
    \[
        \mathfrak r_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
        \ge
        \left(\kappa_K+D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij}\right)
        F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
        -
        \mathfrak e^{\mathrm{low}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    \]
    as Radon measures on the retained regular part, where $\mathfrak e^{\mathrm{low}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}\ge0$ records deformation, commutator, matter, and smoothing errors not carrying the strict redshift sign.
    \item \emph{Tilted error absorption.}  The tilted boundary and lower-order errors obey
    \[
        \int_{[s,t]\times B}e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}\,
        d\mathfrak e^{\mathrm{low}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
        +
        \int_{[s,t]\times B}e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}\,
        d\mathcal E^{\mathrm{bd}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
        \le
        \mathcal E^{\mathrm{end}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}([s,t]\times B),
    \]
    where $\mathcal E^{\mathrm{end}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}$ is supported only on defect, artificial-cap, or horizon-terminal strata already charged by
    $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)$.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the only extra positivity needed beyond the standard redshift multiplier identity is a local surplus margin beating the overlap tilt $D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij}$; the remaining debt is to prove this surplus from the deformation tensor and source absorption estimates.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Redshift deformation-margin certificate]\label{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate}
Fix an optical chart $U_i$, an overlap $U_i\cap U_j$, and a future causal covector $\xi$.  Let
\[
    F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    :=
    \rho_{\varepsilon,i}
    Q[W_\varepsilon](\xi,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
\]
be the one-chart Bel--Robinson flux.  The chart carries a \emph{redshift deformation-margin certificate relative to the overlap} if the Bel--Robinson divergence identity can be disintegrated on every generator-saturated tube so that
\[
    -D_LF_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    \ge
    \mathcal R_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    -
    \mathcal L_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    -
    \mathcal B_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
\]
as Radon measures along retained generators, with the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Deformation margin.}  The redshift deformation part has a pointwise current lower bound
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
        \ge
        \left(\kappa_K+D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij}\right)
        F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
        +
        \mathcal S_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    \]
    for some $\kappa_K>0$ independent of $i,j,\varepsilon,\xi$, where $\mathcal S_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}\ge0$ is a disposable surplus current.
    \item \emph{Tilted lower-order absorption.}  On every generator-saturated tube $[s,t]\times B$,
    \[
        \int_{[s,t]\times B}e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}\,
        d\mathcal L_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
        +
        \int_{[s,t]\times B}e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}\,
        d\mathcal B_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
        \le
        \mathcal E^{\mathrm{end}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}([s,t]\times B),
    \]
    where $\mathcal E^{\mathrm{end}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}$ is supported only on defect, artificial-cap, Bondi-renormalized, or horizon-terminal strata already charged by
    $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)$.
    \item \emph{Compact comparability.}  The overlap accumulation $A_{\varepsilon,ij}$ is uniformly bounded above on $K$, and all fluxes used in the disintegrated identity are comparable, with constants depending only on the compact optical gauge, to the compact-cone Bel--Robinson current in Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCertificate}.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: this certificate is useful exactly when the redshift deformation tensor supplies a quantitative margin stronger than the overlap variation coefficient.  The remaining analytic debt is a local deformation-tensor computation plus tilted absorption of lower-order terms.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Finite compact-cone redshift matrix certificate]\label{def:FiniteCompactConeRedshiftMatrixCertificate}
Fix an optical chart $U_i$, an overlap $U_i\cap U_j$, and a future causal covector $\xi$.  Let
\[
    Z_\varepsilon
    =
    (\alpha_\varepsilon,\beta_\varepsilon,\rho_\varepsilon^W,\sigma_\varepsilon^W,
    \underline\beta_\varepsilon,\underline\alpha_\varepsilon)
    \in \mathcal W_K\simeq\mathbb R^{10}
\]
denote the Weyl curvature state in a compact normalized null frame
$(L_\varepsilon,\underline L_\varepsilon,E_{1,\varepsilon},E_{2,\varepsilon})$; here
$\alpha_\varepsilon$ and $\underline\alpha_\varepsilon$ are trace-free screen
two-tensors, $\beta_\varepsilon,\underline\beta_\varepsilon$ are screen one-forms,
and $\rho_\varepsilon^W,\sigma_\varepsilon^W$ are the electric and magnetic scalar
components.  Put $a_{\varepsilon,ij}:=D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij}$ on the regular part
of the generator tube, with the positive singular part of $D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij}$
assigned to the named defect or horizon-terminal endpoint measure.

The chart carries a \emph{finite compact-cone redshift matrix certificate relative
to the overlap} if the following hold on every retained generator-saturated tube.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Quadratic optical-frame representation.}  There are symmetric
    matrices
    \[
        \mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p),
        \qquad
        \mathsf M_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}(p)
        \qquad (p=(\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,i},\xi,a_{\varepsilon,ij}))
    \]
    depending continuously on the compact optical state, the normalized future
    covector, and the overlap-tilt density, such that
    \[
        F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
        =
        \left\langle
        \mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)Z_\varepsilon,Z_\varepsilon
        \right\rangle
    \]
    and the regular redshift deformation current in the Bel--Robinson divergence
    identity is
    \[
        d\mathcal R^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
        =
        \left\langle
        \mathsf M_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}(p)Z_\varepsilon,Z_\varepsilon
        \right\rangle d\lambda\,d\nu_\varepsilon .
    \]
    The remaining deformation, commutator, matter, smoothing, artificial-cap, and
    singular-tilt terms are the nonnegative measures
    $\mathcal L_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}$ and $\mathcal B_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}$ in
    Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate}.

    \item \emph{Compact flux equivalence.}  On the compact parameter set
    $\mathfrak P_K$ swept out by the retained optical states, normalized future
    covectors, and admissible overlap-tilt densities,
    \[
        c_H|Z|^2
        \le
        \left\langle \mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)Z,Z\right\rangle
        \le
        C_H|Z|^2
    \]
    for constants depending only on $K$.  The normalized forms
    \[
        \mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)^{-1/2}
        \left(
            \mathsf M_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}(p)
            -
            (\kappa_K+a(p))\mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)
        \right)
        \mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)^{-1/2}
    \]
    are $L_K$-Lipschitz on $\mathfrak P_K$.

    \item \emph{Finite robust spectral margin.}  There is a finite
    $\eta_K$-net $\mathcal N_K\subset\mathfrak P_K$ and a number
    $\sigma_K>2L_K\eta_K$ such that, for every $q\in\mathcal N_K$ and every
    $Z\in\mathcal W_K$,
    \[
        \left\langle
        \left(
            \mathsf M_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}(q)
            -
            (\kappa_K+a(q))\mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(q)
        \right)Z,Z
        \right\rangle
        \ge
        \sigma_K
        \left\langle
        \mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(q)Z,Z
        \right\rangle .
    \]

    \item \emph{Tilted nonquadratic absorption.}  The lower-order and boundary
    measures obey the tilted absorption estimate in
    Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate}.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the hard redshift sign has been reduced to finitely
many positive-semidefinite matrix inequalities on the normalized Weyl curvature
state; the remaining debt is to compute those matrices from the local optical
frame equations and verify the endpoint absorption of nonquadratic terms.
Failure probe: the criterion fails exactly if a normalized Weyl state in the
compact optical cone makes the redshift deformation matrix lose this spectral
gap, or if the overlap-tilt variation concentrates outside the defect/horizon
accounting.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Interval Gram redshift matrix certificate]\label{def:IntervalGramRedshiftMatrixCertificate}
Fix the data of Definition~\ref{def:FiniteCompactConeRedshiftMatrixCertificate}.
An \emph{interval Gram redshift matrix certificate} consists of a finite
coordinate cover
\[
    \mathfrak P_K\subset \bigcup_{\ell=1}^N \Phi_\ell(B_\ell),
    \qquad
    B_\ell=\prod_{m=1}^{d_\ell}[a_{\ell m},b_{\ell m}],
\]
of the compact optical parameter set by rational boxes, together with rational
matrix functions on each box satisfying the following conditions.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Extracted rational frame algebra.}  In the chart
    $p=\Phi_\ell(u)$ the flux and deformation matrices have the form
    \[
        \mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)
        =
        d_\ell(u)^{-1}\mathsf H_\ell(u),
        \qquad
        \mathsf M_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}(p)
        =
        d_\ell(u)^{-1}\mathsf M_\ell(u),
    \]
    where $d_\ell(u)\ge d_{0,K}>0$ on $B_\ell$.  The entries of
    $\mathsf H_\ell,\mathsf M_\ell$, and the overlap-tilt function $a_\ell(u)$
    are rational functions with interval enclosures certified on $B_\ell$.

    \item \emph{Flux Gram positivity.}  There are constants
    $0<c_H<C_H<\infty$ and symmetric polynomial-matrix Gram decompositions,
    with interval remainders $E^H_\ell(u)$, such that on $B_\ell$
    \[
        \mathsf H_\ell(u)-c_Hd_\ell(u)I
        =
        V^H_\ell(u)^*G^H_\ell V^H_\ell(u)+E^H_\ell(u),
        \qquad
        C_Hd_\ell(u)I-\mathsf H_\ell(u)
        \succeq0,
    \]
    where $G^H_\ell\succeq0$ and
    $\|E^H_\ell(u)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le \frac12 c_Hd_{0,K}$.
    After decreasing $c_H$ by a fixed factor, this gives a certified uniform
    lower bound for the Bel--Robinson flux form.  Equivalently, the same flux
    lower and upper bounds may be supplied by an interval Cholesky or
    block-Gershgorin certificate after a finite subdivision of $B_\ell$.

    \item \emph{Redshift margin Gram positivity.}  There are constants
    $\kappa_K>0$ and $\sigma_K>0$ such that the margin matrix
    \[
        \mathsf P_\ell(u)
        :=
        \mathsf M_\ell(u)
        -
        (\kappa_K+a_\ell(u)+\sigma_K)\mathsf H_\ell(u)
    \]
    admits a polynomial-matrix Gram decomposition with interval remainder,
    \[
        \mathsf P_\ell(u)
        =
        V^P_\ell(u)^*G^P_\ell V^P_\ell(u)+E^P_\ell(u),
        \qquad
        G^P_\ell\succeq \mu_K I,
        \qquad
        \|E^P_\ell(u)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
        \le
        \frac12\mu_K\,\lambda_{\min}\!\bigl(V^P_\ell(u)^*V^P_\ell(u)\bigr),
    \]
    or, equivalently, an interval Cholesky certificate proving
    $\mathsf P_\ell(u)\succeq0$ throughout $B_\ell$.

    \item \emph{Derivative and overlap audit.}  Interval arithmetic on each box
    gives a finite bound $L_K$ for the derivative of the normalized form
    \[
        \mathsf H(p)^{-1/2}
        \left(
            \mathsf M(p)-(\kappa_K+a(p))\mathsf H(p)
        \right)
        \mathsf H(p)^{-1/2},
    \]
    and the tilted nonquadratic absorption estimate of
    Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate} holds for the
    lower-order and endpoint measures not represented by the quadratic matrices.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the remaining redshift sign problem is now an
auditable finite algebra package--rational optical-frame extraction, interval
denominator control, Gram positivity, derivative bounds, and endpoint
absorption.  The debt is not hidden: the Gram files or hand computations must
still be produced from the null-frame equations.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Block-dominant redshift normal-form certificate]\label{def:BlockDominantRedshiftNormalFormCertificate}
Fix the data of Definition~\ref{def:FiniteCompactConeRedshiftMatrixCertificate}
and decompose the normalized Weyl state into the five invariant optical blocks
\[
    \mathcal W_K
    =
    \mathcal W_{\alpha}\oplus\mathcal W_{\beta}
    \oplus\mathcal W_{0}
    \oplus\mathcal W_{\underline\beta}
    \oplus\mathcal W_{\underline\alpha},
    \qquad
    \mathcal W_0=\operatorname{span}\{\rho^W,\sigma^W\}.
\]
A \emph{block-dominant redshift normal-form certificate} consists of a finite
rational box cover and rational optical-frame matrices
$\mathsf H_\ell,\mathsf M_\ell,d_\ell,a_\ell$ as in
Definition~\ref{def:IntervalGramRedshiftMatrixCertificate}, but with the Gram
positivity witnesses replaced by the following scalar interval inequalities.
Let
\[
    \mathsf P_\ell(u)
    :=
    \mathsf M_\ell(u)
    -
    (\kappa_K+a_\ell(u)+\sigma_K)\mathsf H_\ell(u),
\]
and write $\mathsf H_{\ell,AB}$ and $\mathsf P_{\ell,AB}$ for the blocks
between $A,B\in\{\alpha,\beta,0,\underline\beta,\underline\alpha\}$.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Rational extraction and denominators.}  The rational extraction,
    denominator lower bound $d_\ell\ge d_{0,K}>0$, interval enclosure of
    $a_\ell$, and derivative audit for the normalized margin form are those of
    Definition~\ref{def:IntervalGramRedshiftMatrixCertificate}.

    \item \emph{Flux block dominance.}  There are constants
    $0<c_H<C_H<\infty$ and nonnegative interval numbers
    $e^H_{\ell,AB}=e^H_{\ell,BA}$ such that, on $B_\ell$,
    \[
        \left\langle \mathsf H_{\ell,AA}z_A,z_A\right\rangle
        \ge
        \underline h_{\ell,A}d_\ell |z_A|^2,
        \qquad
        \left\langle \mathsf H_{\ell,AA}z_A,z_A\right\rangle
        \le
        \overline h_{\ell,A}d_\ell |z_A|^2,
    \]
    and
    \[
        \|\mathsf H_{\ell,AB}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
        \le
        e^H_{\ell,AB}d_\ell
        \qquad(A\ne B).
    \]
    The row sums satisfy
    \[
        \underline h_{\ell,A}
        -
        \sum_{B\ne A}e^H_{\ell,AB}
        \ge c_H,
        \qquad
        \overline h_{\ell,A}
        +
        \sum_{B\ne A}e^H_{\ell,AB}
        \le C_H .
    \]

    \item \emph{Redshift margin block dominance.}  There are nonnegative interval
    numbers $e^P_{\ell,AB}=e^P_{\ell,BA}$ and a strict reserve $\tau_K>0$ such
    that, on $B_\ell$,
    \[
        \left\langle \mathsf P_{\ell,AA}z_A,z_A\right\rangle
        \ge
        \underline p_{\ell,A}d_\ell |z_A|^2,
        \qquad
        \|\mathsf P_{\ell,AB}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
        \le
        e^P_{\ell,AB}d_\ell
        \qquad(A\ne B),
    \]
    and
    \[
        \underline p_{\ell,A}
        -
        \sum_{B\ne A}e^P_{\ell,AB}
        \ge
        \tau_K
        \qquad\text{for every block }A.
    \]

    \item \emph{Endpoint absorption.}  The tilted nonquadratic absorption estimate
    of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate} holds for the
    lower-order and endpoint measures not represented by the block matrices.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the interval Gram package can be certified by
five scalar row-dominance inequalities for the Weyl blocks.  The remaining debt
is to compute the diagonal redshift weights and off-block couplings from the
null-frame deformation tensor.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Principal redshift-weight certificate]\label{def:PrincipalRedshiftWeightCertificate}
Fix the data of Definition~\ref{def:BlockDominantRedshiftNormalFormCertificate}.
A \emph{principal redshift-weight certificate} consists of the same finite
rational box cover and rational optical-frame matrices
$\mathsf H_\ell,\mathsf M_\ell,d_\ell,a_\ell$, together with interval bounds
for the raw redshift deformation tensor before subtracting the overlap margin.
On each box $B_\ell$ let
\[
    \lambda_\ell^+
    :=
    \kappa_K+\sigma_K+\sup_{B_\ell} a_\ell .
\]
The certificate requires:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Flux block audit.}  The flux matrix obeys the flux block
    dominance estimates in
    Definition~\ref{def:BlockDominantRedshiftNormalFormCertificate}, with
    constants $\underline h_{\ell,A}$, $\overline h_{\ell,A}$, and
    $e^H_{\ell,AB}$.

    \item \emph{Raw deformation diagonal weights.}  For every Weyl block
    $A\in\{\alpha,\beta,0,\underline\beta,\underline\alpha\}$ there is an
    interval lower bound $\underline m_{\ell,A}$ such that
    \[
        \left\langle \mathsf M_{\ell,AA}(u)z_A,z_A\right\rangle
        \ge
        \underline m_{\ell,A}d_\ell(u)|z_A|^2
        \qquad\text{on }B_\ell .
    \]

    \item \emph{Raw off-block couplings.}  For $A\ne B$ there are nonnegative
    interval bounds $e^M_{\ell,AB}=e^M_{\ell,BA}$ such that
    \[
        \|\mathsf M_{\ell,AB}(u)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
        \le
        e^M_{\ell,AB}d_\ell(u)
        \qquad\text{on }B_\ell .
    \]

    \item \emph{Principal redshift reserve.}  There is $\tau_K>0$ such that, for
    every block $A$ and every box $B_\ell$,
    \[
        \underline m_{\ell,A}
        -
        \lambda_\ell^+\overline h_{\ell,A}
        -
        \sum_{B\ne A}
        \left(
            e^M_{\ell,AB}
            +
            \lambda_\ell^+ e^H_{\ell,AB}
        \right)
        \ge
        \tau_K .
    \]

    \item \emph{Rational audit and endpoint absorption.}  The rational extraction,
    denominator lower bound, derivative audit, and tilted endpoint absorption
    clauses in Definition~\ref{def:BlockDominantRedshiftNormalFormCertificate}
    hold.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: one need not build the margin matrix first.  It is
enough to prove lower bounds for the raw diagonal deformation weights and upper
bounds for the raw off-block couplings; subtracting the overlap margin is then
an explicit scalar row computation.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Null-frame deformation component certificate]\label{def:NullFrameDeformationComponentCertificate}
Fix the data of Definition~\ref{def:PrincipalRedshiftWeightCertificate}.  On a
rational box $B_\ell$ let
\[
    \varpi_\ell(u)
    =
    \bigl(\varpi_{\ell,1}(u),\ldots,\varpi_{\ell,N_*}(u)\bigr)
\]
denote the finite list of normalized null-frame deformation components of the
redshift multiplier fields which enter the quadratic deformation term in the
Bel--Robinson divergence identity.  Concretely, the list is formed from the
frame components of the relevant deformation tensors, such as
$\pi_{LL}$, $\pi_{L\underline L}$, $\pi_{\underline L\underline L}$,
$\pi_{LA}$, $\pi_{\underline L A}$, $\hat\pi_{AB}$, and
$\tr_\gamma\pi_{AB}$, together with the corresponding normalized covector
components of $\xi$ and the compact gauge variables.  The chart carries a
\emph{null-frame deformation component certificate} if the following hold.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Exact component expansion.}  For every pair of optical Weyl
    blocks
    $A,B\in\{\alpha,\beta,0,\underline\beta,\underline\alpha\}$ there are
    universal symmetric block matrices
    $\Gamma_{\ell,r,AB}(u)$, rational in the compact optical-frame coordinates,
    such that the quadratic redshift deformation matrix has the exact
    block expansion
    \[
        \mathsf M_{\ell,AB}(u)
        =
        d_\ell(u)
        \sum_{r=1}^{N_*}
        \varpi_{\ell,r}(u)\Gamma_{\ell,r,AB}(u).
    \]
    The matrices $\Gamma_{\ell,r,AB}$ are the algebraic coefficients obtained
    by contracting the Bel--Robinson tensor with the normalized frame and the
    deformation tensors; no sign condition is imposed in this expansion.

    \item \emph{Component interval audit.}  Interval arithmetic on $B_\ell$
    gives lower bounds $\underline m^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,A}$ and upper bounds
    $e^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,AB}=e^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,BA}$ such that
    \[
        \sum_{r=1}^{N_*}
        \varpi_{\ell,r}(u)
        \left\langle
        \Gamma_{\ell,r,AA}(u)z_A,z_A
        \right\rangle
        \ge
        \underline m^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,A}|z_A|^2
    \]
    for all $u\in B_\ell$ and all $z_A\in\mathcal W_A$, and
    \[
        \left\|
        \sum_{r=1}^{N_*}
        \varpi_{\ell,r}(u)\Gamma_{\ell,r,AB}(u)
        \right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
        \le
        e^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,AB}
        \qquad(A\ne B)
    \]
    on $B_\ell$.  These bounds are computed before subtracting the overlap
    margin.

    \item \emph{Flux and overlap audit.}  The flux matrix $\mathsf H_\ell$,
    overlap density $a_\ell$, rational extraction, denominator lower bound,
    derivative audit, and tilted endpoint absorption are those required in
    Definition~\ref{def:PrincipalRedshiftWeightCertificate}.  Put
    \[
        \lambda_\ell^+
        :=
        \kappa_K+\sigma_K+\sup_{B_\ell}a_\ell .
    \]

    \item \emph{Component redshift reserve.}  There is $\tau_K>0$ such that,
    for every block $A$ and every box $B_\ell$,
    \[
        \underline m^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,A}
        -
        \lambda_\ell^+\overline h_{\ell,A}
        -
        \sum_{B\ne A}
        \left(
            e^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,AB}
            +
            \lambda_\ell^+ e^H_{\ell,AB}
        \right)
        \ge
        \tau_K .
    \]
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the principal redshift weights are no longer
primitive assumptions; they are produced from explicit null-frame deformation
components and universal Bel--Robinson algebra.  The remaining debt is the
geometric computation of the component intervals for a chosen redshift
multiplier and the tilted absorption of terms not represented in the quadratic
matrix.  Failure probe: the certificate fails if any compact optical box admits
a normalized Weyl block for which the component expansion loses the displayed
reserve after the overlap margin is subtracted.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Transport-generated redshift jet reserve]\label{def:TransportGeneratedRedshiftJetReserve}
Fix the data of Definition~\ref{def:NullFrameDeformationComponentCertificate}.
A \emph{transport-generated redshift jet reserve} on a compact retained optical
region consists of the following data.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Actual redshift multiplier fields.}  On each regular
    generator-saturated tube there is a redshift multiplier field
    \[
        X_\ell
        =
        f_\ell L+\underline f_\ell\,\underline L+Y_\ell^AE_A
    \]
    whose normalized null-frame one-jet
    $J_\ell=(X_\ell,\nabla X_\ell)$ is generated by transport along $L$ from
    the initial cut.  The transport equations for
    $f_\ell,\underline f_\ell,Y_\ell$, and their screen derivatives have
    coefficients in the compact optical state; their nonregular parts are
    assigned to the endpoint, defect, or horizon measures already allowed in
    the tilted absorption clause.

    \item \emph{Universal Killing-symbol extraction.}  There are universal
    rational maps $\mathsf K_{\ell,r}(u,J)$, depending only on the compact
    normalized optical state $u$ and the normalized one-jet $J$, such that the
    deformation components in
    Definition~\ref{def:NullFrameDeformationComponentCertificate} are
    \[
        \varpi_{\ell,r}(u)=\mathsf K_{\ell,r}(u,J_\ell(u)).
    \]
    Equivalently,
    \[
        \mathsf M_{\ell,AB}(u)
        =
        d_\ell(u)
        \sum_{r=1}^{N_*}
        \mathsf K_{\ell,r}(u,J_\ell(u))\Gamma_{\ell,r,AB}(u)
    \]
    is the exact Bel--Robinson deformation block produced by the multiplier
    $X_\ell$.

    \item \emph{Strict jet reserve.}  For each Weyl block put
    \[
        \mu_{\ell,A}(u)
        :=
        \lambda_{\min}
        \left(
        \sum_{r=1}^{N_*}
        \mathsf K_{\ell,r}(u,J_\ell(u))\Gamma_{\ell,r,AA}(u)
        \right)
    \]
    and, for $A\ne B$,
    \[
        \epsilon_{\ell,AB}(u)
        :=
        \left\|
        \sum_{r=1}^{N_*}
        \mathsf K_{\ell,r}(u,J_\ell(u))\Gamma_{\ell,r,AB}(u)
        \right\|_{\mathrm{op}} .
    \]
    There is $\tau_K>0$ such that, on the whole compact optical parameter set,
    \[
        \mu_{\ell,A}(u)
        -
        \lambda_\ell^+\overline h_{\ell,A}
        -
        \sum_{B\ne A}
        \left(
            \epsilon_{\ell,AB}(u)
            +
            \lambda_\ell^+ e^H_{\ell,AB}
        \right)
        \ge
        4\tau_K
    \]
    for every block $A$.

    \item \emph{Compact oscillation control.}  The functions
    $\mu_{\ell,A}$ and $\epsilon_{\ell,AB}$ are uniformly continuous on the
    compact optical state space, with moduli determined by the compact gauge,
    the transport coefficient bounds, and the rational Killing-symbol maps.
    The rational extraction, denominator lower bound, derivative audit, flux
    block audit, overlap bound, and tilted endpoint absorption clauses are those
    of Definition~\ref{def:NullFrameDeformationComponentCertificate}.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: instead of guessing finite deformation intervals,
one constructs an actual transported redshift multiplier whose one-jet has a
strict open reserve; compactness then turns the open reserve into the finite
component certificate.  The remaining debt is to build such a multiplier from
the optical structure equations and to prove the stated source/endpoint
absorption.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Redshift jet viability cone certificate]\label{def:RedshiftJetViabilityConeCertificate}
Fix the compact optical state space and the universal Killing-symbol maps of
Definition~\ref{def:TransportGeneratedRedshiftJetReserve}.  Let
$\mathcal J_K$ be the finite-dimensional space of normalized null-frame one-jets
$J=(X,\nabla X)$ for multiplier fields
$X=fL+\underline f\,\underline L+Y^AE_A$.  For $u$ in the compact optical state
space and $J\in\mathcal J_K$, define
\[
    \mu_{\ell,A}(u,J)
    :=
    \lambda_{\min}
    \left(
    \sum_{r=1}^{N_*}\mathsf K_{\ell,r}(u,J)\Gamma_{\ell,r,AA}(u)
    \right)
\]
and
\[
    \epsilon_{\ell,AB}(u,J)
    :=
    \left\|
    \sum_{r=1}^{N_*}\mathsf K_{\ell,r}(u,J)\Gamma_{\ell,r,AB}(u)
    \right\|_{\mathrm{op}}
    \qquad(A\ne B).
\]
Put
\[
    \mathcal R_{\ell,A}(u,J)
    :=
    \mu_{\ell,A}(u,J)
    -
    \lambda_\ell^+\overline h_{\ell,A}
    -
    \sum_{B\ne A}
    \left(
        \epsilon_{\ell,AB}(u,J)+\lambda_\ell^+e^H_{\ell,AB}
    \right).
\]
A \emph{redshift jet viability cone certificate} consists of the following
data.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Prolonged jet transport.}  On the regular part of every retained
    generator tube the compact optical state and multiplier one-jet obey a
    locally Lipschitz coupled transport system
    \[
        D_Lu=V_\ell(u),
        \qquad
        D_LJ=W_\ell(u,J),
    \]
    obtained by prolonging the chosen transport equations for
    $f,\underline f,Y$ and their screen derivatives.  The singular,
    smoothing, artificial-cap, defect, and horizon-terminal parts of the
    prolonged equations are assigned to the tilted endpoint absorption budget.

    \item \emph{Finite reserve cone.}  There is a compact fiberwise convex
    family
    \[
        \mathfrak C_\ell(u)
        =
        \{J\in\mathcal J_K:\Phi_{\ell,m}(u,J)\ge0,\ 1\le m\le M_\ell\},
    \]
    where the barrier functions $\Phi_{\ell,m}$ are rational in the compact
    coordinates and locally Lipschitz on the compact parameter set.
    Every jet in the cone has strict reserve:
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}(u,J)\ge4\tau_K
        \qquad
        \text{for all }J\in\mathfrak C_\ell(u)
        \text{ and all Weyl blocks }A .
    \]

    \item \emph{Initial viable seed.}  On each initial cut one can choose the
    normalized one-jet $J_{\ell,0}$ of the multiplier so that
    $J_{\ell,0}\in\mathfrak C_\ell(u_0)$, with every active barrier separated
    from zero except where it is forced by a prescribed symmetry or gauge
    normalization.

    \item \emph{Inward pointing boundary inequalities.}  At every regular
    boundary point of the moving cone, namely whenever
    $J\in\mathfrak C_\ell(u)$ and $\Phi_{\ell,m}(u,J)=0$, the upper Dini
    derivative of the active barrier along the prolonged transport field is
    nonnegative:
    \[
        D^+
        \Phi_{\ell,m}
        \bigl((V_\ell(u),W_\ell(u,J))\bigr)
        \ge0 .
    \]
    At nonsmooth intersections of several active barriers the same inequality
    holds for every active barrier after replacing $\Phi_{\ell,m}$ by any
    rational local supporting barrier defining the same face.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the construction of the redshift multiplier is
reduced to an invariant-cone problem for the null-frame one-jet ODE.  The
remaining debt is no longer a hidden positivity assertion; it is the production
of a cone $\mathfrak C_\ell$ and the verification of finitely many inward
pointing inequalities from the optical structure equations.  Failure probe:
the certificate fails precisely when the prolonged transport field points out
of every cone that keeps
$\mathcal R_{\ell,A}\ge4\tau_K$ for all Weyl blocks.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Interval-Farkas redshift barrier synthesis certificate]\label{def:IntervalFarkasRedshiftBarrierSynthesisCertificate}
Fix the data entering Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftJetViabilityConeCertificate}.
An \emph{interval-Farkas redshift barrier synthesis certificate} consists of a
finite rational box cover $Q_\ell$ of the compact optical state space, rational
candidate barriers
\[
    \Phi_{\ell,m}(u,J)\qquad(1\le m\le M_\ell),
\]
and a finite dictionary of rational atoms
\[
    \mathcal D_\ell=\{\Psi_{\ell,r}(u,J)\}_{r=1}^{N_\ell}
\]
with the following certified properties on each box.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Atom nonnegativity.}  Each atom is certified nonnegative on
    $Q_\ell$ by interval arithmetic, an interval Gram witness, or an interval
    Cholesky witness:
    \[
        \Psi_{\ell,r}(u,J)\ge0 .
    \]
    The dictionary includes the constant atom $1$ and the jet-size atoms needed
    to enforce a compact fiber bound $|J|\le C_K$ from finitely many barriers.

    \item \emph{Interval Farkas reserve arrays.}  For every Weyl block $A$
    there are interval coefficients
    \[
        \beta_{\ell,A,m}\ge0,\qquad
        \gamma_{\ell,A,r}\ge0,
    \]
    and a certified nonnegative interval remainder
    $\rho_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}\ge0$ such that, after clearing a positive
    rational denominator on $Q_\ell$,
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}(u,J)-4\tau_K
        =
        \sum_m\beta_{\ell,A,m}\Phi_{\ell,m}(u,J)
        +
        \sum_r\gamma_{\ell,A,r}\Psi_{\ell,r}(u,J)
        +
        \rho_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}(u,J).
    \]
    The interval signs are part of the certificate, so the equality is stable
    under the stated rational interval enclosures.

    \item \emph{Interval Farkas inward arrays.}  For every active face $m$ there
    are interval coefficients
    \[
        \delta_{\ell,mn}\ge0\quad(n\ne m),
        \qquad
        \eta_{\ell,mr}\ge0,
    \]
    and a certified nonnegative interval remainder
    $\rho_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}\ge0$ such that, after clearing a positive
    rational denominator and restricting to the face $\Phi_{\ell,m}=0$,
    \[
        D^+\Phi_{\ell,m}\bigl((V_\ell,W_\ell)\bigr)
        =
        \sum_{n\ne m}\delta_{\ell,mn}\Phi_{\ell,n}(u,J)
        +
        \sum_r\eta_{\ell,mr}\Psi_{\ell,r}(u,J)
        +
        \rho_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}(u,J).
    \]
    At nonsmooth intersections the same data are supplied for every active
    supporting barrier.

    \item \emph{Transport audit.}  The prolonged system
    $D_Lu=V_\ell(u)$, $D_LJ=W_\ell(u,J)$ is locally Lipschitz on the compact
    audited set, and its singular, smoothing, artificial-cap, defect, and
    horizon-terminal pieces are assigned to the tilted endpoint absorption
    budget.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the dual-barrier problem is reduced to a finite
interval-Farkas search.  A verifier only has to check denominator signs,
nonnegative atoms, nonnegative coefficient arrays, and exact rational
identities.  The remaining debt is to find such arrays from the prolonged
optical-frame equations.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Moving-box Bernstein redshift trap certificate]\label{def:MovingBoxBernsteinRedshiftTrapCertificate}
Fix the data entering
Definition~\ref{def:IntervalFarkasRedshiftBarrierSynthesisCertificate}.  A
\emph{moving-box Bernstein redshift trap certificate} is the following
stronger, but more directly searchable, finite package on each rational state
box $Q_\ell$.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Rational moving jet coordinates.}  There are rational maps
    \[
        z=\mathcal A_\ell(u)J+\mathcal B_\ell(u),
        \qquad
        J=\mathcal I_\ell(u,z),
    \]
    inverse to each other on the audited set, and a rational rectangle
    \[
        B_\ell=\prod_{a=1}^{d_\ell}[z_{\ell,a}^-,z_{\ell,a}^+].
    \]
    All denominators in $\mathcal A_\ell$, $\mathcal B_\ell$,
    $\mathcal I_\ell$, the reserve functions, and the transformed transport
    field are certified positive on $Q_\ell\times B_\ell$.  The transformed
    jet equation is
    \[
        D_Lz=\widehat W_\ell(u,z)
        :=
        D_u(\mathcal A_\ell(u)J+\mathcal B_\ell(u))V_\ell(u)
        +\mathcal A_\ell(u)W_\ell(u,J)
        \quad\text{with }J=\mathcal I_\ell(u,z),
    \]
    and is locally Lipschitz on the audited box.

    \item \emph{Side barriers.}  The candidate barriers are the sides of the
    moving rectangle,
    \[
        \Phi_{\ell,a,-}:=z^a-z_{\ell,a}^-,
        \qquad
        \Phi_{\ell,a,+}:=z_{\ell,a}^+-z^a .
    \]
    The inverse-coordinate audit gives a uniform jet bound
    $|J|\le C_K$ on $Q_\ell\times B_\ell$, and the initial multiplier jet is
    carried into $B_\ell$ on the initial cut, with strict separation from all
    nonforced sides.

    \item \emph{Bernstein atom dictionary.}  After affine rescaling of
    $Q_\ell\times B_\ell$ to the unit cube, the atom dictionary contains the
    tensor Bernstein atoms
    \[
        \mathfrak B_{\alpha,n}(y)
        =
        \prod_i \binom{n_i}{\alpha_i}
        y_i^{\alpha_i}(1-y_i)^{n_i-\alpha_i},
        \qquad 0\le\alpha_i\le n_i,
    \]
    for the finitely many multi-degrees used below, pulled back to rational
    functions of $(u,J)$ by the moving coordinate map.  It also contains any
    interval Gram or Cholesky atoms used to certify denominator signs or
    structural square terms.  Every listed atom is certified nonnegative on
    the audited box.

    \item \emph{Reserve Bernstein margins.}  For every Weyl block $A$, after
    clearing a certified positive denominator, the exact rational numerator of
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}\bigl(u,\mathcal I_\ell(u,z)\bigr)-4\tau_K
    \]
    has a Bernstein expansion on $Q_\ell\times B_\ell$ whose rational
    coefficient intervals have nonnegative lower endpoints.

    \item \emph{Inward Bernstein margins.}  On the lower side
    $z^a=z_{\ell,a}^-$, after clearing a certified positive denominator, the
    exact numerator of $\widehat W_\ell^a(u,z)$ has a Bernstein expansion on
    that face with nonnegative coefficient intervals.  On the upper side
    $z^a=z_{\ell,a}^+$, the same statement holds for
    $-\widehat W_\ell^a(u,z)$.  The corresponding face restrictions are
    supplied for every side participating in a nonsmooth corner.

    \item \emph{Endpoint audit.}  Singular, smoothing, artificial-cap, defect,
    and horizon-terminal terms in the prolonged equation are assigned to the
    same tilted endpoint absorption budget as in
    Definition~\ref{def:IntervalFarkasRedshiftBarrierSynthesisCertificate}.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: instead of guessing an arbitrary viability cone,
one searches for rational coordinates in which the multiplier one-jet is
trapped in a moving rectangle.  Bernstein positivity then turns reserve and
inward-pointing estimates into exact finite algebra.  The remaining debt is to
find the moving coordinates and boxes from the optical-frame equations; the
definition does not assume the Penrose inequality or a global area comparison.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Strict Bernstein elevation]\label{lem:StrictBernsteinElevation}
Let $P=N/D$ be a rational function on a rational compact box $Q$, with $N$ and
$D$ polynomials with rational coefficients after affine rescaling of $Q$ to the
unit cube.  Assume $D>0$ on $Q$ and $P\ge m>0$ on $Q$.  Then, after clearing the
positive denominator $D$, there is a finite tensor Bernstein degree for which
the numerator $N$ has an exact Bernstein expansion with all coefficients
positive rational numbers.  The same assertion holds on any rational face of
$Q$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
After rescaling $Q$ to $[0,1]^d$, the positivity assumptions give
$N\ge mD$.  Since $D$ is positive and continuous on the compact cube, $N$ has a
strict positive minimum.  For a polynomial, the Bernstein coefficients of its
degree-$n$ tensor Bernstein representation converge uniformly, as
$n\to\infty$, to the values of the polynomial on the corresponding Bernstein
grid.  Choosing $n$ large enough makes every coefficient positive.  The
coefficients are rational because the polynomial coefficients and the box
endpoints are rational.  Restricting to a rational face gives the same argument
in one lower dimension.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Riccati-shadow redshift trap certificate]\label{def:RiccatiShadowRedshiftTrapCertificate}
Fix the data entering
Definition~\ref{def:MovingBoxBernsteinRedshiftTrapCertificate}.  A
\emph{Riccati-shadow redshift trap certificate} consists of the following
finite rational data on each state box $Q_\ell$.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Raw moving coordinates, centers, and radii.}  There are
    rational raw jet coordinates
    \[
        q=\mathcal A_\ell(u)J+\mathcal B_\ell(u),
        \qquad
        J=\mathcal I_\ell^q(u,q),
    \]
    a rational center $c_\ell(u)$, and positive rational radii
    $r_{\ell,a}(u)$ such that the normalized coordinates
    \[
        z^a=\frac{q^a-c_\ell^a(u)}{r_{\ell,a}(u)}
    \]
    are defined on the audited set.  The target box is
    $B_\ell=[-1,1]^{d_\ell}$.  All denominators in these coordinate maps,
    centers, radii, and inverses are audited positive.

    \item \emph{Raw transformed transport.}  In the raw coordinates,
    \[
        D_Lq=F_\ell(u,q),
        \qquad q=c_\ell(u)+r_\ell(u)z,
    \]
    with $F_\ell$ rational and locally Lipschitz on
    $Q_\ell\times B_\ell$ after the same denominator audit.

    \item \emph{Diagonal redshift shadow.}  For every coordinate $a$ there are
    rational functions
    $\nu_{\ell,a}\ge0$, $S_{\ell,a}\ge0$, and a strict margin
    $m_{\ell,a}>0$ such that
    \[
        G_{\ell,a}(u,z)
        :=
        F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell+r_\ell z)
        -D_uc_\ell^a(u)V_\ell(u)
        +\nu_{\ell,a}(u)r_{\ell,a}(u)z^a
    \]
    obeys the two-sided residual bounds
    \[
        S_{\ell,a}-G_{\ell,a}\ge0,
        \qquad
        S_{\ell,a}+G_{\ell,a}\ge0,
    \]
    and the radius domination inequality
    \[
        D_ur_{\ell,a}(u)V_\ell(u)
        +\nu_{\ell,a}(u)r_{\ell,a}(u)
        -S_{\ell,a}(u,z)-m_{\ell,a}\ge0
    \]
    on $Q_\ell\times B_\ell$.

    \item \emph{Reserve shadow.}  Put
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,q)
        :=
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}(u,\mathcal I_\ell^q(u,q)).
    \]
    For every Weyl block $A$ there are rational derivative majorants
    $P_{\ell,A,a}(u)\ge0$, depending only on the state coordinate $u$, and a
    strict margin $m_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}>0$
    such that, on $Q_\ell\times B_\ell$,
    \[
        P_{\ell,A,a}(u)
        -\partial_{q^a}\mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,c_\ell+r_\ell z)\ge0,
        \qquad
        P_{\ell,A,a}(u)
        +\partial_{q^a}\mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,c_\ell+r_\ell z)\ge0,
    \]
    and, on $Q_\ell$,
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,c_\ell(u))-4\tau_K
        -\sum_a P_{\ell,A,a}(u)r_{\ell,a}(u)
        -m_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}\ge0 .
    \]

    \item \emph{Finite positivity audit.}  After clearing certified positive
    denominators, every displayed nonnegative quantity above has a Bernstein,
    interval Gram, or interval Cholesky witness, and the strict margins have
    rational positive lower bounds on their boxes.  The singular, smoothing,
    artificial-cap, defect, and horizon-terminal pieces of the prolonged
    transport are assigned to the tilted endpoint absorption budget.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the moving-box search is reduced to a
center-radius Riccati comparison problem.  The redshift sink
$\nu_{\ell,a}$ must dominate the residual drift of each normalized one-jet
coordinate, while the reserve shadow asks only for a center reserve and
derivative majorants.  The remaining debt is to construct these centers,
radii, sinks, and majorants from the optical null-frame equations.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Redshift-dominated one-jet linearization certificate]\label{def:RedshiftDominatedOneJetLinearizationCertificate}
Fix the data entering
Definition~\ref{def:RiccatiShadowRedshiftTrapCertificate}.  A
\emph{redshift-dominated one-jet linearization certificate} is the following
finite analytic package on each rational state box $Q_\ell$.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Raw coordinates, center, and radii.}  There are rational raw
    jet coordinates
    \[
        q=\mathcal A_\ell(u)J+\mathcal B_\ell(u),
        \qquad J=\mathcal I_\ell^q(u,q),
    \]
    a rational center $c_\ell(u)$, and positive rational radii
    $r_{\ell,a}(u)$.  The normalized box is
    $q=c_\ell(u)+r_\ell(u)z$, $z\in[-1,1]^{d_\ell}$, and all denominators are
    audited positive.

    \item \emph{Centered one-jet expansion.}  In the raw coordinates the
    prolonged transport is $D_Lq=F_\ell(u,q)$, and for each coordinate $a$
    there is an exact rational identity on $Q_\ell\times[-1,1]^{d_\ell}$:
    \[
        F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell+r_\ell z)
        -D_uc_\ell^a(u)V_\ell(u)
        =
        H_{\ell,a}(u)
        +
        \sum_b A_{\ell,ab}(u)r_{\ell,b}(u)z^b
        +
        N_{\ell,a}(u,z).
    \]
    The functions $H_{\ell,a}$ are center residuals, $A_{\ell,ab}$ are the
    audited linear one-jet couplings, and $N_{\ell,a}$ is the nonlinear
    Taylor remainder.

    \item \emph{Diagonal redshift dominance.}  The diagonal coefficients are
    redshift sinks:
    \[
        -A_{\ell,aa}(u)\ge \kappa_{\ell,a}(u)>0 .
    \]
    There are rational nonnegative majorants $B_{\ell,a}(u)$,
    $C_{\ell,ab}(u)$ for $b\ne a$, and $E_{\ell,a}(u,z)$ such that
    \[
        B_{\ell,a}\pm H_{\ell,a}\ge0,\qquad
        C_{\ell,ab}\pm A_{\ell,ab}\ge0,\qquad
        E_{\ell,a}\pm N_{\ell,a}\ge0 .
    \]
    For a strict margin $m_{\ell,a}>0$,
    \[
        D_ur_{\ell,a}(u)V_\ell(u)
        -A_{\ell,aa}(u)r_{\ell,a}(u)
        -B_{\ell,a}(u)
        -\sum_{b\ne a}C_{\ell,ab}(u)r_{\ell,b}(u)
        -E_{\ell,a}(u,z)
        -m_{\ell,a}
        \ge0
    \]
    on the whole normalized box.

    \item \emph{Reserve linearization margin.}  For every Weyl block $A$,
    with
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,q)
        :=
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}(u,\mathcal I_\ell^q(u,q)),
    \]
    there are state-only rational derivative majorants
    $P_{\ell,A,a}(u)\ge0$ and a strict margin
    $m_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}>0$ such that
    \[
        P_{\ell,A,a}(u)
        \pm
        \partial_{q^a}\mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,c_\ell+r_\ell z)\ge0
    \]
    on $Q_\ell\times[-1,1]^{d_\ell}$, and
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,c_\ell(u))-4\tau_K
        -\sum_aP_{\ell,A,a}(u)r_{\ell,a}(u)
        -m_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}\ge0
    \]
    on $Q_\ell$.

    \item \emph{Finite positivity audit.}  After clearing certified positive
    denominators, every displayed nonnegative quantity above has a Bernstein,
    interval Gram, or interval Cholesky witness, with strict rational positive
    lower bounds for $\kappa_{\ell,a}$ and the margins.  Singular, smoothing,
    artificial-cap, defect, and horizon-terminal pieces are assigned to the
    tilted endpoint absorption budget.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the hard one-jet trapping problem is reduced to a
redshift-dominated perturbative estimate.  A diagonal sink in the prolonged
null-frame ODE must beat the center residual, off-diagonal couplings, nonlinear
Taylor remainder, and radius variation.  This is an analytic target rather than
a purely algebraic search, and it is falsifiable by a single failed dominance
inequality.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Taylor-dominated optical redshift normal form]\label{def:TaylorDominatedOpticalRedshiftNormalForm}
Fix the data entering
Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDominatedOneJetLinearizationCertificate}.  A
\emph{Taylor-dominated optical redshift normal form} is the following finite
package on each rational state box $Q_\ell$.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Optical one-jet coordinates.}  There are rational raw one-jet
    coordinates
    \[
        q=\mathcal A_\ell(u)J+\mathcal B_\ell(u),
        \qquad J=\mathcal I_\ell^q(u,q),
    \]
    a rational center $c_\ell(u)$, and positive rational radii
    $r_{\ell,a}(u)$.  In these coordinates the prolonged optical one-jet
    equation is $D_Lq=F_\ell(u,q)$, with $F_\ell$ rational and $C^2$ on the
    audited tube
    \[
        \mathcal U_\ell
        :=
        \{(u,q):u\in Q_\ell,\ q=c_\ell(u)+r_\ell(u)z,\ |z^a|\le1\}.
    \]
    All denominators in the coordinate maps, $F_\ell$, its first two
    $q$-derivatives, the reserve functions, and the reserve first
    $q$-derivatives are certified positive on $\mathcal U_\ell$.

    \item \emph{Linearized redshift matrix.}  Define
    \[
        H_{\ell,a}(u):=
        F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell(u))-D_uc_\ell^a(u)V_\ell(u),
        \qquad
        A_{\ell,ab}(u):=
        \partial_{q^b}F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell(u)).
    \]
    The diagonal entries have a redshift gap
    \[
        -A_{\ell,aa}(u)\ge\kappa_{\ell,a}(u)>0 .
    \]
    There are rational nonnegative majorants $B_{\ell,a}(u)$ and
    $C_{\ell,ab}(u)$, $b\ne a$, such that
    \[
        B_{\ell,a}\pm H_{\ell,a}\ge0,
        \qquad
        C_{\ell,ab}\pm A_{\ell,ab}\ge0 .
    \]

    \item \emph{Quadratic Taylor remainder budget.}  There are rational
    nonnegative Hessian majorants $M_{\ell,a,bc}(u)$ and strict slack
    $\sigma_{\ell,a}>0$ such that, on $\mathcal U_\ell$,
    \[
        M_{\ell,a,bc}(u)
        \pm
        \partial_{q^b}\partial_{q^c}F_\ell^a(u,q)\ge0 .
    \]
    Set
    \[
        E_{\ell,a}(u)
        :=
        \sigma_{\ell,a}
        +
        \frac12
        \sum_{b,c}
        M_{\ell,a,bc}(u)r_{\ell,b}(u)r_{\ell,c}(u).
    \]
    For a strict margin $m_{\ell,a}>0$, the scalar dominance inequality
    \[
        D_ur_{\ell,a}(u)V_\ell(u)
        -A_{\ell,aa}(u)r_{\ell,a}(u)
        -B_{\ell,a}(u)
        -\sum_{b\ne a}C_{\ell,ab}(u)r_{\ell,b}(u)
        -E_{\ell,a}(u)
        -m_{\ell,a}
        \ge0
    \]
    holds on $Q_\ell$.

    \item \emph{Reserve Taylor margin.}  For every Weyl block $A$, put
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,q)
        :=
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}(u,\mathcal I_\ell^q(u,q)).
    \]
    There are state-only rational derivative majorants
    $P_{\ell,A,a}(u)\ge0$ and a strict margin
    $m_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}>0$ such that, on $\mathcal U_\ell$,
    \[
        P_{\ell,A,a}(u)
        \pm
        \partial_{q^a}\mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,q)\ge0,
    \]
    and, on $Q_\ell$,
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,c_\ell(u))-4\tau_K
        -\sum_aP_{\ell,A,a}(u)r_{\ell,a}(u)
        -m_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}\ge0 .
    \]

    \item \emph{Finite positivity audit.}  After clearing certified positive
    denominators, all displayed nonnegative quantities above have Bernstein,
    interval Gram, or interval Cholesky witnesses, and the redshift gaps,
    Taylor slacks, and margins have rational positive lower bounds.  Singular,
    smoothing, artificial-cap, defect, and horizon-terminal pieces are assigned
    to the tilted endpoint absorption budget.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the remaining redshift construction is reduced to
the standard analytic data of a commuted optical ODE: its center residual, its
linearized matrix, and a Hessian bound for its Taylor remainder.  The decisive
quantity is the diagonal redshift gap
$-A_{\ell,aa}$, which must dominate the measured off-diagonal and quadratic
optical couplings.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Commuted null-frame redshift spectral package]\label{def:CommutedNullFrameRedshiftSpectralPackage}
Fix the data entering
Definition~\ref{def:TaylorDominatedOpticalRedshiftNormalForm}.  A
\emph{commuted null-frame redshift spectral package} is the following
finite package on each rational state box $Q_\ell$.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Commuted optical closure.}  In a compact normalized null frame,
    the retained optical state $u$ and the redshift multiplier one-jet
    coordinates
    \[
        q=\mathcal A_\ell(u)J+\mathcal B_\ell(u),
        \qquad J=\mathcal I_\ell^q(u,q),
    \]
    obey the regular commuted transport system
    \[
        D_Lu=V_\ell(u),
        \qquad
        D_Lq=F_\ell(u,q).
    \]
    The maps $V_\ell$, $F_\ell$, the coordinate maps, a rational center
    $c_\ell(u)$, and positive rational radii $r_{\ell,a}(u)$ are obtained from
    the null-frame structure equations and their first commutations.  The map
    $F_\ell$ is rational and $C^2$ in $q$ on the audited tube
    \[
        \mathcal U_\ell
        =
        \{(u,q):u\in Q_\ell,\ q=c_\ell(u)+r_\ell(u)z,\ |z^a|\le1\}.
    \]
    Singular, smoothing, artificial-cap, defect, and horizon-terminal pieces
    of the commuted equations are assigned to the tilted endpoint absorption
    budget.

    \item \emph{Redshift spectral splitting.}  For each one-jet coordinate
    $a$, the diagonal linearized coefficient at the center has an exact
    commuted null-frame decomposition
    \[
        \partial_{q^a}F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell(u))
        =
        -\Omega_{\ell,a}(u)+\Pi_{\ell,a}(u).
    \]
    There are rational nonnegative functions
    $\omega_{\ell,a}$, $\pi_{\ell,a}$, and
    $\kappa_{\ell,a}$ such that
    \[
        \Omega_{\ell,a}-\omega_{\ell,a}\ge0,
        \qquad
        \pi_{\ell,a}\pm\Pi_{\ell,a}\ge0,
        \qquad
        \omega_{\ell,a}-\pi_{\ell,a}-\kappa_{\ell,a}\ge0,
        \qquad
        \kappa_{\ell,a}>0 .
    \]
    For $b\ne a$ there are rational nonnegative off-diagonal majorants
    $C_{\ell,ab}(u)$ with
    \[
        C_{\ell,ab}(u)
        \pm
        \partial_{q^b}F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell(u))
        \ge0 .
    \]

    \item \emph{Commuted center-source budget.}  There are rational
    nonnegative functions $B_{\ell,a}(u)$ such that
    \[
        B_{\ell,a}(u)
        \pm
        \bigl(
        F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell(u))-D_uc_\ell^a(u)V_\ell(u)
        \bigr)
        \ge0 .
    \]
    These are the retained regular source terms after all nonregular pieces
    have been charged to the endpoint budget.

    \item \emph{Second-commuted remainder and reserve gradients.}  There are
    rational nonnegative Hessian majorants $M_{\ell,a,bc}(u)$ and strict
    rational slacks $\sigma_{\ell,a}>0$ such that, on $\mathcal U_\ell$,
    \[
        M_{\ell,a,bc}(u)
        \pm
        \partial_{q^b}\partial_{q^c}F_\ell^a(u,q)
        \ge0 .
    \]
    For every Weyl block $A$, with
    $\mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,q)
    =\mathcal R_{\ell,A}(u,\mathcal I_\ell^q(u,q))$, there are
    state-only rational derivative majorants $P_{\ell,A,a}(u)\ge0$ and a
    strict margin $m_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}>0$ such that
    \[
        P_{\ell,A,a}(u)
        \pm
        \partial_{q^a}\mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,q)
        \ge0
        \quad\text{on }\mathcal U_\ell,
    \]
    and
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,c_\ell(u))-4\tau_K
        -\sum_aP_{\ell,A,a}(u)r_{\ell,a}(u)
        -m_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}\ge0
        \quad\text{on }Q_\ell .
    \]

    \item \emph{Spectral dominance closure.}  Set
    \[
        E_{\ell,a}(u)
        :=
        \sigma_{\ell,a}
        +
        \frac12
        \sum_{b,c}
        M_{\ell,a,bc}(u)r_{\ell,b}(u)r_{\ell,c}(u).
    \]
    For a strict margin $m_{\ell,a}>0$,
    \[
        D_ur_{\ell,a}(u)V_\ell(u)
        +
        \bigl(\omega_{\ell,a}(u)-\pi_{\ell,a}(u)\bigr)r_{\ell,a}(u)
        -B_{\ell,a}(u)
        -\sum_{b\ne a}C_{\ell,ab}(u)r_{\ell,b}(u)
        -E_{\ell,a}(u)
        -m_{\ell,a}
        \ge0
    \]
    holds on $Q_\ell$.

    \item \emph{Finite positivity audit.}  After clearing certified positive
    denominators, every displayed nonnegative quantity above has a Bernstein,
    interval Gram, or interval Cholesky witness.  The denominator audit covers
    the null-frame normalization, the coordinate maps and inverses, $F_\ell$,
    its first two $q$-derivatives, the reserve functions, and the reserve
    first $q$-derivatives.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the redshift one-jet construction is no longer a
black-box Taylor estimate.  The commuted null-frame equations must exhibit a
principal redshift frequency $\Omega_{\ell,a}$ whose gap survives the
perturbative commutator diagonal $\Pi_{\ell,a}$, while the off-diagonal,
source, Hessian, and reserve-gradient terms are measured separately.  The
remaining debt is the concrete null-frame computation of these quantities in
the chosen compact optical gauge.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Commuted redshift spectrum gives Taylor normal form]\label{thm:CommutedNullFrameSpectralPackageGivesTaylorNormalForm}
If a retained compact exterior region admits commuted null-frame redshift
spectral packages in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:CommutedNullFrameRedshiftSpectralPackage}, then it admits
Taylor-dominated optical redshift normal forms in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:TaylorDominatedOpticalRedshiftNormalForm}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Use the same compact state boxes, one-jet coordinates, center, radii, and
regular commuted transport field $F_\ell$.  The commuted optical closure gives
the $C^2$ rational ODE and the denominator audit required in the first clause
of Definition~\ref{def:TaylorDominatedOpticalRedshiftNormalForm}.

Define
\[
    H_{\ell,a}
    :=
    F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell)-D_uc_\ell^aV_\ell,
    \qquad
    A_{\ell,ab}
    :=
    \partial_{q^b}F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell).
\]
The center-source budget gives $B_{\ell,a}\pm H_{\ell,a}\ge0$.  For
$b\ne a$, the off-diagonal part of the spectral package gives
$C_{\ell,ab}\pm A_{\ell,ab}\ge0$.  On the diagonal,
\[
    -A_{\ell,aa}
    =
    \Omega_{\ell,a}-\Pi_{\ell,a}
    \ge
    \omega_{\ell,a}-\pi_{\ell,a}
    \ge
    \kappa_{\ell,a}>0,
\]
which is exactly the Taylor-normal-form redshift gap.

The second-commuted remainder clause supplies the Hessian majorants
$M_{\ell,a,bc}$ and the Taylor slack $\sigma_{\ell,a}$.  The reserve-gradient
part of the same clause is word-for-word the reserve Taylor margin.  Finally,
since
\[
    -A_{\ell,aa}r_{\ell,a}
    \ge
    (\omega_{\ell,a}-\pi_{\ell,a})r_{\ell,a},
\]
the spectral dominance closure implies the scalar dominance inequality in
Definition~\ref{def:TaylorDominatedOpticalRedshiftNormalForm}.  The finite
positivity and endpoint audits are included in the spectral package.  Hence all
Taylor-normal-form clauses hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Commuted-spectral local route to Penrose]\label{cor:CommutedSpectralLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that
every retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration
data, a defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination,
generator-saturated optical flow boxes, and commuted null-frame redshift
spectral packages for all one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then
every closed trapped surface satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:CommutedNullFrameSpectralPackageGivesTaylorNormalForm} gives
Taylor-dominated optical redshift normal forms.  Corollary~\ref{cor:TaylorNormalFormLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Taylor normal form gives redshift-dominated linearization]\label{thm:TaylorNormalFormGivesRedshiftDominatedLinearization}
If a retained compact exterior region admits Taylor-dominated optical redshift
normal forms in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:TaylorDominatedOpticalRedshiftNormalForm}, then it admits
redshift-dominated one-jet linearization certificates in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDominatedOneJetLinearizationCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Use the same coordinates, center, radii, raw transport field, center residuals,
and linearized matrix:
\[
    H_{\ell,a}=F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell)-D_uc_\ell^aV_\ell,
    \qquad
    A_{\ell,ab}=\partial_{q^b}F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell).
\]
For $q=c_\ell+r_\ell z$, define the exact rational Taylor residual
\[
    N_{\ell,a}(u,z)
    :=
    F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell+r_\ell z)
    -F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell)
    -
    \sum_bA_{\ell,ab}(u)r_{\ell,b}(u)z^b .
\]
Then the centered one-jet expansion required in
Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDominatedOneJetLinearizationCertificate} is an
identity.

Taylor's theorem along the segment
$c_\ell+s r_\ell z$, $0\le s\le1$, gives
\[
    N_{\ell,a}(u,z)
    =
    \int_0^1(1-s)
    \sum_{b,c}
    \partial_{q^b}\partial_{q^c}F_\ell^a
    (u,c_\ell+s r_\ell z)
    r_{\ell,b}r_{\ell,c}z^bz^c\,ds .
\]
Since $|z^b|\le1$ and
$|\partial_{q^b}\partial_{q^c}F_\ell^a|\le M_{\ell,a,bc}$ on the audited tube,
\[
    |N_{\ell,a}(u,z)|
    \le
    \frac12\sum_{b,c}
    M_{\ell,a,bc}r_{\ell,b}r_{\ell,c}
    <
    E_{\ell,a}(u).
\]
Thus $E_{\ell,a}\pm N_{\ell,a}$ are strictly positive on the normalized box.
Together with the signed bounds for $H_{\ell,a}$ and $A_{\ell,ab}$, the
diagonal gap and scalar dominance inequality are exactly the diagonal redshift
dominance clause of
Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDominatedOneJetLinearizationCertificate}.

The reserve Taylor margin is identical to the reserve linearization margin.
The strict positivity of $E_{\ell,a}\pm N_{\ell,a}$, the denominator audits,
and Lemma~\ref{lem:StrictBernsteinElevation} supply finite Bernstein witnesses
for the Taylor residual majorants; the remaining finite witnesses are included
in the Taylor-normal-form audit.  The endpoint audit is unchanged.  Hence all
clauses of the redshift-dominated one-jet linearization certificate hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Taylor-normal-form local route to Penrose]\label{cor:TaylorNormalFormLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that
every retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration
data, a defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination,
generator-saturated optical flow boxes, and Taylor-dominated optical redshift
normal forms for all one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then every
closed trapped surface satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:TaylorNormalFormGivesRedshiftDominatedLinearization} gives the
redshift-dominated one-jet linearization certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:DominatedLinearizationLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Redshift-dominated linearization gives Riccati shadows]\label{thm:RedshiftDominatedLinearizationGivesRiccatiShadow}
If a retained compact exterior region admits redshift-dominated one-jet
linearization certificates in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDominatedOneJetLinearizationCertificate}, then it
admits Riccati-shadow redshift trap certificates in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:RiccatiShadowRedshiftTrapCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Use the same raw coordinates $q$, center $c_\ell$, radii $r_{\ell,a}$, and raw
transport field $F_\ell$.  Set
\[
    \nu_{\ell,a}:=-A_{\ell,aa},
    \qquad
    S_{\ell,a}
    :=
    B_{\ell,a}
    +\sum_{b\ne a}C_{\ell,ab}r_{\ell,b}
    +E_{\ell,a}.
\]
The diagonal redshift clause gives $\nu_{\ell,a}\ge\kappa_{\ell,a}>0$, and the
signed majorants give $S_{\ell,a}\ge0$.

For the Riccati residual in
Definition~\ref{def:RiccatiShadowRedshiftTrapCertificate}, the centered
expansion gives
\[
    G_{\ell,a}
    =
    H_{\ell,a}
    +\sum_{b\ne a}A_{\ell,ab}r_{\ell,b}z^b
    +N_{\ell,a},
\]
because the diagonal term $A_{\ell,aa}r_{\ell,a}z^a$ is exactly cancelled by
$\nu_{\ell,a}r_{\ell,a}z^a$.  Since $|z^b|\le1$, the signed majorants imply
\[
    -S_{\ell,a}\le G_{\ell,a}\le S_{\ell,a},
\]
which is the two-sided residual bound in the Riccati-shadow certificate.
The displayed redshift dominance inequality is exactly
\[
    D_ur_{\ell,a}V_\ell
    +\nu_{\ell,a}r_{\ell,a}
    -S_{\ell,a}
    -m_{\ell,a}
    \ge0 .
\]
Thus the diagonal redshift shadow clause holds.

The reserve linearization margin is word-for-word the reserve shadow clause,
with the same state-only derivative majorants $P_{\ell,A,a}$ and center
reserve margin.  The coordinate, denominator, positivity, and endpoint audits
are also identical to the corresponding Riccati-shadow clauses.  Hence all
requirements of
Definition~\ref{def:RiccatiShadowRedshiftTrapCertificate} are satisfied.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Dominated-linearization local route to Penrose]\label{cor:DominatedLinearizationLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that
every retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration
data, a defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination,
generator-saturated optical flow boxes, and redshift-dominated one-jet
linearization certificates for all one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps.
Then every closed trapped surface satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:RedshiftDominatedLinearizationGivesRiccatiShadow} gives the
Riccati-shadow redshift trap certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:RiccatiShadowLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Riccati shadows generate moving boxes]\label{thm:RiccatiShadowGivesMovingBox}
If a retained compact exterior region admits Riccati-shadow redshift trap
certificates in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:RiccatiShadowRedshiftTrapCertificate}, then it admits
moving-box Bernstein redshift trap certificates in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:MovingBoxBernsteinRedshiftTrapCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Use the normalized coordinates
$z^a=(q^a-c_\ell^a)/r_{\ell,a}$ and the box $B_\ell=[-1,1]^{d_\ell}$.
The coordinate and denominator audits give the rational moving-coordinate and
compact-fiber clauses of
Definition~\ref{def:MovingBoxBernsteinRedshiftTrapCertificate}.

We first prove the side-inward inequalities.  In normalized coordinates,
\[
    D_Lz^a
    =
    \frac{
        F_\ell^a(u,c_\ell+r_\ell z)
        -D_uc_\ell^aV_\ell
        -z^aD_ur_{\ell,a}V_\ell
    }{r_{\ell,a}} .
\]
On the lower side $z^a=-1$ this gives
\[
    D_L(z^a+1)
    =
    \frac{
        \nu_{\ell,a}r_{\ell,a}
        +G_{\ell,a}
        +D_ur_{\ell,a}V_\ell
    }{r_{\ell,a}}
    \ge
    \frac{
        \nu_{\ell,a}r_{\ell,a}
        -S_{\ell,a}
        +D_ur_{\ell,a}V_\ell
    }{r_{\ell,a}}
    \ge
    \frac{m_{\ell,a}}{r_{\ell,a}}>0 .
\]
On the upper side $z^a=1$,
\[
    D_L(1-z^a)
    =
    \frac{
        \nu_{\ell,a}r_{\ell,a}
        -G_{\ell,a}
        +D_ur_{\ell,a}V_\ell
    }{r_{\ell,a}}
    \ge
    \frac{m_{\ell,a}}{r_{\ell,a}}>0 .
\]
Thus every side of the normalized rectangle is strictly inward-pointing.

Next fix a Weyl block $A$.  Along the segment
$q_s=c_\ell+s r_\ell z$, the fundamental theorem of calculus and the derivative
majorants give
\[
    \left|
    \mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,c_\ell+r_\ell z)
    -\mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,c_\ell)
    \right|
    \le
    \sum_a P_{\ell,A,a}(u)r_{\ell,a}.
\]
The reserve shadow therefore implies
\[
    \mathcal R_{\ell,A}^q(u,c_\ell+r_\ell z)-4\tau_K
    \ge
    m_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}>0
\]
on the whole box.

The reserve and side-inward quantities are rational functions with audited
positive denominators and strict positive lower bounds on compact rational
boxes or rational faces.  Lemma~\ref{lem:StrictBernsteinElevation} supplies a
finite Bernstein degree for each cleared numerator with positive rational
coefficients.  These elevated Bernstein expansions are exactly the reserve and
inward Bernstein-margin clauses in
Definition~\ref{def:MovingBoxBernsteinRedshiftTrapCertificate}.  The endpoint
audit is part of the Riccati-shadow certificate, so all moving-box clauses
hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Riccati-shadow local route to Penrose]\label{cor:RiccatiShadowLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that
every retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration
data, a defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination,
generator-saturated optical flow boxes, and Riccati-shadow redshift trap
certificates for all one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then every
closed trapped surface satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:RiccatiShadowGivesMovingBox} gives the moving-box Bernstein
redshift trap certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:MovingBoxLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Moving boxes generate interval-Farkas barriers]\label{thm:MovingBoxBernsteinGivesIntervalFarkas}
If a retained compact exterior region admits moving-box Bernstein redshift trap
certificates in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:MovingBoxBernsteinRedshiftTrapCertificate}, then it admits
interval-Farkas redshift barrier synthesis certificates in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:IntervalFarkasRedshiftBarrierSynthesisCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Use the side functions
$\Phi_{\ell,a,-}=z^a-z_{\ell,a}^-$ and
$\Phi_{\ell,a,+}=z_{\ell,a}^+-z^a$ as the rational barriers.  The
inverse-coordinate audit gives the compact fiber bound and the initial seed
condition.  The denominator and endpoint audits are exactly the denominator and
transport audits required in the interval-Farkas certificate.

The Bernstein atom dictionary supplies rational atoms which are nonnegative on
the audited box.  For a reserve term, clear the certified positive denominator.
By the reserve Bernstein-margin clause the exact numerator has the form
\[
    D_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}
    \left(
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}
        (u,\mathcal I_\ell(u,z))-4\tau_K
    \right)
    =
    \sum_\alpha c_{\ell,A,\alpha}\mathfrak B_{\alpha,n}(u,z)
\]
with all $c_{\ell,A,\alpha}\ge0$.  Pulling the atoms back from $(u,z)$ to
$(u,J)$ gives the reserve interval-Farkas identity with all barrier
coefficients equal to zero, atom coefficients
$\gamma_{\ell,A,\alpha}=c_{\ell,A,\alpha}$, and zero remainder.

For a lower side,
$D^+\Phi_{\ell,a,-}((V_\ell,W_\ell))=\widehat W_\ell^a$ on the face
$z^a=z_{\ell,a}^-$.  For an upper side,
$D^+\Phi_{\ell,a,+}((V_\ell,W_\ell))=-\widehat W_\ell^a$ on the face
$z^a=z_{\ell,a}^+$.  The inward Bernstein-margin clause therefore gives,
after clearing a positive denominator on the face, exact identities
\[
    D_{\ell,a,\pm}^{\mathrm{in}}
    D^+\Phi_{\ell,a,\pm}((V_\ell,W_\ell))
    =
    \sum_\alpha d_{\ell,a,\pm,\alpha}
    \mathfrak B_{\alpha,n}^{\mathrm{face}}(u,z),
    \qquad d_{\ell,a,\pm,\alpha}\ge0 .
\]
These are precisely the interval-Farkas inward identities, again with zero
inactive-barrier coefficients and zero remainder.  At a corner of the moving
rectangle the argument is applied to each active side separately, which is the
supporting-barrier convention in
Definition~\ref{def:IntervalFarkasRedshiftBarrierSynthesisCertificate}.  Hence
all clauses of the interval-Farkas certificate hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Moving-box local route to Penrose]\label{cor:MovingBoxLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that
every retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration
data, a defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination,
generator-saturated optical flow boxes, and moving-box Bernstein redshift trap
certificates for all one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then every
closed trapped surface satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:MovingBoxBernsteinGivesIntervalFarkas} gives the
interval-Farkas redshift barrier synthesis certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:IntervalFarkasLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Interval Farkas barriers give dual-barrier synthesis]\label{thm:IntervalFarkasGivesDualBarrierSynthesis}
If a retained compact exterior region admits an interval-Farkas redshift
barrier synthesis certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:IntervalFarkasRedshiftBarrierSynthesisCertificate}, then it
admits a dual-barrier redshift cone synthesis certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:DualBarrierRedshiftConeSynthesisCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Use the same rational barriers $\Phi_{\ell,m}$ to define
\[
    \mathfrak C_\ell(u)=\{J:\Phi_{\ell,m}(u,J)\ge0\ \text{for all }m\}.
\]
The atom and jet-size clauses give the compact fiber witness and the initial
barrier audit.  The transport audit is exactly the transport and endpoint audit
required in Definition~\ref{def:DualBarrierRedshiftConeSynthesisCertificate}.

For the reserve clause, set
\[
    b_{\ell,A,m}:=\beta_{\ell,A,m},
    \qquad
    S_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}
    :=
    \sum_r\gamma_{\ell,A,r}\Psi_{\ell,r},
    \qquad
    E_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}
    :=
    \rho_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}} .
\]
The coefficients $\gamma_{\ell,A,r}$ and atoms $\Psi_{\ell,r}$ are
nonnegative, so $S_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}$ is certified nonnegative by the
same interval atom witnesses, after grouping Gram or Cholesky atoms if needed.
The interval-Farkas reserve identity is therefore exactly the reserve dual
multiplier identity of
Definition~\ref{def:DualBarrierRedshiftConeSynthesisCertificate}.

For an active face $m$, set
\[
    c_{\ell,mn}:=\delta_{\ell,mn},
    \qquad
    S_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}
    :=
    \sum_r\eta_{\ell,mr}\Psi_{\ell,r},
    \qquad
    E_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}
    :=
    \rho_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}} .
\]
Again the coefficient signs and atom certificates prove
$S_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}\ge0$, and the interval-Farkas inward identity is the
face-inward dual identity.  The supporting-barrier clause handles nonsmooth
intersections.  Thus all clauses of the dual-barrier synthesis certificate
hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Interval-Farkas local route to Penrose]\label{cor:IntervalFarkasLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that every
retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration data, a
defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination, generator-saturated
optical flow boxes, and interval-Farkas redshift barrier synthesis certificates
for all one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then every closed trapped
surface satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:IntervalFarkasGivesDualBarrierSynthesis} gives the
dual-barrier redshift cone synthesis certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:DualBarrierLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Dual-barrier redshift cone synthesis certificate]\label{def:DualBarrierRedshiftConeSynthesisCertificate}
Fix the data entering Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftJetViabilityConeCertificate}.
A \emph{dual-barrier redshift cone synthesis certificate} consists of a finite
rational box cover of the compact optical state space and, on each box
$Q_\ell$, rational barrier functions
\[
    \Phi_{\ell,m}(u,J)\qquad(1\le m\le M_\ell)
\]
defining a compact fiberwise convex candidate cone
\[
    \mathfrak C_\ell(u)
    =
    \{J:\Phi_{\ell,m}(u,J)\ge0,\ 1\le m\le M_\ell\},
\]
together with the following dual algebra witnesses.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Compact fiber witness.}  Among the barriers are rational upper
    and lower jet-size barriers which imply $|J|\le C_K$ on
    $\mathfrak C_\ell(u)$, uniformly for $u\in Q_\ell$.  The initial jet
    $J_{\ell,0}$ satisfies all barriers on the initial cut.

    \item \emph{Reserve dual multipliers.}  For every Weyl block $A$ there are
    nonnegative rational multiplier functions
    $b_{\ell,A,m}(u,J)\ge0$ and a nonnegative interval remainder
    $E_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}\ge0$ on the ambient box such that
    \[
        \mathcal R_{\ell,A}(u,J)-4\tau_K
        =
        S_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}(u,J)
        +
        \sum_{m=1}^{M_\ell}
        b_{\ell,A,m}(u,J)\Phi_{\ell,m}(u,J)
        +
        E_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}(u,J),
    \]
    where $S_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}$ is certified nonnegative by an interval
    Gram, interval Cholesky, or sum-of-squares witness on $Q_\ell$.

    \item \emph{Face-inward dual multipliers.}  For every face $m$ there are
    nonnegative rational multiplier functions
