    $c_{\ell,mn}(u,J)\ge0$ for $n\ne m$ and a nonnegative interval remainder
    $E_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}\ge0$ such that, on the face
    $\Phi_{\ell,m}=0$,
    \[
        D^+\Phi_{\ell,m}\bigl((V_\ell,W_\ell)\bigr)
        =
        S_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}(u,J)
        +
        \sum_{n\ne m}
        c_{\ell,mn}(u,J)\Phi_{\ell,n}(u,J)
        +
        E_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}(u,J),
    \]
    with $S_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}$ certified nonnegative on $Q_\ell$ by the
    same interval algebra.  At a nonsmooth face intersection the identity is
    supplied for each active supporting barrier.

    \item \emph{Transport and endpoint audit.}  The prolonged transport system
    $D_Lu=V_\ell(u)$, $D_LJ=W_\ell(u,J)$ is locally Lipschitz on the compact
    audited set, and its singular, smoothing, cap, defect, and
    horizon-terminal parts are assigned to the tilted endpoint absorption
    budget.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: the viability cone is no longer guessed; it is
certified by a finite dual algebra package.  Reserve positivity and inward
pointing reduce to nonnegative-multiplier identities plus interval
Gram/Cholesky witnesses.  The remaining debt is to produce these rational
barriers and dual identities from the prolonged optical-frame equations.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Dual barriers synthesize a redshift viability cone]\label{thm:DualBarrierSynthesisGivesViabilityCone}
If a retained compact exterior region admits a dual-barrier redshift cone
synthesis certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:DualBarrierRedshiftConeSynthesisCertificate}, then it
admits a redshift jet viability cone certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftJetViabilityConeCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The compact fiber witness gives a compact fiberwise convex cone
$\mathfrak C_\ell(u)$ and supplies the initial viable seed.  The prolonged
transport and endpoint audit is exactly the transport clause of
Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftJetViabilityConeCertificate}.

It remains to prove the strict reserve and inward pointing clauses.  If
$J\in\mathfrak C_\ell(u)$, then all $\Phi_{\ell,m}(u,J)$ are nonnegative.  The
reserve dual identity gives
\[
    \mathcal R_{\ell,A}(u,J)-4\tau_K
    =
    S_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}
    +
    \sum_m b_{\ell,A,m}\Phi_{\ell,m}
    +
    E_{\ell,A}^{\mathrm{res}}
    \ge0
\]
because each summand on the right is nonnegative.  Hence
$\mathcal R_{\ell,A}\ge4\tau_K$ on the cone for every Weyl block.

Now let $J\in\mathfrak C_\ell(u)$ lie on the active face
$\Phi_{\ell,m}=0$.  The face-inward dual identity gives
\[
    D^+\Phi_{\ell,m}\bigl((V_\ell,W_\ell)\bigr)
    =
    S_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}
    +
    \sum_{n\ne m}c_{\ell,mn}\Phi_{\ell,n}
    +
    E_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}
    \ge0,
\]
again because $S_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{in}}$, the multipliers, the inactive
barriers, and the interval remainder are nonnegative.  The same argument
applies to each active supporting barrier at a nonsmooth intersection.  Thus
all inward pointing boundary inequalities required in
Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftJetViabilityConeCertificate} hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Dual-barrier local route to Penrose]\label{cor:DualBarrierLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that every
retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration data, a
defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination, generator-saturated
optical flow boxes, and dual-barrier redshift cone synthesis certificates for
all one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then every closed trapped
surface satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:DualBarrierSynthesisGivesViabilityCone} gives the redshift jet
viability cone certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:ViabilityConeLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Viability cones produce transported redshift reserves]\label{thm:ViabilityConeProducesTransportedRedshiftReserve}
If a retained compact exterior region admits a redshift jet viability cone
certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftJetViabilityConeCertificate}, then it admits a
transport-generated redshift jet reserve in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:TransportGeneratedRedshiftJetReserve}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Solve the prolonged transport system
\[
    D_Lu=V_\ell(u),\qquad D_LJ=W_\ell(u,J)
\]
from the viable initial seed.  Local existence and uniqueness hold on the
regular part because the vector field is locally Lipschitz on the compact
parameter set; the compactness of the fibers $\mathfrak C_\ell(u)$ prevents
escape of the normalized one-jet as long as the solution remains viable.

We prove viability by the standard first-exit argument.  Suppose that along a
regular generator there is a first time $s_*$ at which
$J(s_*)$ leaves the moving cone.  Then for some active barrier
$\Phi_{\ell,m}$ one has
\[
    \Phi_{\ell,m}(u(s_*),J(s_*))=0
\]
and the upper Dini derivative of this barrier along the solution is negative.
This contradicts the inward pointing boundary inequality.  At a corner of the
cone the same argument is applied to an active rational supporting barrier.
Therefore the transported jet remains in
$\mathfrak C_\ell(u(s))$ for the whole regular portion of the tube.

The strict reserve clause of the cone now gives
\[
    \mathcal R_{\ell,A}(u(s),J(s))\ge4\tau_K
\]
for every block $A$ and every regular parameter value $s$.  Unwinding the
definition of $\mathcal R_{\ell,A}$ gives exactly the strict jet reserve clause
of Definition~\ref{def:TransportGeneratedRedshiftJetReserve}, with
$J_\ell=J$.  The universal Killing-symbol extraction is part of the viability
data, and compact oscillation control follows from compactness and local
Lipschitz continuity of the rational maps, the barriers, and the prolonged
transport field.  The singular, smoothing, cap, defect, and horizon-terminal
pieces were assigned to the tilted endpoint absorption budget in the prolonged
transport clause.  Hence all clauses of
Definition~\ref{def:TransportGeneratedRedshiftJetReserve} hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Viability-cone local route to Penrose]\label{cor:ViabilityConeLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that every
retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration data, a
defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination, generator-saturated
optical flow boxes, and redshift jet viability cone certificates for all
one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then every closed trapped surface
satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:ViabilityConeProducesTransportedRedshiftReserve} gives the
transport-generated redshift jet reserves.  Corollary~\ref{cor:TransportedRedshiftLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Transported redshift jets give component certificates]\label{thm:TransportedRedshiftJetsGiveComponentCertificate}
If a retained compact exterior region admits a transport-generated redshift jet
reserve in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:TransportGeneratedRedshiftJetReserve},
then, after a finite rational subdivision of the compact optical parameter set,
the one-chart Bel--Robinson flux carries a null-frame deformation component
certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:NullFrameDeformationComponentCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The exact component expansion in
Definition~\ref{def:NullFrameDeformationComponentCertificate} is the universal
Killing-symbol extraction of
Definition~\ref{def:TransportGeneratedRedshiftJetReserve}, with
$\varpi_{\ell,r}=\mathsf K_{\ell,r}(u,J_\ell(u))$.  The rational extraction,
denominator control, derivative audit, flux audit, overlap bound, and endpoint
absorption clauses are inherited from the reserve definition.  It remains to
produce finite component interval bounds with a positive reserve.

Because the compact optical parameter set is compact and the functions
$\mu_{\ell,A}$ and $\epsilon_{\ell,AB}$ are uniformly continuous, choose a
finite rational subdivision by boxes $B_{\ell,q}$ so fine that the oscillation
of every $\mu_{\ell,A}$ and $\epsilon_{\ell,AB}$ on each box is at most
$\tau_K/(1+N_b)$, where
$N_b=|\{\alpha,\beta,0,\underline\beta,\underline\alpha\}|-1=4$.  On such a
box set
\[
    \underline m^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,q,A}
    :=
    \inf_{u\in B_{\ell,q}}\mu_{\ell,A}(u),
    \qquad
    e^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,q,AB}
    :=
    \sup_{u\in B_{\ell,q}}\epsilon_{\ell,AB}(u).
\]
The definitions of $\mu_{\ell,A}$ and $\epsilon_{\ell,AB}$ give exactly the
diagonal lower bound and off-block operator-norm bound required in the
component interval audit.  Moreover the strict jet reserve gives, on each box,
\[
    \underline m^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,q,A}
    -
    \lambda_\ell^+\overline h_{\ell,A}
    -
    \sum_{B\ne A}
    \left(
        e^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,q,AB}
        +
        \lambda_\ell^+e^H_{\ell,AB}
    \right)
    \ge
    \tau_K ,
\]
after decreasing the reserve by the total allowed oscillation.  This is the
component redshift reserve of
Definition~\ref{def:NullFrameDeformationComponentCertificate}.  Hence all
clauses of that certificate hold on the finite subdivision.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Transported-redshift local route to Penrose]\label{cor:TransportedRedshiftLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that every
retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration data, a
defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination, generator-saturated
optical flow boxes, and transport-generated redshift jet reserves for all
one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then every closed trapped surface
satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:TransportedRedshiftJetsGiveComponentCertificate} gives the
null-frame deformation component certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:ComponentDeformationLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Deformation components give principal redshift weights]\label{thm:DeformationComponentsGivePrincipalWeights}
If the one-chart Bel--Robinson flux $F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}$ carries a
null-frame deformation component certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:NullFrameDeformationComponentCertificate}, then it carries
a principal redshift-weight certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:PrincipalRedshiftWeightCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The flux block audit, rational extraction, denominator control, derivative
audit, overlap bound, and tilted endpoint absorption are common to the two
certificates.  We prove the raw deformation bounds required in
Definition~\ref{def:PrincipalRedshiftWeightCertificate}.

For a diagonal block $A$, the exact component expansion gives
\[
\begin{split}
    \left\langle \mathsf M_{\ell,AA}(u)z_A,z_A\right\rangle
    &=
    d_\ell(u)
    \sum_{r=1}^{N_*}
    \varpi_{\ell,r}(u)
    \left\langle
    \Gamma_{\ell,r,AA}(u)z_A,z_A
    \right\rangle  \\
    &\ge
    \underline m^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,A}d_\ell(u)|z_A|^2 .
\end{split}
\]
Thus the principal certificate may take
$\underline m_{\ell,A}:=\underline m^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,A}$.  For
$A\ne B$ the same expansion gives
\[
    \|\mathsf M_{\ell,AB}(u)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
    \le
    d_\ell(u)e^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,AB},
\]
so set $e^M_{\ell,AB}:=e^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\ell,AB}$.  The component redshift
reserve is exactly the principal redshift reserve with these choices.  Hence
all clauses of Definition~\ref{def:PrincipalRedshiftWeightCertificate} hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Component-deformation local route to Penrose]\label{cor:ComponentDeformationLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that every
retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration data, a
defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination, generator-saturated
optical flow boxes, and null-frame deformation component certificates for all
one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then every closed trapped surface
satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:DeformationComponentsGivePrincipalWeights} gives the principal
redshift-weight certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:PrincipalRedshiftLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Principal redshift weights give block dominance]\label{thm:PrincipalRedshiftWeightsGiveBlockDominance}
If the one-chart Bel--Robinson flux $F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}$ carries a principal
redshift-weight certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:PrincipalRedshiftWeightCertificate}, then it carries a
block-dominant redshift normal-form certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:BlockDominantRedshiftNormalFormCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The rational extraction, denominator, derivative, endpoint-absorption, and flux
block dominance clauses are identical to the corresponding clauses of
Definition~\ref{def:BlockDominantRedshiftNormalFormCertificate}.  We only need
to prove redshift margin block dominance for
\[
    \mathsf P_\ell
    =
    \mathsf M_\ell
    -
    (\kappa_K+a_\ell+\sigma_K)\mathsf H_\ell .
\]
Since $\kappa_K+a_\ell+\sigma_K\le\lambda_\ell^+$ on $B_\ell$, the diagonal
block satisfies
\[
\begin{split}
    \left\langle \mathsf P_{\ell,AA}z_A,z_A\right\rangle
    &=
    \left\langle \mathsf M_{\ell,AA}z_A,z_A\right\rangle
    -
    (\kappa_K+a_\ell+\sigma_K)
    \left\langle \mathsf H_{\ell,AA}z_A,z_A\right\rangle \\
    &\ge
    \left(
        \underline m_{\ell,A}
        -
        \lambda_\ell^+\overline h_{\ell,A}
    \right)d_\ell |z_A|^2 .
\end{split}
\]
Thus one may take
\[
    \underline p_{\ell,A}
    :=
    \underline m_{\ell,A}-\lambda_\ell^+\overline h_{\ell,A}.
\]
For $A\ne B$,
\[
    \|\mathsf P_{\ell,AB}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
    \le
    \|\mathsf M_{\ell,AB}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
    +
    (\kappa_K+a_\ell+\sigma_K)\|\mathsf H_{\ell,AB}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
    \le
    \left(e^M_{\ell,AB}+\lambda_\ell^+e^H_{\ell,AB}\right)d_\ell .
\]
Hence set
\[
    e^P_{\ell,AB}
    :=
    e^M_{\ell,AB}+\lambda_\ell^+e^H_{\ell,AB}.
\]
The principal redshift reserve is exactly
\[
    \underline p_{\ell,A}-\sum_{B\ne A}e^P_{\ell,AB}\ge\tau_K,
\]
which is the redshift margin block-dominance clause.  All clauses of
Definition~\ref{def:BlockDominantRedshiftNormalFormCertificate} follow.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Principal-redshift local route to Penrose]\label{cor:PrincipalRedshiftLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that every
retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration data, a
defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination, generator-saturated
optical flow boxes, and principal redshift-weight certificates for all one-chart
Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then every closed trapped surface satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:PrincipalRedshiftWeightsGiveBlockDominance} gives the
block-dominant redshift normal-form certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:BlockDominantLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Block dominance gives the interval Gram redshift certificate]\label{thm:BlockDominanceIntervalGramCertificate}
If the one-chart Bel--Robinson flux $F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}$ carries a
block-dominant redshift normal-form certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:BlockDominantRedshiftNormalFormCertificate}, then it carries
an interval Gram redshift matrix certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:IntervalGramRedshiftMatrixCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The rational extraction, denominator control, derivative audit, and tilted
endpoint absorption clauses are common to the two certificates.  It remains only
to replace block dominance by the positivity witnesses required in
Definition~\ref{def:IntervalGramRedshiftMatrixCertificate}.

For the flux form write $Z=\sum_A Z_A$ according to the optical Weyl block
decomposition.  The block estimates give
\[
\begin{split}
    \left\langle \mathsf H_\ell Z,Z\right\rangle
    &\ge
    d_\ell\sum_A\underline h_{\ell,A}|Z_A|^2
    -
    2d_\ell\sum_{A<B}e^H_{\ell,AB}|Z_A|\,|Z_B|  \\
    &\ge
    d_\ell\sum_A
    \left(
        \underline h_{\ell,A}-\sum_{B\ne A}e^H_{\ell,AB}
    \right)|Z_A|^2
    \ge
    c_Hd_\ell |Z|^2 .
\end{split}
\]
The same estimate with the signs reversed gives
\[
    \left\langle \mathsf H_\ell Z,Z\right\rangle
    \le
    C_Hd_\ell |Z|^2.
\]
Thus the flux lower and upper bounds are certified by the block-Gershgorin
alternative allowed in Definition~\ref{def:IntervalGramRedshiftMatrixCertificate}.

For the redshift margin matrix $\mathsf P_\ell$ the same calculation gives
\[
\begin{split}
    \left\langle \mathsf P_\ell Z,Z\right\rangle
    &\ge
    d_\ell\sum_A
    \left(
        \underline p_{\ell,A}-\sum_{B\ne A}e^P_{\ell,AB}
    \right)|Z_A|^2  \\
    &\ge
    \tau_K d_\ell |Z|^2
    \ge 0 .
\end{split}
\]
This is an interval block-Gershgorin certificate for
$\mathsf P_\ell(u)\succeq0$ on the whole box.  Since the reserve $\tau_K$ is
strict and the boxes are compact, a finite subdivision supplies an interval
Cholesky witness if one wants the positivity in Cholesky form; no new analytic
input is used in that subdivision.  Hence the redshift margin Gram-positivity
clause of Definition~\ref{def:IntervalGramRedshiftMatrixCertificate} holds.
All clauses of the interval Gram certificate are therefore verified.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Block-dominant local route to Penrose]\label{cor:BlockDominantLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that every
retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration data, a
defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination, generator-saturated
optical flow boxes, and block-dominant redshift normal-form certificates for all
one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then every closed trapped surface
satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:BlockDominanceIntervalGramCertificate} gives the interval Gram
redshift matrix certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:IntervalGramLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Interval Gram certificate gives finite compact-cone matrix margin]\label{thm:IntervalGramRedshiftMatrixCertificateFiniteCone}
If the one-chart Bel--Robinson flux $F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}$ carries an interval
Gram redshift matrix certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:IntervalGramRedshiftMatrixCertificate}, then it carries a
finite compact-cone redshift matrix certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:FiniteCompactConeRedshiftMatrixCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The extracted rational frame algebra gives the quadratic optical-frame
representation required in Definition~\ref{def:FiniteCompactConeRedshiftMatrixCertificate}.
The denominator bound $d_\ell\ge d_{0,K}>0$ makes the rational representation
uniform on the compact parameter set, so no pole can create an uncharged
singular contribution.

The flux Gram positivity clause gives
\[
    \left\langle \mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)Z,Z\right\rangle
    \ge
    c'_H |Z|^2
\]
after lowering $c_H$ to absorb the interval remainder; the certified upper
bound gives the corresponding $C_H$ inequality.  Thus compact flux equivalence
holds.  The derivative audit gives the Lipschitz constant $L_K$ for the
normalized margin form.

It remains to produce the finite robust spectral margin.  On each box the Gram
identity for $\mathsf P_\ell$ and the interval remainder bound imply
\[
    \mathsf M_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}(p)
    -
    (\kappa_K+a(p))\mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)
    \succeq
    \sigma_K\mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)
\]
for every $p\in\Phi_\ell(B_\ell)$.  Since finitely many boxes cover
$\mathfrak P_K$, this inequality holds on all of $\mathfrak P_K$.  Compactness
now gives a finite $\eta_K$-net $\mathcal N_K$ with
$\eta_K<\sigma_K/(2L_K)$.  Restricting the uniform inequality to this net gives
exactly the finite spectral margin clause of
Definition~\ref{def:FiniteCompactConeRedshiftMatrixCertificate}.

The tilted nonquadratic absorption clause is included verbatim in the interval
Gram certificate.  Hence all clauses of the finite compact-cone redshift matrix
certificate hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Interval Gram local route to Penrose]\label{cor:IntervalGramLocalRoutePenrose}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume that every
retained compact exterior region admits local master optical calibration data, a
defect-exact transition gauge, compact synchronized states, primitive
compact-gauge structure equations, source-defect domination, generator-saturated
optical flow boxes, and interval Gram redshift matrix certificates for all
one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes on overlaps. Then every closed trapped surface
satisfies
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}} .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:IntervalGramRedshiftMatrixCertificateFiniteCone} gives the
finite compact-cone redshift matrix certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:CompactConeMatrixMarginLocalRoutePenrose}
then gives the conditional Penrose conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Finite compact-cone matrix margin gives deformation margin]\label{thm:FiniteCompactConeMatrixMarginDeformationMargin}
If the one-chart Bel--Robinson flux $F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}$ carries a finite
compact-cone redshift matrix certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:FiniteCompactConeRedshiftMatrixCertificate}, then it carries
a redshift deformation-margin certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
First work on a smooth regularized tube, so that $A_{\varepsilon,ij}$ is
absolutely continuous along the retained generators.  For
$p\in\mathfrak P_K$ choose $q\in\mathcal N_K$ with $d(p,q)\le\eta_K$.  By the
Lipschitz clause for the normalized forms and the finite spectral margin,
\[
\begin{split}
    &\left\langle
    \left(
        \mathsf M_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}(p)
        -
        (\kappa_K+a(p))\mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)
    \right)Z,Z
    \right\rangle  \\
    &\qquad\ge
    (\sigma_K-L_K\eta_K)
    \left\langle
    \mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)Z,Z
    \right\rangle
    \ge
    \frac{\sigma_K}{2}
    \left\langle
    \mathsf H_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(p)Z,Z
    \right\rangle .
\end{split}
\]
Substituting $Z=Z_\varepsilon$ and using the quadratic representation gives the
pointwise regular-current inequality
\[
    d\mathcal R^{\mathrm{reg}}_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    \ge
    (\kappa_K+a_{\varepsilon,ij})
    F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}\,d\lambda\,d\nu_\varepsilon
    +
    \frac{\sigma_K}{2}F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}\,d\lambda\,d\nu_\varepsilon .
\]
Define the disposable surplus on the regular part by
\[
    d\mathcal S_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}^{\mathrm{reg}}
    :=
    \frac{\sigma_K}{2}F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}\,d\lambda\,d\nu_\varepsilon .
\]
The positive singular part of $D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij}$ and the singular
redshift-current concentration are, by the certificate definition, assigned to
the endpoint or defect/horizon measures; any nonnegative singular redshift
current may be added to $\mathcal S_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}$.  Therefore the
distributional inequality
\[
    \mathcal R_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    \ge
    \left(\kappa_K+D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij}\right)
    F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    +
    \mathcal S_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
\]
holds as a Radon-measure inequality on the retained tube, after the named
endpoint accounting.

Expanding the Bel--Robinson divergence identity in the optical frame gives
\[
    -D_LF_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    \ge
    \mathcal R_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    -
    \mathcal L_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    -
    \mathcal B_{\varepsilon,i,\xi},
\]
where the nonquadratic pieces are precisely the lower-order and boundary
measures from the certificate.  The tilted absorption clause is the second
clause of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate}, while the
compact flux equivalence gives its compact comparability clause.  Passing from
smooth regularizations to the retained BV limit uses weak lower semicontinuity
of nonnegative Radon measures and the stability of finite-dimensional
positive-semidefinite inequalities under uniform convergence of the compact
optical-frame coefficients.  Hence all clauses of
Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate} hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Deformation margin produces redshift surplus]\label{thm:DeformationMarginProducesRedshiftSurplus}
If the one-chart Bel--Robinson flux $F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}$ carries a redshift deformation-margin certificate in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate}, then it carries an overlap-tilted redshift surplus certificate in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:OverlapTiltedRedshiftSurplusCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Define the redshift surplus measure and the lower-order error by
\[
    \mathfrak r_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    :=
    \mathcal R_{\varepsilon,i,\xi},
    \qquad
    \mathfrak e^{\mathrm{low}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    :=
    \mathcal L_{\varepsilon,i,\xi},
    \qquad
    \mathcal E^{\mathrm{bd}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    :=
    \mathcal B_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}.
\]
The disintegrated Bel--Robinson balance in Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate} gives
\[
    -D_LF_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    \ge
    \mathfrak r_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    -
    \mathfrak e^{\mathrm{low}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    -
    \mathcal E^{\mathrm{bd}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}.
\]
Moving the nonnegative lower-order measure to the error side weakens this to the untilted balance required in Definition~\ref{def:OverlapTiltedRedshiftSurplusCertificate}.  Equivalently, after integrating on a generator-saturated tube, the resulting error is bounded by the sum of the lower-order and boundary terms.

The deformation-margin clause gives
\[
    \mathfrak r_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    =
    \mathcal R_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    \ge
    \left(\kappa_K+D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij}\right)
    F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    +
    \mathcal S_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    \ge
    \left(\kappa_K+D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij}\right)
    F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    -
    \mathfrak e^{\mathrm{low}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi},
\]
which is the surplus-over-tilt clause.  Finally, the tilted lower-order absorption clause in Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate} is exactly the tilted error absorption clause in Definition~\ref{def:OverlapTiltedRedshiftSurplusCertificate}, after the harmless relabeling above.  Compact comparability ensures that no change of Bel--Robinson current or multiplier normalization changes the constants except by the fixed compact-gauge factor already absorbed into $\kappa_K$ and $\mathcal E^{\mathrm{end}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}$.  Hence all clauses of Definition~\ref{def:OverlapTiltedRedshiftSurplusCertificate} hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Deformation-margin route to strict redshift]\label{cor:DeformationMarginStrictRedshiftRoute}
Assume the retained regular optical cover admits generator-saturated flow boxes.  If every one-chart Bel--Robinson flux on every overlap carries a redshift deformation-margin certificate, then every overlap carries a transported strict redshift flux certificate.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:DeformationMarginProducesRedshiftSurplus} gives overlap-tilted redshift surplus certificates.  Theorem~\ref{thm:RedshiftSurplusAbsorbsOverlapTilt} gives the tube redshift inequalities.  Theorem~\ref{thm:FlowBoxTubeRedshiftStrictCertificate} then gives transported strict redshift flux certificates on every overlap.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Compact-cone matrix-margin route to strict redshift]\label{cor:CompactConeMatrixMarginStrictRedshiftRoute}
Assume the retained regular optical cover admits generator-saturated flow boxes.
If every one-chart Bel--Robinson flux on every overlap carries a finite
compact-cone redshift matrix certificate, then every overlap carries a
transported strict redshift flux certificate.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:FiniteCompactConeMatrixMarginDeformationMargin} gives the
redshift deformation-margin certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:DeformationMarginStrictRedshiftRoute}
then gives transported strict redshift flux certificates on every overlap.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Redshift surplus absorbs the overlap tilt]\label{thm:RedshiftSurplusAbsorbsOverlapTilt}
If $F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}$ carries an overlap-tilted redshift surplus certificate in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:OverlapTiltedRedshiftSurplusCertificate}, then it satisfies the tube redshift inequality required in Theorem~\ref{thm:TubeRedshiftDisintegrationStrictFlux}.  More precisely,
\[
\begin{split}
    &\int_B e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}(t,y)}
    F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(t,y)\,d\nu_\varepsilon(y)
    +
    \kappa_K
    \int_s^t\int_B
    e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}
    F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}\,d\nu_\varepsilon d\lambda\\
    &\qquad\le
    \int_B e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}(s,y)}
    F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}(s,y)\,d\nu_\varepsilon(y)
    +
    \mathcal E^{\mathrm{end}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}([s,t]\times B).
\end{split}
\]
Consequently the generatorwise strict one-chart decay in Definition~\ref{def:TransportedStrictRedshiftFluxCertificate} follows away from the named defect and horizon-terminal strata.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
First argue for smooth approximants.  The untilted balance gives
\[
    -D_LF_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    \ge
    \mathfrak r_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    -
    \mathcal E^{\mathrm{bd}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}.
\]
Using the product rule for the tilted flux,
\[
    -D_L\!\left(e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}\right)
    =
    e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}
    \left(
        -D_LF_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
        -
        (D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij})F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    \right),
\]
and substituting the surplus inequality gives
\[
\begin{split}
    -D_L\!\left(e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}\right)
    &\ge
    e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}
    \left(
        \mathfrak r_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
        -
        (D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij})F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    \right)
    -
    e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}\mathcal E^{\mathrm{bd}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}\\
    &\ge
    \kappa_K e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}
    -
    e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}\mathfrak e^{\mathrm{low}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    -
    e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}\mathcal E^{\mathrm{bd}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}.
\end{split}
\]
Integrating this inequality over the saturated tube $[s,t]\times B$ gives the displayed tube estimate after applying the tilted error-absorption clause.

For BV/Radon limits, apply the smooth inequality to regularized approximants.  The left side is lower semicontinuous for nonnegative flux measures, while $A_{\varepsilon,ij}$ is uniformly bounded on the compact optical gauge region.  The weak limit of the tilted lower-order and boundary errors is, by definition, part of
$\mathcal E^{\mathrm{end}}_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}$ and is charged to
$\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)$.  Thus the same tube inequality survives in the limiting regular construction.  The final generatorwise strict decay is then exactly Theorem~\ref{thm:TubeRedshiftDisintegrationStrictFlux}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Flow-box tube redshift produces strict overlap certificates]\label{thm:FlowBoxTubeRedshiftStrictCertificate}
On a retained compact exterior region $K$, assume that the local optical cover admits the generator-saturated flow-box refinement of Lemma~\ref{lem:FlowBoxTransportedOverlapPartitions}.  Assume also that, for every overlap $U_i\cap U_j$, every $\ell\in\{i,j\}$, and every future causal covector $\xi$, the one-chart flux
\[
    F_{\varepsilon,\ell,\xi}
    =
    \rho_{\varepsilon,\ell}
    Q[W_\varepsilon](\xi,T_{\varepsilon,\ell},N_{\varepsilon,\ell},N_{\varepsilon,\ell})
\]
satisfies the tube redshift inequality of Theorem~\ref{thm:TubeRedshiftDisintegrationStrictFlux} with the overlap coefficient $A_{\varepsilon,ij}$ and a uniform constant $\kappa_K>0$.  Then every overlap carries a transported strict redshift flux certificate in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:TransportedStrictRedshiftFluxCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Choose the partition of unity supplied by Lemma~\ref{lem:FlowBoxTransportedOverlapPartitions}.  Its overlap factors satisfy
\[
    -D_L(\psi_i\psi_j)\ge0
\]
as Radon measures, after assigning any terminal cutoff contribution to $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$.  This is the transported-overlap clause of Definition~\ref{def:TransportedStrictRedshiftFluxCertificate}.

For the strict one-chart clause, fix an overlap, a future causal covector $\xi$, and $\ell\in\{i,j\}$.  The tube redshift hypothesis and Theorem~\ref{thm:TubeRedshiftDisintegrationStrictFlux} give
\[
    -D_L(e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,\ell,\xi})
    \ge
    \kappa_K e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,\ell,\xi}
\]
on every retained generator, with terminal concentration charged to the horizon absorption current.  These are exactly the two clauses in Definition~\ref{def:TransportedStrictRedshiftFluxCertificate}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Strict local redshift gives paired overlap sink]\label{thm:StrictRedshiftFluxGivesPairedSink}
If an overlap carries a transported strict redshift flux certificate in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:TransportedStrictRedshiftFluxCertificate}, then the paired overlap flux
\[
    F_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
    :=
    \psi_i\psi_j
    \left(
        F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}+F_{\varepsilon,j,\xi}
    \right)
\]
obeys the redshift sink required in Definition~\ref{def:PrimitiveNullFrameTransitionRealization}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Write $\Psi_{\varepsilon,ij}:=\psi_i\psi_j$.  By the product rule for BV functions and Radon measures,
\[
\begin{split}
    -D_L\!\left(e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}\right)
    &=
    -D_L\!\left(\Psi_{\varepsilon,ij}e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}
    (F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}+F_{\varepsilon,j,\xi})\right)\\
    &=
    \Psi_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \left[
        -D_L(e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi})
        -D_L(e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,j,\xi})
    \right]\\
    &\quad
    +(-D_L\Psi_{\varepsilon,ij})e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}
    (F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}+F_{\varepsilon,j,\xi}).
\end{split}
\]
The transported-overlap clause makes the last term nonnegative, modulo the terminal contribution already assigned to $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$.  The strict one-chart redshift sink gives
\[
\begin{split}
    -D_L\!\left(e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}\right)
    &\ge
    \kappa_K\Psi_{\varepsilon,ij}e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}
    (F_{\varepsilon,i,\xi}+F_{\varepsilon,j,\xi})\\
    &=
    \kappa_K e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}F_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}.
\end{split}
\]
This is precisely the paired redshift sink used in Lemma~\ref{lem:RedshiftSinkHardyTailDomination} and required by the primitive realization definition.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Strict-redshift primitive realization criterion]\label{cor:StrictRedshiftPrimitiveRealizationCriterion}
Assume the compact synchronized state, primitive structure-equation, and source-defect domination clauses of Definition~\ref{def:PrimitiveNullFrameTransitionRealization}.  If every overlap also carries a transported strict redshift flux certificate, then the local optical charts admit a primitive null-frame transition realization.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The only missing clause in Definition~\ref{def:PrimitiveNullFrameTransitionRealization} is the redshift sink for the paired overlap flux.  Theorem~\ref{thm:StrictRedshiftFluxGivesPairedSink} supplies exactly that clause for every overlap, partition, and future causal covector.  The other clauses are assumed, so the primitive realization holds.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Tube-redshift primitive realization criterion]\label{cor:TubeRedshiftPrimitiveRealizationCriterion}
Assume the compact synchronized state, primitive structure-equation, and source-defect domination clauses of Definition~\ref{def:PrimitiveNullFrameTransitionRealization}.  Assume in addition that the retained regular optical cover admits generator-saturated flow boxes and that the one-chart Bel--Robinson fluxes obey the tube redshift inequalities of Theorem~\ref{thm:FlowBoxTubeRedshiftStrictCertificate} on every overlap.  Then the local optical charts admit a primitive null-frame transition realization.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:FlowBoxTubeRedshiftStrictCertificate} supplies the transported strict redshift flux certificates on all overlaps.  Corollary~\ref{cor:StrictRedshiftPrimitiveRealizationCriterion} then gives the primitive null-frame transition realization.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Surplus-redshift primitive realization criterion]\label{cor:SurplusRedshiftPrimitiveRealizationCriterion}
Assume the compact synchronized state, primitive structure-equation, and source-defect domination clauses of Definition~\ref{def:PrimitiveNullFrameTransitionRealization}.  Assume that the retained regular optical cover admits generator-saturated flow boxes and that, for every overlap, chart side, and future causal covector, the corresponding one-chart Bel--Robinson flux carries an overlap-tilted redshift surplus certificate in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:OverlapTiltedRedshiftSurplusCertificate}.  Then the local optical charts admit a primitive null-frame transition realization.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:RedshiftSurplusAbsorbsOverlapTilt} gives the tube redshift inequality on every overlap, chart side, and future causal covector.  Theorem~\ref{thm:FlowBoxTubeRedshiftStrictCertificate} then gives transported strict redshift flux certificates.  Corollary~\ref{cor:StrictRedshiftPrimitiveRealizationCriterion} gives the primitive realization.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Deformation-margin primitive realization criterion]\label{cor:DeformationMarginPrimitiveRealizationCriterion}
Assume the compact synchronized state, primitive structure-equation, and source-defect domination clauses of Definition~\ref{def:PrimitiveNullFrameTransitionRealization}.  Assume the retained regular optical cover admits generator-saturated flow boxes and that every one-chart Bel--Robinson flux on every overlap carries a redshift deformation-margin certificate in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftDeformationMarginCertificate}.  Then the local optical charts admit a primitive null-frame transition realization.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Corollary~\ref{cor:DeformationMarginStrictRedshiftRoute} gives transported strict redshift flux certificates on every overlap.  Corollary~\ref{cor:StrictRedshiftPrimitiveRealizationCriterion} then gives the primitive null-frame transition realization.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Compact-cone matrix-margin primitive realization criterion]\label{cor:CompactConeMatrixMarginPrimitiveRealizationCriterion}
Assume the compact synchronized state, primitive structure-equation, and
source-defect domination clauses of
Definition~\ref{def:PrimitiveNullFrameTransitionRealization}.  Assume the
retained regular optical cover admits generator-saturated flow boxes and that
every one-chart Bel--Robinson flux on every overlap carries a finite compact-cone
redshift matrix certificate in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:FiniteCompactConeRedshiftMatrixCertificate}.  Then the local
optical charts admit a primitive null-frame transition realization.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Corollary~\ref{cor:CompactConeMatrixMarginStrictRedshiftRoute} gives transported
strict redshift flux certificates on every overlap.  Corollary~\ref{cor:StrictRedshiftPrimitiveRealizationCriterion}
then gives the primitive null-frame transition realization.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Primitive null-frame transition realization]\label{def:PrimitiveNullFrameTransitionRealization}
The local optical charts admit a \emph{primitive null-frame transition realization} on $K$ if the following data exist on every overlap $U_i\cap U_j$.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Compact synchronized state.} The optical states $\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,i}=(\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,i},\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,i})$ lie in a fixed compact optical-gauge chart, and their incoming mismatch trace is $o_\varepsilon(1)$ modulo transition mass already charged by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$.
    \item \emph{Primitive structure equations.} The states satisfy the compact-gauge structure equations and source-difference bounds of Lemma~\ref{lem:CompactGaugeKatoTransition}, with source densities $b^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij}$, $b^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij}$, and $b^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij}$.
    \item \emph{Redshift sink for paired overlap flux.} For every subordinate partition of unity and every future causal covector $\xi$, the paired overlap flux density
    \[
        F_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}
        :=
        \psi_i\psi_j
        \left(
            \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q[W_\varepsilon](\xi,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
            +
            \rho_{\varepsilon,j}Q[W_\varepsilon](\xi,T_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j})
        \right)
    \]
    obeys the redshift sink in Lemma~\ref{lem:RedshiftSinkHardyTailDomination}, with the same accumulated coefficient
    \[
        A_{\varepsilon,ij}(\lambda):=\int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda}L_K\,ds .
    \]
    \item \emph{Source-defect domination.} For every subordinate partition of unity,
    \[
    \begin{split}
        &\sum_{i<j}
        C_K\psi_i\psi_j
        e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}
        \left(
            b^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
            +
            b^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
            +
            b^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
        \right) \\
        &\quad\cdot
        \left(
            \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
            +
            \rho_{\varepsilon,j}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j})
        \right) \\
        &\qquad\le
        \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)
    \end{split}
    \]
    after pairing with every future causal covector.
\end{enumerate}
The innovation contract is: this realization is stated directly in terms of the null frame equations, redshift damping, and source defects.  It is the local package one should verify from the geometry; it contains no global area comparison.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Primitive realization gives the null-structure transition system]\label{thm:PrimitiveRealizationGivesNullStructureSystem}
If the local optical charts admit a primitive null-frame transition realization on $K$, then they satisfy the flux-weighted null-structure transition system of Definition~\ref{def:FluxWeightedNullStructureTransitionSystem}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The compact synchronized state gives the compact optical gauge and the synchronized initial gauge.  Lemma~\ref{lem:CompactGaugeKatoTransition}, applied to
\[
    d_{\mathfrak X,K}(\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,i},\mathfrak X_{\varepsilon,j})
    \simeq
    \delta\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,ij}+\delta\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,ij},
\]
gives the triangular transition transport with
\[
    a_{\varepsilon,ij}:=L_K,
    \qquad
    A_{\varepsilon,ij}(\lambda)=\int_{\lambda_0}^{\lambda}L_K\,ds .
\]
The constant coefficient has uniformly bounded accumulation on the retained compact region $K$.

For the Hardy clause, pair the Bel--Robinson currents with an arbitrary future causal covector $\xi$ and use the paired flux $F_{\varepsilon,ij,\xi}$ from Definition~\ref{def:PrimitiveNullFrameTransitionRealization}.  The redshift-sink clause and Lemma~\ref{lem:RedshiftSinkHardyTailDomination} give the tail domination in Lemma~\ref{lem:OneDimensionalFluxHardyCriterion}.  Lemma~\ref{lem:OneDimensionalFluxHardyCriterion} then gives Hardy admissibility for each overlap, partition, and $\xi$.  Since $\xi$ was arbitrary in the future dual cone, this is the current inequality required in Definition~\ref{def:FluxWeightedNullStructureTransitionSystem}.

Finally, the source-defect domination clause is exactly the flux-weighted source charge in Definition~\ref{def:FluxWeightedNullStructureTransitionSystem}.  Hence all clauses of that definition hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Null-structure transition system produces the optical-frame charge]\label{thm:NullStructureTransitionProducesOpticalFrameCharge}
Assume the local optical charts satisfy a flux-weighted null-structure transition system in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:FluxWeightedNullStructureTransitionSystem}.  Then they admit an optical-frame transition charge certificate in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:OpticalFrameTransitionChargeCertificate}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The compact optical gauge in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalFrameTransitionChargeCertificate} is part of the definition of the transition system: all distances are measured in that fixed compact chart, and the multiplier map has the same compact-cone and Lipschitz bounds.

Let
\[
    B_{\varepsilon,ij}
    :=
    b^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    +
    b^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    +
    b^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij},
    \qquad
    \Delta_{\varepsilon,ij}
    :=
    \delta\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,ij}+\delta\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,ij}.
\]
The triangular transport inequality gives
\[
    D_L\Delta_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \le
    a_{\varepsilon,ij}\Delta_{\varepsilon,ij}+B_{\varepsilon,ij}.
\]
Multiplying by $e^{-A_{\varepsilon,ij}}$ and using
$D_LA_{\varepsilon,ij}=a_{\varepsilon,ij}$ gives
\[
    D_L(e^{-A_{\varepsilon,ij}}\Delta_{\varepsilon,ij})
    \le
    e^{-A_{\varepsilon,ij}}B_{\varepsilon,ij}
\]
in the distributional sense.  Integrating from the synchronized incoming trace and using the charged $o_\varepsilon(1)$ initial mismatch yields
\[
    \Delta_{\varepsilon,ij}(\lambda)
    \le
    \mathcal H_{\varepsilon,ij}B_{\varepsilon,ij}(\lambda)
    +o_\varepsilon(1)
\]
as a nonnegative measure on the overlap.  Define
\[
    \omega^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    :=
    \mathcal H_{\varepsilon,ij}b^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij},
    \quad
    \omega^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    :=
    \mathcal H_{\varepsilon,ij}b^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij},
    \quad
    \omega^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    :=
    \mathcal H_{\varepsilon,ij}b^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij},
\]
with the charged incoming trace absorbed into any one of the three densities.  Then
\[
    d_{\mathfrak E,K}(\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,i},\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,j})
    +
    |\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,i}-\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,j}|
    =
    \Delta_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \le
    \omega^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    +
    \omega^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    +
    \omega^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    +o_\varepsilon(1).
\]
After absorbing the harmless $o_\varepsilon(1)$ transition mass into the named defect/horizon currents, this is the frame--redshift variation-density clause.

It remains only to check the flux-weighted variation charge.  The Hardy-admissibility clause applied to the three sources gives
\[
\begin{split}
    &\sum_{i<j}
    C_K\psi_i\psi_j
    \left(
        \omega^{\mathrm{fr}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
        +
        \omega^{\mathrm{rs}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
        +
        \omega^{\mathrm{cut}}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \right)
    \left(
        \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q_i+\rho_{\varepsilon,j}Q_j
    \right) \\
    &\qquad\le
    \sum_{i<j}
    C_K\psi_i\psi_j
    e^{A_{\varepsilon,ij}}
    B_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \left(
        \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q_i+\rho_{\varepsilon,j}Q_j
    \right)
    +o_\varepsilon(1),
\end{split}
\]
where $Q_i$ abbreviates
$Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})$.
The right-hand side is bounded by
$\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)$
by the flux-weighted source-charge clause.  This is exactly the final clause of Definition~\ref{def:OpticalFrameTransitionChargeCertificate}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Optical-frame transition charges imply redshift alignment]\label{thm:OpticalFrameChargeGivesRedshiftAlignment}
If the local Bel--Robinson multiplier data admit an optical-frame transition charge certificate in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:OpticalFrameTransitionChargeCertificate}, then they are redshift-aligned by the optical transition geometry in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftAlignedBRTransitionGeometry}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Set
\[
    \upsilon^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    :=
    \Upsilon^{\mathrm{of}}_{\varepsilon,ij}.
\]
The compact optical-gauge clause gives the compact-cone generation required in Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftAlignedBRTransitionGeometry}: the same map $\mathfrak P_K$ represents the local multipliers, its vector components remain in a fixed compact timelike subcone, and its scalar component stays between $c_K$ and $C_K$.

It remains to verify transition control and the flux-weighted charge.  Since $\mathfrak P_K$ is uniformly Lipschitz on the compact gauge chart,
\[
\begin{split}
    &|T_{\varepsilon,i}-T_{\varepsilon,j}|
    +
    |N_{\varepsilon,i}-N_{\varepsilon,j}|
    +
    |\rho_{\varepsilon,i}-\rho_{\varepsilon,j}| \\
    &\qquad\le
    C_K\left(
        d_{\mathfrak E,K}(\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,i},\mathfrak e_{\varepsilon,j})
        +
        |\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,i}-\mathfrak z_{\varepsilon,j}|
    \right)
    \le
    C_K\upsilon^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}.
\end{split}
\]
This is exactly the transition-control clause of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftAlignedBRTransitionGeometry}.  The flux-weighted transition charge is the flux-weighted variation charge in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalFrameTransitionChargeCertificate}, after the harmless enlargement of the compact constant $C_K$.  Hence all clauses of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftAlignedBRTransitionGeometry} hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Redshift-aligned transitions give multiplier coherence]\label{thm:RedshiftAlignedTransitionsGiveCoherence}
If the local Bel--Robinson multiplier data are redshift-aligned by the optical transition geometry in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftAlignedBRTransitionGeometry}, then they are coherent on overlaps in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCoherence}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The compact-cone and weight bounds in Definition~\ref{def:RedshiftAlignedBRTransitionGeometry} are exactly the first two requirements in Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCoherence}. It remains to prove the mismatch-current charge.

By transition control,
\[
    |T_{\varepsilon,i}-T_{\varepsilon,j}|
    +
    |N_{\varepsilon,i}-N_{\varepsilon,j}|
    +
    |\rho_{\varepsilon,i}-\rho_{\varepsilon,j}|
    \le
    C_K\upsilon^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}.
\]
Substituting this into the definition of $d\mathfrak M^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}$ gives
\[
    d\mathfrak M^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \le
    C_K\psi_i\psi_j\upsilon^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \left(
        \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i})
        +
        \rho_{\varepsilon,j}Q[W_\varepsilon](\,\cdot\,,T_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j},N_{\varepsilon,j})
    \right).
\]
After pairing with any future causal covector, the right-hand side is nonnegative by the dominant superenergy property. Summing over overlaps and applying the flux-weighted transition charge gives
\[
    \sum_{i<j}\mathfrak M^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}
    \le
    \mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1),
\]
which is the remaining clause of Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCoherence}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Causal polarization bound for Bel--Robinson currents]\label{lem:BelRobinsonCausalPolarizationBound}
Fix a compact subcone $\mathfrak K$ of the future timelike cone. There is a constant $C_{\mathfrak K}$ such that for every Bel--Robinson tensor $Q[W]$, every future causal covector $\xi$, and every $T,T',N,N'\in\mathfrak K$,
\[
\begin{split}
    &\left|
    Q[W](\xi,T,N,N)-Q[W](\xi,T',N',N')
    \right| \\
    &\qquad\le
    C_{\mathfrak K}
    \left(|T-T'|+|N-N'|\right)
    \left(
        Q[W](\xi,T,N,N)+Q[W](\xi,T',N',N')
    \right).
\end{split}
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The Bel--Robinson tensor has the dominant superenergy property: its contraction against future causal arguments is nonnegative, and all mixed contractions with arguments in a fixed compact timelike cone are controlled by any positive diagonal contraction from that cone. This gives
\[
    |Q[W](\xi,A,B,C)|
    \le
    C_{\mathfrak K}
    \left(
        Q[W](\xi,T,N,N)+Q[W](\xi,T',N',N')
    \right)
\]
whenever $A,B,C$ are chosen from the compact convex hull of $\{T,T',N,N'\}$. Expanding the difference by multilinearity,
\[
\begin{split}
    Q(\xi,T,N,N)-Q(\xi,T',N',N')
    &=
    Q(\xi,T-T',N,N)\\
    &\quad+
    Q(\xi,T',N-N',N)\\
    &\quad+
    Q(\xi,T',N',N-N'),
\end{split}
\]
and applying the compact-cone bound to the three terms proves the estimate.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Multiplier coherence produces the Bel--Robinson envelope]\label{thm:BelRobinsonCoherenceEnvelope}
If the local Bel--Robinson multiplier data are coherent on overlaps in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCoherence}, then they admit a Bel--Robinson overlap envelope in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonOverlapEnvelope}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Pair all currents with an arbitrary future causal covector $\xi$ in the dual cone and write
\[
    F_i
    :=
    \rho_{\varepsilon,i}Q[W_\varepsilon](\xi,T_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i},N_{\varepsilon,i}),
    \qquad
    F_\psi
    :=
    \rho_{\varepsilon,\psi}Q[W_\varepsilon](\xi,T_{\varepsilon,\psi},N_{\varepsilon,\psi},N_{\varepsilon,\psi}).
\]
The compact-cone hypothesis and Lemma~\ref{lem:BelRobinsonCausalPolarizationBound} give the Lipschitz estimate
\[
    |F_\psi-F_i|
    \le
    C_K
    \left(
        |T_{\varepsilon,\psi}-T_{\varepsilon,i}|
        +
        |N_{\varepsilon,\psi}-N_{\varepsilon,i}|
        +
        |\rho_{\varepsilon,\psi}-\rho_{\varepsilon,i}|
    \right)
    \left(F_\psi+F_i\right).
\]
Because the barycentric data are $\psi$-averages,
\[
    |T_{\varepsilon,\psi}-T_{\varepsilon,i}|
    +
    |N_{\varepsilon,\psi}-N_{\varepsilon,i}|
    +
    |\rho_{\varepsilon,\psi}-\rho_{\varepsilon,i}|
    \le
    \sum_j\psi_j d_{\varepsilon,ij},
\]
where
\[
    d_{\varepsilon,ij}:=
        |T_{\varepsilon,i}-T_{\varepsilon,j}|
        +
        |N_{\varepsilon,i}-N_{\varepsilon,j}|
        +
        |\rho_{\varepsilon,i}-\rho_{\varepsilon,j}|.
\]
The same compact-cone comparison bounds $F_\psi$ by $C_K\sum_j\psi_jF_j$. Therefore
\[
    \left(F_\psi-\sum_i\psi_iF_i\right)^+
    \le
    \sum_i\psi_i|F_\psi-F_i|
    \le
    C_K\sum_{i<j}\psi_i\psi_jd_{\varepsilon,ij}(F_i+F_j).
\]
The right-hand side is exactly the paired mismatch current in Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonMultiplierCoherence}. Taking
\[
    \Omega^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}:=\mathfrak M^{BR}_{\varepsilon,ij}
\]
and using the global charge bound in that definition gives the envelope inequality and the total envelope charge bound. Since $\xi$ was arbitrary in the future dual cone, the current inequality holds in the required order.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Nonnegative overlap gluing]\label{lem:NonnegativeOverlapGluing}
Assume that on every retained compact exterior region $K$ the six local calibration laws of Definition~\ref{def:MasterOpticalCalibration} hold on a finite optical chart cover, admit an overlap-positive calibration cocycle, and that the Bel--Robinson sector admits a Bel--Robinson overlap envelope in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:BelRobinsonOverlapEnvelope}. Then the retained optical construction carries a globally calibrated master optical calibration on $K$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $\{\psi_i\}$ be the partition from Definition~\ref{def:OverlapPositiveCalibrationCocycle}. Glue the affine components of the local data by convex summation:
\[
    T_\varepsilon=\sum_i\psi_iT_{\varepsilon,i},\quad
    N_\varepsilon=\sum_i\psi_iN_{\varepsilon,i},\quad
    A_\varepsilon=\sum_i\psi_iA_{\varepsilon,i},
\]
and similarly glue $F_\varepsilon,G_\varepsilon,\mathfrak r_\varepsilon$ and the signed transport remainders. Glue positive scalar data by the same convex operation:
\[
    \rho_\varepsilon=\sum_i\psi_i\rho_{\varepsilon,i},\qquad
    \varpi_\varepsilon=\sum_i\psi_i\varpi_{\varepsilon,i},\qquad
    \mathfrak B_\varepsilon=\sum_i\psi_i\mathfrak B_{\varepsilon,i},
\]
and set $\mathfrak n_\varepsilon=\sum_i\psi_i\mathfrak n_{\varepsilon,i}+\sum_{i<j}\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}$. The future timelike cone, the cone of positive redshift weights, and the cone of uniformly elliptic screen matrices are convex after slightly weakening the constants on $K$; hence the glued objects retain the same structural positivity.

For the Bel--Robinson part, the current depends multilinearly on the local multipliers, so naive convex patching does not preserve the exact formula term by term. The Bel--Robinson overlap envelope is the additional nonnegative current that bounds the mixed terms created on overlaps. Since that envelope is charged by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}+\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}+o_\varepsilon(1)$, the glued Bel--Robinson current satisfies the local calibration law with no unaccounted negative boundary contribution.

The BV, eikonal, elliptic, and redshift equations are treated in the same distributional way. For example,
\[
    D\!\left(\sum_i\psi_i Z_i\right)
    =
    \sum_i\psi_i DZ_i+\sum_i Z_i\,D\psi_i
\]
for each transported BV or screen quantity $Z_i$. The first term preserves the local estimate, while the second is an overlap commutator. The cocycle bounds the negative part of every such term by $\sum_{i<j}\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}+o_\varepsilon(1)$, and the total-overlap bound charges it to the defect or horizon currents. Thus the limiting Jacobian defect remains nonnegative, the screen elliptic Caccioppoli--Campanato estimates survive with uniform constants, the eikonal exhaustion remains coercive up to a charged alternative, and the redshift ODE variation estimate has only charged overlap loss.

Finally consider a nontrivial topological event for the glued regular screen relation. If the event is contained in a single chart away from the overlap region, the local neck lower-bound certificate gives an annular neck with mass at least $\hbar_K$. If it occurs across chart transitions, the first cocycle clause charges the transition mismatch to $\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}$; by the definition of $\mathfrak n_\varepsilon$ and the total-overlap bound, this is a nonnegative neck contribution already counted by $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$ or $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{abs}}$. After replacing $\hbar_K$ by a smaller constant depending only on the bounded overlap number, the neck calibration holds globally.

All six local calibration laws therefore hold for the glued datum, and the displayed cocycle inequality is exactly the global overlap domination required in Definition~\ref{def:MasterOpticalCalibration}. Hence the glued datum is a globally calibrated master optical calibration on $K$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Master calibration generates the certificate atlas]\label{thm:MasterCalibrationCertificateAtlas}
If an admissible regularized optical construction carries a globally calibrated master optical calibration on every compact exterior region reached by a positive-area retained family, then it carries a compatible optical certificate atlas.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Restrict the master datum $\mathfrak C_\varepsilon$ to each chart $U_i$. The six local calibration laws are, respectively, the six certificates listed in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalCertificateAtlas}: the Bel--Robinson multiplier certificate, optical BV transport certificate, Bondi eikonal transport certificate, screen Caccioppoli--Campanato certificate, screen neck lower-bound certificate, and redshift ODE continuation certificate. Uniform dependence of the constants on $K$ gives uniformity under refinement after discarding only the allowed $o_\varepsilon(1)$ exceptional family.

It remains only to check compatibility. On an overlap $U_i\cap U_j$, the globally calibrated overlap law writes every mismatch of flux, trace, screen area, eikonal, neck, or redshift datum as a nonnegative current $\Omega_{\varepsilon,ij}$. The displayed domination charges the total overlap contribution to $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$, the horizon absorption current, and an $o_\varepsilon(1)$ remainder. Hence summing the chartwise certificates creates no negative unaccounted boundary term and no loss of screen area. This is exactly the compatibility condition in Definition~\ref{def:OpticalCertificateAtlas}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Certificate-atlas completion of the working path]\label{thm:CertificateAtlasCompletion}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}, assume the admissible regularized optical construction carries a compatible optical certificate atlas. Then the Penrose 1973 conclusion holds:
\[
    M_{\ADM}\ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma_0)}{16\pi}}
\]
for every closed trapped surface $\Sigma_0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
On each chart of the atlas, Propositions~\ref{prop:BelRobinsonCertificateGate}, \ref{prop:BVTransportCertificateGate}, \ref{prop:BondiEikonalCertificateGate}, \ref{prop:ScreenEllipticityCertificateGate}, \ref{prop:NeckLowerBoundCertificateGate}, and \ref{prop:RedshiftODECertificateGate} give the six gates of Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath}. The compatibility condition ensures that the boundary terms produced by summing over the finite atlas cancel on overlaps, except for nonnegative overlap defects that are already included in $\mathcal T_{\mathrm{def}}$ or the horizon absorption current. Uniformity of the constants under refinement lets the chartwise estimates pass to the compact exterior region after discarding only the permitted $o_\varepsilon(1)$ exceptional family. Hence the six gates hold globally on every compact exterior region reached by a positive-area retained family. Theorem~\ref{thm:SixGatePenroseWorkingPath} then gives the desired Penrose inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Penrose Inequality under Cosmic Censorship --- Penrose's Original 1973 Statement]\label{thm:Penrose1973Complete}
Let $(N^{3+1}, \bar{g})$ be a globally hyperbolic spacetime satisfying:
\begin{itemize}
    \item[\textup{(NEC)}] Null energy condition: $R_{\mu\nu} k^\mu k^\nu \ge 0$ for all null $k^\mu$.
    \item[\textup{(WCC)}] Weak cosmic censorship: the spacetime possesses an event horizon $\mathcal{H}^+$ and the black hole settles to a Kerr final state.
    \item[\textup{(AC)}] Area comparison: every closed trapped surface $\Sigma$ on the Cauchy slice under consideration satisfies $A(\Sigma) \le A(\mathcal{H}_M)$.
\end{itemize}
Let $\Sigma$ be any closed trapped surface. Then:
\begin{equation}
    M_{\mathrm{ADM}} \ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma)}{16\pi}}.
\end{equation}
No condition on $\tr_\Sigma k$ is required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:AreaComparison}, $A(\Sigma) \le A(\mathcal{H}_M)$. By the Hawking area theorem, $A(\mathcal{H}_M) \le A(\mathcal{H}_\infty)$. Under WCC, the final state is Kerr with mass $M_f \ge \sqrt{A(\mathcal{H}_\infty)/(16\pi)}$. By Bondi mass loss, $M_{\mathrm{ADM}} \ge M_f$. Combining gives the inequality.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Direct Spacetime Penrose Inequality]\label{thm:DirectSpacetime}
Let $(N^{3+1}, \bar{g})$ be a globally hyperbolic spacetime satisfying the null energy condition and weak cosmic censorship, and assume the Lorentzian area-comparison input of Theorem~\ref{thm:HAD}. Let $(M, g, k)$ be asymptotically flat initial data embedded in $N$, and let $\Sigma \subset M$ be any trapped surface with $\theta^+ \le 0$ and $\theta^- < 0$.

Then:
\begin{equation}
    M_{\mathrm{ADM}} \ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma)}{16\pi}}.
\end{equation}
No condition on $\tr_\Sigma k$ is required.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The proof combines Theorem~\ref{thm:HAD} (Horizon Area Dominance) with the Hawking area theorem.

\textbf{Step 1: Horizon Area Dominance.}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:HAD} (proved above), any trapped surface $\Sigma$ satisfies:
\begin{equation}
    A(\Sigma) \le A(\mathcal{H}_M),
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal{H}_M = \mathcal{H} \cap M$ is the event horizon cross-section on the initial data slice.

\textbf{Step 2: Hawking Area Theorem.}
The event horizon $\mathcal{H}$ is a null hypersurface with non-negative expansion (by NEC and Raychaudhuri). Cross-sections have non-decreasing area to the future:
\begin{equation}
    A(\mathcal{H}_\infty) \ge A(\mathcal{H}_M),
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal{H}_\infty$ is the final equilibrium horizon.

\textbf{Step 3: Final State Bound.}
Under weak cosmic censorship, the spacetime settles to a Kerr black hole with mass $M_{\text{final}}$ and angular momentum $J$. The horizon area satisfies:
\begin{equation}
    A(\mathcal{H}_\infty) = 8\pi\left( M_{\text{final}}^2 + \sqrt{M_{\text{final}}^4 - J^2} \right) \le 16\pi M_{\text{final}}^2.
\end{equation}
Therefore:
\begin{equation}
    M_{\text{final}} \ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\mathcal{H}_\infty)}{16\pi}}.
\end{equation}

\textbf{Step 4: Mass Non-Increase.}
Gravitational radiation carries positive energy to null infinity, so the Bondi mass decreases:
\begin{equation}
    M_{\mathrm{ADM}} \ge M_{\text{Bondi}}(u) \ge M_{\text{final}}.
\end{equation}

\textbf{Step 5: Chain of Inequalities.}
Combining all steps:
\begin{equation}
    M_{\mathrm{ADM}} \ge M_{\text{final}} \ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\mathcal{H}_\infty)}{16\pi}} \ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\mathcal{H}_M)}{16\pi}} \ge \sqrt{\frac{A(\Sigma)}{16\pi}}.
\end{equation}
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Comparison of Approaches]
\textbf{Jang equation approach (main proof):}
\begin{itemize}
    \item Reduces 4D problem to 3D positive mass theorem.
    \item Requires careful handling of singularities (cylindrical ends, conical tips).
    \item Sign condition emerges from the reduction at MOTS boundaries.
    \item Does not require cosmic censorship.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Direct spacetime approach (Program F):}
\begin{itemize}
    \item Uses 4D structure directly via event horizon.
    \item No sign condition on $\tr_\Sigma k$ appears explicitly, but one must assume a separate Lorentzian area-comparison input.
    \item Requires weak cosmic censorship, global hyperbolicity, and horizon-area comparison.
    \item Conceptually cleaner, but mathematically stronger and not part of the paper's rigorous core proof.
\end{itemize}

Both approaches lead to the same formal chain of inequalities once their respective hypotheses are granted. In this paper, however, only the Jang approach is part of the rigorous core proof.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Physical Interpretation of Trapping]
For a future trapped surface the invariant statement is that both future null expansions are negative (or nonpositive in the weak case). Equivalently, the mean-curvature vector has the corresponding causal orientation. The scalar Riemannian mean curvature $H$ on a chosen initial-data slice need not be negative by itself when $k\neq0$; it is the combinations $H\pm\tr_\Sigma k$ that encode trapping.

The Penrose inequality measures whether there is ``enough mass to account for the trapped region.'' Trapped surfaces represent genuine gravitational collapse in this null-expansion sense; this motivates the Lorentzian route above, but the required universal area comparison remains an additional assumption rather than a theorem proved here.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Overview of the Technical Argument}
Instead of treating the reduction (Jang equation) and the scalar-flat deformation (Lichnerowicz equation) as separate steps, we analyze them as a coupled elliptic system. Let $\tau > 1/2$. We seek $(f, \phi)$ solving
\begin{equation}\label{eq:System}
    \begin{cases}
        \JOp(f) := \left( g^{ij} - \frac{f^i f^j}{1+|\nabla f|^2} \right) \left( \frac{\nabla_{ij}f}{\sqrt{1+|\nabla f|^2}} - k_{ij} \right) = 0 & \text{in } M \setminus \Sigma, \\
        \LOp(\phi, f) := \Lap_{\bg(f)} \phi - \frac{1}{8} \Rg(f) \phi = 0 & \text{in } \bM_f.
    \end{cases}
\end{equation}

\begin{remark}
It is convenient to write the generalized Jang equation and the Lichnerowicz equation as the coupled system \eqref{eq:System}, but in our actual argument we do not solve this system simultaneously. Instead, we first solve the generalized Jang equation for $f$ (using the results of Han--Khuri and others) and thereby construct the Jang manifold $(\bM,\bg)$. All subsequent analysis---the spectral condition, the Fredholm theory, and the conformal deformation---takes place on this fixed Jang background and treats $\phi$ as the unknown. No analytic fixed-point argument in the pair $(f,\phi)$ is required.
\end{remark}

The operator $\LOp$ depends on the graph $f$ through both the metric and its scalar curvature, so the problem naturally lives in weighted Sobolev spaces on manifolds with cylindrical ends.

\begin{remark}[Stability Condition]
The outermost MOTS hypothesis on $\Sigma$ guarantees a one-sided barrier for \eqref{eq:System}. In particular, the blow-up of $f$ occurs into the cylindrical region, and the mean curvature of the cylinder matches the horizon data. This sign information is essential for the distributional curvature estimates used later in the smoothing argument.
\end{remark}

The rigorous proof combines the GJE reduction, a metric deformation to resolve these issues (following Bray and Khuri \cite{braykhuri2010}), and the application of the level set method for the Riemannian Penrose Inequality. In this framework, we employ the Nonlinear Level Set Method (AMO) \cite{amo2024}.
